# Promising Research Directions: Whole-Body Coordination — Loco-Manipulation, Mobile Manipulation, Force-Adaptive Control

> [!abstract] Overview
> A humanoid that manipulates *while it moves* faces one structural fact: **the arm and the legs are mechanically coupled** — an arm reach is a reaction torque on the base, so whole-body competence does not factor into "an arm controller plus a leg controller." The cross-term is where part-wise stacks fail under load and out of distribution.
> These **12 directions across 4 clusters** organize the bets around that coupling: make it an *explicit predicted quantity* (A), coordinate it with a *moving base* (B), hold it under *external force* (C), and get the *demonstration data* to learn it (D).
> The editorial bet: **the coupling is structure to *predict*, not data to *collect*** — the directions that model it win on a fixed data budget, where more teleop does not.

---

## Methodology

**Scope.** This doc reads ~27 humanoid / loco-manipulation / mobile-manipulation / teleoperation surveys and benchmarks (the Survey Landscape below) plus ~246 whole-body method papers from `_KnowledgeHub_/`, cross-checked against [[11_Robotics-and-Embodied-AI|11_Robotics-and-Embodied-AI]] and the `Embodied-AI/` deep-dives [[02_Dataset-Benchmark-Environment|02_Dataset-Benchmark-Environment]], [[11_Contact-Rich-and-Tactile-Control|11_Contact-Rich-and-Tactile-Control]], and [[15_Sim-to-Real-Transfer|15_Sim-to-Real-Transfer]]. It owns the *coupling* between manipulation and locomotion — whole-body loco-manipulation, mobile manipulation (moving base + arm together), force-adaptive control under external load, and whole-body teleoperation / retargeting; a direction is admitted only if both base/leg motion and manipulation are essential. Pure locomotion goes to [[Locomotion|Locomotion]]; fixed-base or arm-only manipulation goes to [[Manipulation|Manipulation]]; world-model and sim-to-real substrates are cross-referenced ([[WAM|WAM]], [[Sim2Real|Sim2Real]]), not re-clustered.

---

> [!tip] Convergence patterns
> - **Flat, monolithic RL does not train on the coupled high-DoF action space — the benchmarks measure the collapse, not just assert it** (5-way): [[2403.10506|HumanoidBench]] (flat RL fails most whole-body loco-manipulation tasks; the high-DoF action space is the exploration bottleneck, and the suite's own analysis calls for hierarchical decomposition), [[2510.08807|Humanoid-Everyday]] (SOTA imitation learning scores **0%** on its hard loco-manip split), [[2407.07788|BiGym]] (IL and RL collapse to near-**0%** on long-horizon sequences and stacking), [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]] (task-specific reward engineering reward-hacks; the **8**-task / **3**-embodiment SkillBench finds no reusable primitive substrate to compose), [[2503.05652|BRS]] (high-DoF mobile manipulators amplify end-effector drift, OOD states and safety violations — whole-body action modeling is unsolved) — five suites independently report the same wall, the empirical mandate for Cluster A's structured decomposition over monolithic scaling.
> - **The whole-body data wall — coupled loco-manip demonstrations are scarce, stationary-arm-centric, and fragmented across rigs** (3-way): [[2510.08807|Humanoid-Everyday]] (existing datasets are stationary-arm-centric and neglect full-body locomotion), [[2412.17730|Mimicking-Bench]] (no large-scale human-demo benchmark for scene-geometry generalization; small manual demo sets cap what can be studied), [[2505.12748|TeleOpBench]] (**30** tasks / **4** modalities / **3** humanoids, yet teleop evaluation couples hardware + software and leaves the interface trade-offs that set data cost unquantified) — three benchmarks converge on a substrate too small, too arm-centric, and too rig-specific to train coupled behaviour, the empirical mandate for Cluster D's capture / retargeting / synthesis directions.
> - **No standardized whole-body evaluation protocol — methods cannot be compared and failures cannot be localized** (4-way): [[2603.20147|AGILE]] (no standardized I/O contract, late environment-bug discovery, and missing motion-quality diagnostics), [[2505.12748|TeleOpBench]] (hardware-coupled evaluation blocks cross-method comparison; no shared simulator-centric platform), [[2510.08807|Humanoid-Everyday]] (no standardized loco-manip evaluation exists), [[2306.11565|HomeRobot]] (open-vocab perception silently collapses success from **5–15%** to **0.4–0.6%** the moment a real detector replaces ground truth — a failure no task-success number reveals) — four suites name the same missing layer: a shared whole-body protocol that localizes *where* a coupled policy fails, which the Benchmark Gaps section enumerates per direction.

---

## Formal Framing

**The whole-body coupled-dynamics object.** A whole-body system carries a configuration $q = (q_{\text{base}}, q_{\text{leg}}, q_{\text{arm}}, q_{\text{hand}})$ on a single floating base. Its dynamics couple every subsystem through the shared inertia matrix and the external-wrench Jacobian:

$$\ddot q = M(q)^{-1}\!\left(\tau - C(q,\dot q)\,\dot q - g(q) + J_{\text{ext}}^{\top} F_{\text{ext}}\right)$$

The coupling is structural: $M(q)$ is **not block-diagonal** across (base, leg, arm). An arm acceleration induces a base/leg reaction torque (the off-diagonal $M_{\text{arm,leg}}$ term), and an external hand wrench $F_{\text{ext}}$ propagates through $J_{\text{ext}}^{\top}$ to *every* joint, including the legs that keep the centre-of-mass over the support polygon. So a part-wise policy that factors $\pi = (\pi_{\text{leg}}, \pi_{\text{arm}})$ with independent value $V(a_{\text{leg}}) + V(a_{\text{arm}})$ throws away the load-bearing cross-term — the central reframing of Cluster A.

Three coupling formalisms organize this doc:

| Object | Formalism | Cluster |
|---|---|---|
| **Arm-as-disturbance** | An arm command $a_{\text{arm}}$ injects a reaction $\delta_{\text{base}} = M_{\text{base,arm}}\,\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$ the leg policy must reject *anticipatorily*; the whole-body value is non-separable across subsystems | A |
| **Base-as-manipulation-DoF** | The mobile-manipulation action is a joint $a = (a_{\text{base}}, a_{\text{arm}}) \sim \pi(a \mid o, l)$ where base velocity is a *manipulation* degree of freedom — repositioning extends the reachable workspace mid-task, not before it | B |
| **External wrench under load** | A payload / contact force $F_{\text{ext}}$ at the hand sets a whole-body equilibrium constraint $J_{\text{ext}}^{\top} F_{\text{ext}} + g(q) \in$ feasible-torque $\times$ support-polygon; the legs compensate for what the arms feel | C |

**The mobile-manipulation joint action.** The mobile-manipulation action is a single joint distribution over the whole body, $p(a_{\text{base}}, a_{\text{torso}}, a_{\text{arm}} \mid o)$, and the order in which it is factored is *not* arbitrary. Autoregressive conditioning — drawing the base action first, then the torso conditioned on it, then the arm conditioned on both — is the factoring

$$p(a_{\text{base}}, a_{\text{torso}}, a_{\text{arm}} \mid o) = p(a_{\text{base}} \mid o)\,p(a_{\text{torso}} \mid a_{\text{base}}, o)\,p(a_{\text{arm}} \mid a_{\text{base}}, a_{\text{torso}}, o)$$

so that each downstream link absorbs the displacement its upstream link just committed, rather than the independent factorization $\prod_i p(a_i \mid o)$ that lets the end-effector drift accumulate across links. This is the formal content of "base is a manipulation DoF" — the base action is the *condition* the arm action is drawn under, not a separate navigate-then-grasp phase. Cluster B builds on this.

**The feasibility-preserving composition.** A whole-body action assembled as a per-joint convex combination of a fixed set of $K$ individually-feasible primitives,

$$a(q) = \sum_{k=1}^{K} w_k(q)\,\pi_k^{\text{prim}}(q), \qquad w_k(q) \ge 0,\ \textstyle\sum_k w_k(q) = 1,$$

lies in the convex hull of $\{\pi_k^{\text{prim}}\}$ by construction. If each primitive respects the torque / contact / balance constraints, any simplex-weighted blend of them does too — feasibility is then a property of the *combination rule*, not of a per-task reward, and it is the simplex constraint ($w \ge 0$, $\sum_k w_k = 1$) that supplies it. This is the inverse of a monolith, which must rediscover the feasible set from scratch on every new task. Cluster A builds on this.

---

## Cluster Overview

| Cluster | Directions | Shared bottleneck | Cross-direction synergy |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A — Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation** | A1, A2, A3, A4 | The arm–leg coupling is non-separable: an arm reach is a balance disturbance the legs must anticipate, and flat RL fails on the coupled high-DoF action space | **A1 is the lead** — it makes the arm→leg coupling an *explicit* predicted quantity ([[2604.07993\|HEX]]'s ablation: removing the coupling architecture costs −5/12 on Pouring, the largest single-component drop, vs +1/12 for its 12M-frame pretraining). **A4's unified-latent interface is the high-level command surface that grounds into A1's coupling**, drawing on Cluster D's demos for breadth. A1's predictor is the balance model A2's blended skills and A3's tracker both need; A3's active base is the workspace-extension A4 commands. [[2604.07993\|HEX]] and [[2506.09366\|SkillBlender]] set the bar for A1 and A2 |
| **B — Mobile Manipulation (Nav↔Manip Coupling)** | B1, B2 | A moving base continuously changes what's observable and what's in reach — the target can leave the frame, and out-of-view state must be remembered, not just factored into the action | B1's where-to-look decision feeds the observation B2 must remember; both ride on the same coupled base-arm action substrate — [[2503.05652\|BRS]]'s autoregressive base→torso→arm decoding and [[2401.02117\|Mobile-ALOHA]]'s whole-body teleop — as established infrastructure, not a direction of its own |
| **C — Force-Adaptive Coordination Under Load** | C1, C2, C3 | An external hand wrench propagates through the kinematic chain to the support polygon; the legs compensate for what the arms feel, and the task reward omits the force | C1's force-adaptive policy is the performance layer C2 bounds with safety; C2's CBF/QP filter is the guarantee C1's learned compensation lacks. Both share the external-wrench formalism and the anchors [[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] (0.37 vs 0.60) and [[2604.07457\|CMP]] (86.7% extreme-OOD). **C3 adds a third lever neither C1 nor C2 touches** — instead of anticipating the wrench (C1) or certifying the fall/collision boundary (C2), it makes the joint's own stiffness a policy-chosen response to the load, and its bet directly stresses C2's certified safe set (H4) |
| **D — Whole-Body Teleoperation & Human-Motion Retargeting** | D1, D2, D3 | The data wall — demonstrations of *coupled* loco-manipulation are scarce, embodiment-mismatched, and per-platform | **D1 is the acquisition substrate**: it retargets existing human motion with interaction preserved, and now also tests whether a captured-not-retargeted reference is even necessary on a legged platform ([[2602.10106\|EgoHumanoid]]'s +51 pp generalization, [[2509.04443\|EMMA]]'s wheeled +30 pp). **D3 attacks the same wall from the other side** — synthesizing coupled demos ([[2605.27724\|HumanoidMimicGen]]'s 0.89 vs 0.33, [[2606.05160\|GRAIL]]'s 20,000+ from assets) with no human in the per-demo loop at all. D1's feasible reference is what D3 and A1–A4 all consume; D2 amortizes whatever D1/D3 produce across bodies. [[2509.26633\|OmniRetarget]] and [[2605.23733\|Any2Any]]'s ~1%-compute transfer are the shared levers |

---

## Cluster A — Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation

*Coupled arm–leg dynamics on a single floating base — where an arm reach is a balance disturbance, the whole-body value is non-separable, and flat RL fails on the coupled high-DoF action space. The four directions attack the same non-separability from different layers: modelling the coupling term itself (A1), composing feasible primitives so the action never leaves the feasible set (A2), closing the precision loop in the world frame with the base as an active transport DoF (A3), and emitting joint loco+manip commands through one latent interface (A4).*

### A1 — Coupled-Dynamics Whole-Body Action Models

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | A — Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation |
| **Thesis** | Build a whole-body policy that predicts the base/leg reaction an arm reach induces, instead of bolting an arm policy onto a leg policy. The reason it must work: the inertia matrix $M(q)$ is non-block-diagonal, so an arm acceleration *is* a leg-balance disturbance, not a separate sub-problem. The field assumes whole-body competence factors into an arm controller plus a balance controller. The bet is in First-principles below — make the coupling term an *explicit* predicted quantity and beat the implicit-coupling state of the art on the same fixed data. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (benchmark), [[2603.20147\|AGILE]] (method), [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] (method), [[2604.07993\|HEX]] (method), [[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] (method), [[2510.05070\|ResMimic]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | [[2604.07993\|HEX]] 79.8% ID (7 tasks) / 61.8% OOD (8 scene-variation) vs GR00T N1.5 70.2% / 41.0% and π0.5 OOD 44.3%, 100% initial + 53.3% Place-Box on long-horizon box-convey; [[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] 0.37 vs 0.60 upper-body tracking error under large force (~2× over best baseline), 100 N cart-pull + 1.2 kg/hand payload; [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] 12.4 mm world-frame end-effector error compensating base disturbance |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: a whole-body policy must account for the base/leg reaction an arm reach induces, but most stacks control the parts separately and let that coupling term vanish.
- **Today's answers**: [[2604.07993|HEX]] learns the coupling *implicitly* in a predictive MoE (79.8 ID / 61.8 OOD vs part-wise GR00T N1.5 at 70.2 / 41.0); [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] lets it *emerge* from a shared observation between two RL agents (0.37 vs 0.60 upper-body tracking error under load). The closest *explicit* precedent is [[2509.21231|SEEC]], which combines an analytic dynamics term with a learned residual under an L2 torque-matching loss — but it predicts the *base→arm* end-effector-stabilization direction and explicitly assumes the *reverse* arm→base back-coupling is "negligible because arms are dynamically light," the very premise A1 must overturn. Each works in its lane — none makes the *arm→base reaction* an explicit predicted quantity.
- **The opening**: HEX's own ablation (reported in the HEX paper, not yet reproduced from the vault note): removing the coupling component (the UPP) is the single largest drop (Pouring 11/12 → 6/12) while its 12M-frame pretraining adds only second-order gain, so the coupling is the lever and no one predicts the arm→base reaction explicitly yet — and [[2509.21231|SEEC]]'s "arms-are-light" assumption is exactly what fails in the fast/loaded-reach regime where the reaction is largest (H2's stratification).

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: The inertia matrix $M(q)$ is non-block-diagonal — the arm, legs, and base are mechanically linked, so by the equations of motion an arm acceleration *is* a reaction torque on the base and legs. This holds no matter the training data. So the value of a whole-body action cannot be split into an arm part plus a leg part. The evidence that this structure is load-bearing is [[2604.07993|HEX]]'s ablation (as reported in the HEX paper): with the controller and inputs held identical, removing the coupling component (UPP) costs the largest single drop (Pouring 11/12 → 6/12) while the 12M-frame pretraining is second-order — the coupling, not the data, carries whole-body performance. (This keystone number is HEX-paper-reported, not yet reproduced from the vault note — H1 re-runs it as the within-paper control.)
- **Assumption being challenged**: That a humanoid policy factors into an arm policy plus a leg/balance policy — the bet of every part-wise stack — *and* the sharper sub-assumption [[2509.21231|SEEC]] makes explicit: that the arm→base back-coupling is negligible "because arms are dynamically light," so only the base→arm direction needs an explicit term. [[2604.07993|HEX]] names the factoring failure ("uncoordinated and unstable behaviors") and beats GR00T N1.5 (70.2 / 41.0), ACT, and π0.5 (OOD 44.3) under a *matched* controller + inputs; the 41.0→61.8 OOD gap is the cost of discarding the cross-body link, unrecoverable by more per-part data. The coupling-aware systems leave the term *implicit* ([[2604.07993|HEX]]'s learned MoE), *emergent* ([[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s shared observation), or *one-directional* ([[2509.21231|SEEC]]'s base→arm-only explicit term); nobody predicts the arm→base reaction directly.
- **The bet**: An *explicit* predicted base-reaction $\hat\delta_{\text{base}}=\hat M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$ (an analytic term + learned residual, the same recipe [[2509.21231|SEEC]] uses for the *opposite* direction) fed as an auxiliary-loss target beats [[2604.07993|HEX]]'s implicit MoE *on the same fixed dataset* — widening the 41.0→61.8 OOD margin by ≥ +3 pp (into the mid-60s), with the gain concentrated on the top arm-acceleration quartile exactly where [[2509.21231|SEEC]]'s "arms-are-light" assumption is false, and matching the scale of [[2503.05652|BRS]]'s 53%-from-decoding swing without new data. Falsifiable: if explicit ≤ 61.8% at matched latency — or if the arm→base term is genuinely negligible as [[2509.21231|SEEC]] claims — the coupling is better left to the MoE.

**Related research papers.**

Twenty-nine systems that put the *same* arm→leg coupling back in different ways — implicit, emergent, explicit-but-reverse-direction, latent, part-wise, model-based (analytic-QP, analytic+residual, heavy-limb centroidal-momentum), precision-capping, arm-speed-modulation, coupling-as-disturbance, object-side world-model, intent-side, feature-level, anticipatory, hand-built — none of them as an explicit predicted *arm→base* dynamics term. The axis is *how the coupling is represented*:

| System | Coupling mechanism | Coupling is | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [[2604.07993\|HEX]] | predictive MoE (UPP) over a unified whole-body state | implicit (learned) | 79.8 ID / 61.8 OOD vs GR00T N1.5 70.2 / 41.0; UPP-removal is the largest ablation drop (Pouring 11/12 → 6/12), pretraining second-order *(reported in the HEX paper)* | never an explicit term — the coupling is buried in the MoE weights |
| [[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] | shared observation between 2 RL agents ("mutual awareness") | emergent | 0.37 vs 0.60 (PID) / 0.73 (monolithic) upper-body error under load; 100 N cart, 1.2 kg/hand | no single coupled model — the legs pre-compensate but the reaction is never predicted |
| [[2509.21231\|SEEC]] | analytic dynamics term + learned residual under an L2 torque-matching loss | explicit (base→arm only) | 2.26 m/s² mean EE acceleration ('Stepping') vs 5.73 IK; 34.40% degradation under unseen controllers vs 57.45% co-trained; real T1 plate-carrying | the explicit-term recipe A1 borrows, but for the *reverse* direction — and asserts arm→base is "negligible (arms are dynamically light)," the premise A1 falsifies |
| [[2603.08961\|FAME]] | latent-load-conditioned policy expanding the balance envelope | latent (load-conditioned) | 73.84% standing SR vs 29.44% base / 51.40% base+curriculum; real H12 under asymmetric + bimanual loads | conditions on the load to *stand still*, not a forward model of the arm→base reaction during a reach (standing-only) |
| [[2507.04140\|Centroidal-Arm-Motion]] | centroidal-angular-momentum-as-reward + shared observation, emergent anti-phase arm-leg | emergent (CAM-reward) | ~23% higher torque-disturbance recovery vs Fixed-Arms; 1.3 m/s flat + vision-free stairs real | the arm-leg link emerges from a momentum *reward*, never a predicted reaction term — a reward-shaped cousin of FALCON's emergence |
| [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] | world-frame Tracker modulates base + waist to absorb arm disturbance | implicit (precision-capping) | 12.4 mm world-frame EE error; KMP halves hand error (25.2 mm without) | caps *precision* but absorbs the coupling reactively, not anticipated |
| [[2512.11047\|WholeBodyVLA]] | joint loco+manip latents removing "decision-execution misalignment" | latent (intent-side) | 78.0% Bag/Box/Cart beating modular + end-to-end | grounds the coupling in latents, not an explicit dynamics term (feeds A4) |
| [[2512.13093\|PvP]] | proprioceptive-privileged contrastive representation targeting "strong coupling between motor functions" | feature-level | data-efficient WBC on a real LimX Oli | learns coupling-aware *features*, not a forward coupling *prediction* — leaves the explicit-term bet open |
| [[2605.14417\|DAJI]] | 64D joint-intent latent predicting support shifts + momentum *before the body moves* | anticipatory (whole-body) | 94.42% rollout SR, 4.71 ms | anticipates the *body's own* transitions, not the arm→leg reaction specifically — the complement H4 fuses (feeds A4) |
| [[2507.06905\|ULC]] | one curriculum-RL controller for locomotion + 3-DoF torso + dual-arm | unified (implicit) | beats decoupled + hierarchical; 0.06 rad arm error under 2 kg wrist loads | never isolates the coupling term, so it cannot say *which* structure earned the win (feeds A4) |
| [[2603.12263\|Psi0]] | foundation loco-manip model; locomotion routed to System-0 controller | external (uncoupled) | >40% over GR00T N1.6 on 8 long-horizon tasks via a "critical yet overlooked data recipe," beating a 10×-data baseline | upper/lower coupling sits *outside* the learned model — the data-recipe scale A1 must still beat structurally |
| [[2502.14795\|Humanoid-VLA]] | language + egocentric vision + goal-conditioned RL tracking generated kinematics | none (kinematic) | <40 mm error, 10/10 turn-to-object; first humanoid VLA of its kind | the RL stage tracks kinematics with no cross-body coupling term — the egocentric precedent A1 supplies dynamics to |
| [[2605.21133\|Spatial-Brain-Cerebellum]] | hand-built end-effector-reachable-space solver triggers base transport | hand-built (reachability) | 60.0% vs 0% OOD-hard | the coupling is an analytic reachability solver, not learned, so it cannot anticipate the dynamic reaction A1 targets |
| [[2604.07457\|CMP]] | competence-manifold projection onto feasibility *after* the policy acts | robustness wrapper | 86.7% extreme-OOD real | projects onto feasibility post-hoc, not a coupling model — the safety layer A1's predictor rides under (feeds C2) |
| [[2511.21169\|Kinematics-Aware-Multi-Policy]] | delta-command upper-body policy + curriculum lower-body, kinematics-heuristic reward | part-wise (multi-policy) | 57% EE-height-oscillation cut under 4 kg payload; real G1 pushing a 112.8 kg cart | reduces oscillation reactively by *decoupling* into per-part policies — the coupling is suppressed, not predicted (the part-wise stack A1 beats) |
| [[2603.02443\|Safe-WBLM]] | model-based reference-governor QP over a force-tracking WBC | model-based (analytic) | EE velocity MSE ≤0.005 m²/s² linear; compliant external-wrench tracking, recursive feasibility | the coupling is handled by an analytic model + safety QP, not a learned forward prediction — the model-based contrast to A1's learned residual |
| [[2506.14278\|Heavy-Limbs-WBC]] | kino-dynamics MPC (100 Hz) + hierarchical-QP tracker (800 Hz) that couples limb motion to base balance *analytically through the centroidal momentum* | model-based (analytic, centroidal-momentum, no learned residual) | **1.2 m/s** walking, **60 N** disturbance rejection, **5–10 cm** stairs on real Orca I; planner **6.57–7.89 ms** / tracker **0.52–0.58 ms** | the canonical heavy-limb (40–60% mass) base-coupling reference — the coupling is an *explicit analytic centroidal-momentum term* with no learned residual, the pure-model-based foil between [[2603.02443\|Safe-WBLM]]'s safety-QP and [[2504.06662\|RAMBO]]'s analytic+residual; A1's bet is to *learn* the residual this hand-derives |
| [[2504.06662\|RAMBO]] | model-based QP whole-body controller + RL feedback residual correcting base-acceleration targets and joint positions | model-based (analytic + learned residual) | 0.039 m quadruped / 0.036 m biped EE position error; real Go2 three-legged carry + bipedal cart-push | the exact analytic-term + learned-residual recipe A1 borrows, but the residual *corrects* base acceleration reactively rather than *predicting* the arm→base reaction — and on loco-manip quadrupeds, not the humanoid arm→base direction; the model-based-residual lane between [[2603.02443\|Safe-WBLM]]'s pure-analytic QP (no residual) and [[2509.21231\|SEEC]]'s base→arm-only explicit term |
| [[2403.17367\|RoboDuet]] | cooperative two-policy where the Arm Policy emits pitch/roll guidance that drives the Loco Policy's base posture | emergent (explicit arm→base guidance channel) | 67% workspace expansion (51.36 → 85.79 m³), 23% higher object-transfer SR (39/60 vs 32/60), self-collision 50% → 5% | the closest arm-*drives*-base channel, but the signal is a learned inter-policy *guidance command* (desired pitch/roll), not a predicted reaction *term* — a cooperative-dual-policy representation distinct from [[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s shared-observation emergence and [[2511.21169\|Kinematics-Aware-Multi-Policy]]'s part-wise decoupling, still stopping short of A1's explicit dynamics prediction |
| [[2508.10538\|MLM]] | Trajectory-Velocity Prediction head (NAE + memory encoder) forecasting future EE trajectory + base linear velocity, deployment-unobservable yet 'critical for stable whole-body loco-manip' | anticipatory (manipulation-side forecast) | up to 85% real autonomous SR; lowest mean 6D tracking error across 7 tasks vs sMLP / DeepWBC | anticipates the *EE/manipulation* motion that drives coordination, not the arm→leg *reaction* — the manipulation-side complement to [[2605.14417\|DAJI]]'s body-own support-shift/momentum forecast, a distinct anticipation target feeding A1's H4 but still no explicit reaction term |
| [[2606.18772\|HALOMI]] | manifold-constrained whole-body controller over a learned BFM-Zero behavior latent inferring stable full-body motion from sparse head/hand targets | latent (behavior-FM manifold) | 90% Bag Transfer / 85% Pick-Bread-Place real G1; global tracking error E_MGBP 0.0409–0.1163 m on unseen trajectories | resolves the arm-target→whole-body *under-specification* by projecting onto a feasible behavior manifold, not by a forward dynamics term — the behavior-FM-latent variant distinct from [[2512.11047\|WholeBodyVLA]]'s intent latents and [[2602.06341\|HiWET]]'s kinematic-manifold prior, coupling stays implicit in the latent |
| [[2603.13707\|REFINE-DP]] | jointly fine-tunes the diffusion planner and the RL whole-body controller via DPPO so the high level absorbs low-level execution dynamics | joint-optimization (coupling-restoration) | 97–99% Joint-Optimized SR vs 50–70% baselines; EE-orientation error cut up to 50%; 95% SR from 50 trajectories | names A1's exact factoring failure ('offline planners do not account for execution dynamics → poor command tracking') and closes the planner↔WBC gap by joint optimization, not by isolating a coupling *term* — a coupling-restoration mechanism distinct from [[2507.06905\|ULC]]'s single-curriculum monolith, so it cannot say which structure earned the win |
| [[2510.05070\|ResMimic]] | a learned residual over a *frozen* general-motion-tracking (GMT) policy, reward-shaped with per-link contact + point-cloud object-tracking rewards under a virtual-object-force curriculum | residual over a fixed backbone (implicit, reward-shaped) | **92.5%** sim mean SR across loco-manip tasks vs 0% train-from-scratch / **7.5%** fine-tune; 1300 vs 4500+ iters; real G1 carries **4.5–5.5 kg** irregular objects via whole-body contact | the cleanest external instance of A1's exact framing — *add the coupling as a residual on a fixed whole-body backbone* — but the residual is shaped *reactively* by contact/object rewards, never an explicit predicted arm→base reaction term; distinct from [[2504.06662\|RAMBO]]'s model-based analytic+residual and [[2604.07993\|HEX]]'s implicit MoE (the residual-on-a-frozen-backbone lane the table lacked) |
| [[2603.10306\|SteadyTray]] | a *pure-learned* hierarchical RL residual (ReST-RL) that actively cancels gait-induced end-effector perturbation, no analytic term | pure learned residual (base→arm direction) | **74.4%** Push-Robot / **73.9%** Push-Object SR under disturbance; zero-shot real G1 wine-glass / medical-tool transport under external force | completes [[2509.21231\|SEEC]]'s analytic-residual lane with the pure-learned variant — but operates the *base→arm* (gait-induced EE-disturbance) direction, the same as SEEC and the *reverse* of A1's arm→base reaction; the pure-learned-residual representation, not an arm→base precedent |
| [[2412.07773\|Mobile-TeleVision]] | a CVAE "Predictive Motion Prior" the lower-body RL balance policy *observes* — the anticipated upper-body *motion* is fed forward so the legs pre-stabilize, and the paper names A1's gap verbatim ("fully decoupling upper/lower neglects inter-body dynamics → instability") | anticipatory (upper-body-motion prior) | ~**25%** lower projected-gravity error in sim; real push-recovery **4.46 s** vs 5.38 s; centimeter-range arm tracking on H1 | anticipates the *predicted arm motion* the balance policy must absorb, one step short of the arm→base reaction *term* itself — distinct from [[2605.14417\|DAJI]]'s body-own support-shift forecast and [[2508.10538\|MLM]]'s EE-trajectory forecast (feeds A1's H4) |
| [[2508.00355\|TOP]] | a Time Optimization Policy that predicts future timestamps to adaptively *slow* upper-body motion precisely when "rapid upper-body motions induce momentum changes that destabilize the robot" — suppressing the arm→base reaction at the *source* via arm-timing, not via a base-side term | inverse-control (arm-speed modulation) | **95.30%** SR on unseen motions vs Exbody **92.46%**; real base <**2 mm** / EE <**6 mm** tracking; robust push recovery | reduces the reaction by capping arm *speed* rather than predicting/compensating it — the control-side inverse of [[2602.06341\|HiWET]]'s precision-capping, sharpening A1's H2/H5 (reaction scales with arm acceleration) but never an explicit arm→base term |
| [[2502.03206\|Unified-General-Humanoid-Whole-Body]] | "external upper-body intervention training" — the locomotion policy is trained robust to *arbitrary externally-controlled* upper-body movements, modelling the arm→base link as task-agnostic disturbance (the "arm-as-noise" consensus) | coupling-as-disturbance (domain randomization) | minimal tracking degradation under arbitrary upper-body intervention vs a no-intervention policy; versatile real-H1 gaits (walk/jump/hop) with concurrent manipulation | trains the reaction *away as noise* rather than predicting or anticipating it — the cleanest "arm-as-noise" baseline A1's explicit predicted term must beat, the premise A1 overturns (HUGWBC) |
| [[2602.11758\|HAIC]] | a Dynamics-Aware World Model infers high-order *object* dynamics (pos/orient/vel/accel) from proprioception alone for "proactive inertia compensation," explicitly handling "dynamics coupling between robot and underactuated object that challenges balance" | predict-then-compensate (object side) | **100%** skateboard glide / **100%** cart on real G1, beating a proprioception-only baseline; **100%** multi-terrain carry of occluding objects | the closest new paper in *mechanism* to A1's bet (predict-the-coupling-quantity-then-compensate), but the predicted quantity is the *object's* dynamics, not the arm→base reaction — instantiating the recipe for a different coupling source, leaving the arm→base-reaction-prediction bet open |
| [[2607.23473\|PRISM-Motor]] | a factorized *polynomial-interaction* proprioceptive representation (learned affine factors combined element-wise up to degree $K$) that exposes multiplicative physical quantities — joint power, velocity products, contact impulse — as explicit features instead of leaving them for an MLP to discover implicitly | architecture-level (interaction-structured representation, not a dynamics term) | humanoid-locomotion survival **92.50%** vs MLP **51.00%**, linear-velocity error **0.2099** vs **0.4607**; LIBERO manip **91.0%** vs Diffusion Policy **63.8%** / sensor-rich MCC-Oracle **64.5%**; linear probes show slip velocity / joint power / contact impulse become linearly recoverable | a general-purpose *architectural* alternative to A1's explicit-analytic-term recipe — it makes coupling-adjacent products (not specifically the arm→base reaction) explicit via representation structure rather than a predicted physical quantity, and a matched-parameter larger MLP fails to match it, isolating the gain to the polynomial inductive bias, not capacity — but it never isolates or targets the arm→base reaction term A1's bet turns on |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (explicit coupling beats implicit on fixed data), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Explicit base-reaction beats the implicit MoE on HEX's fixed data.**
   - *Prediction*: adding $\hat\delta_{\text{base}} = \hat M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$ as an auxiliary-loss target to [[2604.07993|HEX]]'s exact backbone + same dataset lifts OOD past 61.8% (target ≥ +3 pp, into the mid-60s).
   - *Test*: re-run HEX's UPP ablation with the explicit term swapped in for the implicit MoE at matched parameter count; the within-paper 6/12 → 11/12 Pouring swing is the control.
   - *Row*: HEX (implicit).
   - *Falsifier*: explicit ≤ 61.8% at matched latency → the MoE already captures it.
2. **H2 — The explicit-term gain concentrates on fast arm motions, where SEEC's "arms-are-light" assumption is false.**
   - *Prediction*: stratifying OOD tasks by arm-acceleration magnitude, the explicit-over-implicit margin is largest in the top acceleration quartile (where $\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$, hence the reaction, is largest) and near-zero in the bottom quartile — directly measuring where [[2509.21231|SEEC]]'s negligible-back-coupling premise breaks.
   - *Test*: bin HEX's 8 OOD scene-variation tasks by peak arm acceleration; report margin per bin, and report the measured arm→base reaction magnitude vs SEEC's "negligible" threshold in the top bin.
   - *Row*: HEX (implicit) / SEEC (explicit base→arm, arms-light premise).
   - *Falsifier*: a flat margin across bins, or the top-quartile reaction is genuinely negligible as SEEC claims → the gain isn't the reaction term.
3. **H3 — A shared *predictor* carries more coupling than a shared *observation*.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s "mutual awareness" (shared observation, emergent coupling) recovers less of the 0.60→0.37 force-tracking gain than a shared explicit predictor at equal capacity — i.e., predicting the reaction beats merely observing the other agent.
   - *Test*: ablate FALCON's shared observation vs a shared $\hat\delta_{\text{base}}$ predictor on the same N/M/L-force tracking task.
   - *Row*: FALCON (emergent).
   - *Falsifier*: shared observation matches the shared predictor → emergence is sufficient, explicit prediction is redundant.
4. **H4 — Anticipating the reaction beats anticipating proprioception alone.**
   - *Prediction*: pairing the coupling term with [[2605.14417|DAJI]]'s "before the body moves" support-shift/momentum prediction beats DAJI's proprioception-only anticipation on post-reach balance recovery, and there is an optimal forecast horizon beyond which prediction drift erodes the gain.
   - *Test*: sweep HEX's review-and-forecast horizon with vs without the explicit reaction target; measure post-reach centre-of-mass recovery time.
   - *Row*: DAJI (anticipatory).
   - *Falsifier*: proprioception-only anticipation matches the reaction-augmented one → the reaction adds nothing over generic anticipation.
5. **H5 — Coupling-anticipation tightens world-frame precision during aggressive reaches.**
   - *Prediction*: stacking an explicit coupling predictor under [[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s Commander/Tracker pushes world-frame end-effector error below 12.4 mm *specifically during* the aggressive-reach phases HiWET flags as worst for gait destabilization, with no precision loss between reaches.
   - *Test*: report HiWET-stack EE RMSE during-vs-between fast-reach phases, with and without the coupling predictor.
   - *Row*: HiWET (precision-capping).
   - *Falsifier*: error during aggressive reaches is unchanged → the coupling is already absorbed by HiWET's reactive base/waist modulation.
6. **H6 — Explicit coupling fits the real-time control budget.**
   - *Prediction*: the coupling predictor (a learned residual over a nominal analytic $M_{\text{base,arm}}$) runs within the [[2602.06341|HiWET]]-class loop budget (single-digit ms), so the accuracy gain comes at no inference-latency cost vs the implicit MoE.
   - *Test*: profile per-step latency of explicit vs implicit at matched accuracy on the deployment platform; report the latency–accuracy frontier.
   - *Row*: HiWET (precision-capping) / HEX (implicit).
   - *Falsifier*: the predictor exceeds the loop budget → fall back to HEX's implicit MoE.
7. **H7 — De-risk the explicit-over-implicit margin on a controllable baseline *before* the HEX backbone, and scope it to the loaded-reach tail.**
   - *Prediction*: because H1's test depends on HEX's (possibly closed) 12M-frame backbone, run the arm-acceleration-stratified explicit-vs-implicit ablation first on a fully controllable baseline — [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s shared-observation emergent-coupling policy, or a *self-trained* MoE matched to HEX's capacity — where the implicit coupling is reproducible end-to-end. The explicit-over-implicit margin will be significant in the *top* arm-acceleration quartile and ~0 in the *bottom* quartile.
   - *Test*: train both an explicit-$\hat\delta_{\text{base}}$ and an implicit-MoE head on the controllable baseline at matched capacity/data; stratify the OOD margin by peak arm acceleration; apply the decision rule.
   - *Row*: FALCON (emergent) / HEX (implicit, as the downstream confirmation target).
   - *Falsifier*: explicit-over-implicit margin significant in the top arm-acceleration quartile **and** ~0 in the bottom quartile → **A1 alive, scoped to the loaded-reach tail**, then port to the HEX backbone; else (flat margin, or the controllable MoE captures the gain everywhere) the MoE captures it and A1 does not warrant the HEX-backbone follow-on.
8. **H8 — Frontier-question gate: is the destabilizing arm→base coupling *inertial* (which A1 predicts) or *contact-wrench / support-polygon* (which A1 ignores)?**
   - *Prediction*: on a *walking* biped the dominant arm→base coupling at the destabilizing instant may be mediated through the foot–ground contact wrench and support-polygon transitions (single↔double support, impending slip), not the rigid-body inertial term $M_{\text{base,arm}}$ — so an inertial-only predictor can predict the wrong reaction exactly when it matters. A1's "whole-body" object is currently formalized as a rigid floating-base manipulator with the legs assumed away; this hypothesis instruments contact-state to find out whether that abstraction holds.
   - *Test*: instrument foot-contact state + estimated contact wrench during fast loaded reaches; decompose the measured base reaction into its inertial ($M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$) and contact-wrench / support-polygon-transition components, and report the share each carries at the moment of maximum CoM excursion **before** committing the explicit-inertial predictor.
   - *Row*: HEX (implicit) / [[2606.16542|ADAPT-Locomotion]] (momentum-observer disturbance estimate, the contact-inclusive lumped term).
   - *Falsifier*: the inertial term carries the destabilizing reaction (contact-transition share negligible at the worst instant) → the rigid-floating-base abstraction holds and A1's inertial predictor is well-posed; if the contact-wrench share dominates, A1's coupling term is specified for the wrong quantity and must be reframed as a contact-constrained hybrid-dynamics prediction.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Coupling model needs an accurate inertia model** — predicting $M_{\text{base,arm}}$ depends on the URDF/mass distribution. → Treat the coupling predictor as a learned residual over a nominal analytic model; report sim-vs-real coupling-prediction error, and lean on [[2604.07993|HEX]]'s learned MoE where analytic terms are uncertain.
> - **Explicit may not beat implicit** — HEX's MoE already captures the coupling well, so an explicit term risks adding parameters for no gain. → This is exactly the bet's falsifier (H1): run it head-to-head on the same data at matched latency; if explicit ≤ implicit, report it and keep the MoE.
> - **Gains may be platform-specific** — 79.8%/61.8% is on one humanoid. → H2/H5 test across reach speeds and at world-frame precision; report the margin by motion-aggressiveness class, not a single average ([[2604.07993|HEX]] is already cross-embodiment-pretrained).
> - **Explicit coupling adds latency** — predicting the reaction must fit the control loop. → H6 bounds the predictor to the [[2602.06341|HiWET]]-class real-time budget; if it cannot fit, fall back to HEX's implicit MoE and report the latency–accuracy trade.
> - **Explicit structure may only win in the low-data tail (the regime-boundary contradiction with D2)** — A1 bets that pushing *back* toward an explicit predicted arm→base term beats the implicit end-to-end MoE on fixed data, but D2's sibling evidence (a fine-tuned generalist [[2602.05791|XHugWBC]] gains +10% peak) is that explicit/cheap structure tends to win briefly on a compute budget and then lose to the scaled end-to-end model. → Do **not** assert structure-always-wins: treat the explicit term as the data/compute-efficient regime and *publish the crossover boundary* — at what training-data/compute scale the implicit MoE overtakes the explicit predictor (H1/H7 measure the margin, this risk measures where it expires), reporting the regime where structure is the asset rather than the expiring move.
> - **The coupling may be contact-wrench-dominated, not inertial — the rigid-floating-base abstraction may mis-specify it** — A1 predicts the inertial cross-term $M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$, but at the instant a walking biped breaks or shifts foot contact the destabilizing arm→base reaction may be dominated by contact-wrench / support-polygon transitions (single↔double support, impending slip) the rigid abstraction omits. → Instrument contact-state and decompose the measured base reaction into inertial vs contact-wrench shares *before* committing the explicit predictor (H8); if the contact-wrench share dominates at the worst instant, reframe A1's term as a contact-constrained hybrid-dynamics prediction.

### A2 — Skill-Blending vs Monolithic Whole-Body Control

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | A — Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation |
| **Thesis** | Compose whole-body loco-manipulation from *frozen, individually-feasible* primitives or feasible reference priors, not from one monolithic policy retrained per task. The reason it must work: a softmax-weighted blend of feasible skills *tends to stay* feasible — an empirical property of the combination rule (per SkillBlender's softmax ablation), not a convexity guarantee and not a per-task reward. The field assumes whole-body competence needs per-task reward engineering on a monolith. The bet is in First-principles below — composition matches the monolith's outperformance with zero per-task reward tuning and feeds higher-quality references downstream. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (benchmark), [[2408.00342\|MuJoCo-MPC-HumanoidBench]] (benchmark), [[2506.09366\|SkillBlender]] (method), [[2603.20147\|AGILE]] (benchmark), [[2604.00202\|DreamControl-v2]] (method), [[2606.03536\|Bionic-Whole-Body-Control]] (method), [[2410.21229\|HOVER]] (method), [[2509.13780\|Behavior-Foundation-Model-Humanoid]] (method), [[2606.17833\|HumanoidArena]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2506.09366\|SkillBlender]] consistently outperforms vanilla-RL + hierarchical baselines across 8 loco-manip tasks (3 embodiments H1/G1/H1-2, 4 primitive skills) while avoiding reward hacking; [[2408.00342\|MuJoCo-MPC-HumanoidBench]] dense-cost MPC beats DreamerV3/TD-MPC2/SAC/PPO on HumanoidBench, stable over 8 s where sparse-reward MPC degrades — the monolithic comparator; [[2604.00202\|DreamControl-v2]] RL success 0.925 vs 0.101 zero-shot on complex Group-1, 68% valid-trajectory rate vs 8%, 8 real G1 skills; [[2606.03536\|Bionic-Whole-Body-Control]] 96.0% hardware (125 trials) with foot-sliding 0.004722 m/frame; [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] Kinematic-Manifold-Prior halves hand error as the primitive-prior precedent; [[2508.16943\|LHM-Humanoid]] 71.14% Success-All (unified distilled-monolith student) vs End-to-End RL 0.00% on training tasks, 61.60% on 66 unseen tasks, 18.07% on five-object continuous-episode sequences across 350 messy scenes — the long-horizon no-reset distilled-monolith comparator; [[2403.17367\|RoboDuet]] 67% workspace expansion (51.36→85.79 m³), 23% higher real transfer SR, self-collision 50%→5%, zero-shot Go1→Go2/A1 — the cooperative-decoupled dual-controller baseline the online blend must beat; [[2603.13707\|REFINE-DP]] 90%+ sim SR (97-99% joint-optimized) vs 50-70% baselines and 95% SR from a 50-traj prior — the refine-the-upstream-prior arm of H4 (vs DreamControl-v2's frozen prior); [[2606.17833\|HumanoidArena]] cross-GMT transfer drops 39.9% with 0.0% relative retention on many of 7 leg-critical tasks — the policy-tracker-interface substrate for the pipeline-location head-to-head |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: the reflexive recipe for a new whole-body task is "tune the reward until the monolith does it," but a high-DoF monolith must rediscover feasibility from scratch every time, and [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]] shows task-specific reward engineering "leads to reward hacking and unnatural behaviors."
- **Today's answers**: systems all confine the action to a feasible set, but at *different points in the pipeline*: [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]] blends frozen primitives *during* control (convex hull); [[2606.23680|CoorDex]] coordinates a *latent residual* over frozen body+hand priors (0% without it on WalkGrab); [[2604.00202|DreamControl-v2]] (0.925 vs 0.101 RL) and [[2606.03536|Bionic-Whole-Body-Control]] (96.0% hardware) bake feasibility into a prior *before* control; [[2606.06139|MotionDisco]] discovers it from a *planner* with no demos; [[2606.26201|OmniContact]] composes frozen meta-skills through an interpretable *contact-flow intermediate* (98.7% Carry-Box, multi-stage where baselines hit 0%); [[2602.13850|Humanoid-Hanoi]] composes by *sequencing* skills through a shared adaptive controller (49% complete-task vs baseline adaptation). Meanwhile the wave of strong reuse-vs-monolith systems goes the *opposite* way — [[2506.12779|Experts-to-Generalist]] (66.84% generalist) and [[2606.06493|HANDOFF]] (0.31 m³ workspace vs FALCON's 0.06) *distill* experts into one monolith. So the reuse-vs-monolith field is split: blend-and-keep-frozen vs distill-into-one, and no one has run online-blend against upstream-prior against the distilled monolith on the same skills. (The downstream-RL-reference half — feasible reference → better RL — is now near-consensus across these systems; the live novelty is the pipeline-location head-to-head.)
- **The opening**: SkillBlender's softmax ablation is the legible mechanism: drop the simplex constraint and the feasibility metrics collapse, so the convex-combination rule, not a per-task reward, is what keeps the blend executable.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A softmax-weighted blend of frozen, individually-feasible primitives keeps the weights on the simplex the primitives span — so a mix of feasible skills *tends to stay* feasible with no per-task feasibility reward. This is *not* guaranteed by convexity alone: dynamic/contact-balance feasibility is a non-convex set, so a blend of two balance-feasible motions is not provably feasible. The claim is *empirical* — feasibility is a property of the *combination rule*, evidenced by [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]'s softmax-removal ablation (drop the simplex constraint and the feasibility metrics collapse) — not a theorem about the feasible set's geometry.
- **Assumption being challenged**: That whole-body competence needs task-specific reward engineering on a monolith — the implicit bet of the standard RL recipe and the framing [[2403.10506|HumanoidBench]] documents (flat RL fails, hierarchy helps). [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]'s reward-hacking diagnosis relocates the binding constraint to *feasibility-preserving composition*: a monolith without the feasible substrate rediscovers feasibility and hacks the reward to do it. [[2604.00202|DreamControl-v2]] and [[2606.03536|Bionic-Whole-Body-Control]] show the same constraint is satisfiable *upstream* of control, not just during it.
- **The bet**: The load-bearing claim is the *pipeline-location head-to-head* no one has run: online convex-blend ([[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]) vs upstream feasible prior ([[2604.00202|DreamControl-v2]]) vs a distilled-experts monolith ([[2506.12779|Experts-to-Generalist]] 66.84% / [[2606.06493|HANDOFF]] 0.31 m³) on *one* shared 8-task / 3-embodiment skill set with *zero* per-task reward tuning. Prediction: the feasibility-by-construction blend matches SkillBlender's outperformance of vanilla-RL + hierarchical baselines while the monolith reward-hacks, and the upstream prior used as a downstream-RL reference lifts the valid-trajectory rate toward DreamControl-v2's 68% (vs 8%) and RL success toward 0.925 (vs 0.101) *more* than blend-during-control. Falsifiable: if a reward-tuned monolith (RL or distilled) matches the blend without hacking the feasibility metrics, or if pipeline location makes no difference, composition buys nothing.

**Related research papers.**

Thirty-six feasibility mechanisms that differ on *where in the pipeline* the action is confined — the axis the whole direction tests (during control / jointly-trained-MoE / soft-routing / upstream prior / frozen-prior residual / from a planner / by-sequencing / coordinated-latent-residual / contact-flow intermediate / distilled-into-monolith / capability-aligned-distilled / by-part / generation-layer):

| System | Feasibility mechanism | Enforced | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [[2506.09366\|SkillBlender]] | per-joint softmax blend of 4 frozen primitives (convex hull) | *during* control | outperforms vanilla-RL + hierarchical on 8 SkillBench tasks (H1/G1/H1-2); softmax-removal ablation collapses feasibility | state-based only, not deployed real (its named sim2real gap) |
| [[2604.00202\|DreamControl-v2]] | guided diffusion prior trained directly in robot action space | *before* control (upstream) | 68% valid trajectories vs 8% inference-time; 0.925 vs 0.101 RL; 8 real G1 skills | a prior for tracking, not a *composable* primitive set — can't recombine online |
| [[2606.03536\|Bionic-Whole-Body-Control]] | contact + smoothness regularizers on decoded motion (physics-regularized prior) | *before* control (upstream) | 96.0% hardware (125 trials); foot-sliding 0.004722 m/frame; one tracker for all styles | feasibility for *style transfer*, not loco-manip task composition |
| [[2606.06139\|MotionDisco]] | LLM-evolutionary contact-plan search + kinodynamic opt | *from a planner* (no demos) | 8/8 long-horizon, zero-shot real | per-task search, no reusable feasible library (also D3) |
| [[2602.13850\|Humanoid-Hanoi]] | sequencing reusable skills through a shared dynamics-adaptive controller | *by sequencing* (reuse) | 49% complete-task in sim under dynamics randomization vs baseline adaptation; 40% Digit V3 hardware | composes by *temporal hand-off* not online convex blend — feasibility is per-skill, not a hull guarantee at every instant (the sequencing alternative to blending) |
| [[2506.12779\|Experts-to-Generalist]] | autoencoder-clustered experts distilled into one generalist policy | *distilled into a monolith* | 66.84% MuJoCo generalist vs <51% baselines; expert SR 51.49%→70.37% via cluster-specific delta-FT; real G1 | collapses the skills into a single policy — the *opposite* of frozen-blend, so it can't recombine online; the distill-to-monolith comparator A2 must beat |
| [[2606.06493\|HANDOFF]] | language-driven whole-body controller distilling experts, no task-specific data | *distilled into a monolith* | 0.31 m³ workspace at 97.7% feasibility vs FALCON 0.06 / SONIC 0.26; 0.06 m/s v_x error, real G1 | a single distilled controller — large workspace but no composable frozen library; the generalist-monolith comparator on the reuse-vs-monolith axis |
| [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] | pretrained Kinematic Manifold Prior in a residual action space | *before* control (kinematic) | halves hand error (12.4 vs 25.2 mm) by holding the upper body to a feasible manifold | the prior is *kinematic* only — constrains configuration, not the contact/dynamics feasibility the diffusion priors add |
| [[2506.12851\|KungfuBot]] | filter raw human video into physically-feasible references, then track with adaptive RL | *before* control (per-clip) | 53.25 mm vs >233 mm baselines on agile skills | fixes the reference per-clip, not a *composable* prior — feasibility doesn't transfer to unseen mixes |
| [[2504.11054\|Meta-Motivo]] | behavioral foundation model (FB-CPR), zero-shot WBC with no per-task reward | learned latent (empirical) | preferred over reward-optimized agents; natural motion | a single learned latent substrate, not an *explicitly feasible* primitive set — feasibility is empirical not guaranteed |
| [[2405.18418\|Puppeteer]] | hierarchical world model: frozen tracker + high-level commander (56-DoF) | two-level decomposition | 97.8% naturalness, zero-shot to 3× larger gaps | tracker + planner, not a *blend* of many feasible primitives — can't recombine skills the tracker never saw |
| [[2604.01158\|SMASH]] | motion-prior-augmented mocap + motion-matching RL (whole-body striking) | motion-prior (per-skill) | 93.7% contact / 59.7% return, outdoor egocentric | the prior regularizes one striking skill, not a composable library |
| [[2604.24833\|MotionBricks]] | modular latent "smart primitives" for real-time motion | latent primitive set | real G1 deployment, zero-shot generalization | built for animation authoring — optimizes a keyframe interface over loco-manip task feasibility |
| [[2604.11251\|CLAW]] | composable language-annotated whole-body motion generation | *generation* layer | composes text → motion | no feasibility guarantee on the *decoded robot trajectory* |

| [[2403.17367\|RoboDuet]] | two cooperating RL policies (Loco + Arm) sharing a command interface, the Arm policy guiding base pitch/roll to expand reach | *by cooperating decoupled policies* | 67% workspace expansion (51.36→85.79 m³), 23% higher real transfer SR (39/60 vs 32/60), self-collision 50%→5%, zero-shot Go1→Go2/A1 | the cooperative-decoupled precedent FALCON-LocoMan's coordinator and HANDOFF's distill both build on — but the two policies are *jointly* RL-trained per task, not frozen feasible primitives recombined online (the dual-controller baseline the blend must beat) |

| [[2410.21229\|HOVER]] | multi-mode policy *distillation* into a single controller covering 8 control modes | *distilled into a monolith* | outperforms specialist policies in **11/12** real-robot metrics on a 19-DoF H1; lower tracking error across all 32 metrics vs multi-mode RL | the canonical distilled-into-one comparator the bet must beat — the published baseline [[2509.13780\|Behavior-Foundation-Model-Humanoid]] and FARM beat — but a single monolith with no composable frozen library, so it cannot recombine online (the H1 distilled-generalist arm) |

| [[2511.06371\|Adaptive-Humanoid-Control-Multi-Behavior]] | distills independently-trained fall-recovery + walking policies into ONE MoE controller, needing PCGrad + behavior-specific critics to stop gradient conflict collapsing the monolith | *distilled into a monolith* | **0.975** slope-locomotion / **1.000** slope fall-recovery SR vs HOMIE 0.599 / HoST 0.978; smoother motions; zero-shot real G1 | exposes the conflicting-skills failure the bet predicts — a distilled monolith must add PCGrad + per-behavior critics to reconcile incompatible skills, exactly the cost composition avoids; a sharper H1 distilled arm than a generic multi-task policy |
| [[2606.23680\|CoorDex]] | a coordinated *latent residual* over separate frozen body (29-DoF) + wrist-stabilized hand (20-DoF) priors, sharing a coordination trunk with per-subsystem residual heads | *during* control (coordinated latent residual over frozen priors) | **0.55** WalkGrab (continuous **0.25** m/s, no falls) vs **0%** joint-space / **0%** body-prior-only; **0.66** OpenFridge, **0.89** WalkPickTurn; coordinated beats monolithic-latent (action rate **0.22** vs **0.40**) | the closest external instance of A2's H3 (a bounded residual over frozen feasible priors extends coverage) *plus* the H1 monolithic-vs-composed control — but the priors are *empirical* latents, not an explicitly-feasible simplex hull, so the residual can drift off the priors' feasible set; coordinated body+hand split distinct from [[2506.09366\|SkillBlender]]'s per-joint softmax and [[2503.08564\|MoE-Loco]]'s online gate (feeds A4) |
| [[2606.26201\|OmniContact]] | chains frozen RL meta-skills through a *contact-flow* intermediate (sparse body targets + binary contact signals), a rule-based CF-Gen planner composing + replanning while one CF-Track policy executes | *by an intermediate representation* (contact-flow composition) | **98.7%** Carry-Box / **82.5%** Push-Suitcase (**+40.9%** avg, baselines **0%** multi-stage); replanning **82.5%→94.5%** Push-Suitcase / **56.6%→80.5%** Stack-Boxes; 40-min / 1000-skill-round endurance | composes by an *explicit interpretable intermediate* (contact flow) rather than a convex blend or a learned router — the H6 planner-discovered-feasible-motion-is-recyclable face on a *real long-horizon* deployment, but the composition is rule-based symbolic chaining over a single execution policy, not a feasibility-by-construction hull (the contact-flow intermediate the table lacked, distinct from [[2606.06139\|MotionDisco]]'s per-task planner search and [[2602.13850\|Humanoid-Hanoi]]'s temporal hand-off) |
| [[2607.04837\|Athena-WBC]] | two-round teacher training identifies residual long-tail failures, then distills *capability-aligned* dynamic + balance experts (conservative-penalty removal + Grad-CAPS smoothness / gravity-continuation curriculum) into one DAgger student | *distilled into a monolith* (capability-aligned) | **99.26%** D_AMASS-eval / **94.89%** D_Omni-eval held-out SR (from 98.18 / 91.81); strongest balance-subset **94.73%** SR / **88.61 mm** MPJPE-W; removing conservative penalties lifts SR to **99.46%** but at Action Rate **1.46** vs **0.54** | exposes the *capability bottleneck* the distilled monolith must engineer around: conservative reward penalties suppress the aggressive actuation long-tail motions need, so the student needs per-expert acquisition recipes to recover them, the acquisition-side analog of [[2511.06371\|Adaptive-Humanoid-Control-Multi-Behavior]]'s PCGrad + per-behavior critics (a sharper H1 distilled-arm than a generic multi-task policy). It stays a single distilled controller with no composable frozen library |
| [[2607.18362\|FARO]] | a nested *hierarchy of feasibility tests* (cheap IK mode/edge check → Kinematic Sequence Optimization → full Trajectory Optimization) pruning infeasible contact-mode sequences in a UCT tree search before paying for dynamics | *from a planner* (hierarchical feasibility pruning, cached) | KSO ≈**2 orders of magnitude** faster than full TO (**0.60s** vs **64.70s**), **74.8×** fewer decision variables at **70.0%** constraint coverage; feasibility-guided search finds **26.4** solutions vs TO-only's **0** in a hard box-placement task; filters LLM-generated (GPT-5.5) contact plans at up to **15.5×** speedup with **0.0–1.3%** false-negative rate; real G1 execution via an RL tracker | sharpens [[2606.06139\|MotionDisco]]'s "per-task search, no reusable library" gap from the *cost* side — a cheap kinematic proxy (not a distilled skill library) prunes the combinatorial search before full TO, and its near-zero false-negative rate is direct evidence a cheaper feasibility test can safely gate a planner without discarding true solutions (H6's recyclability premise), but it still searches per-task rather than caching a transferable primitive set |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (convex composition matches the monolith with zero per-task reward), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Composition matches the reward-tuned *and* the distilled monolith with zero per-task reward.**
   - *Prediction*: a convex-hull blend controller equals [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]'s outperformance of vanilla-RL + hierarchical baselines on all 8 SkillBench tasks (3 embodiments) using *one* shared reward, while a monolith — RL, [[2408.00342|MuJoCo-MPC-HumanoidBench]]'s dense-cost MPC re-solved per step, *or* a distilled-experts policy ([[2506.12779|Experts-to-Generalist]] / [[2606.06493|HANDOFF]]) — needs per-task tuning and either hacks the feasibility metrics or destabilizes over the long 8 s horizon to keep up.
   - *Test*: run blend vs RL-monolith vs MPC-monolith vs distilled-monolith on SkillBench with a fixed reward; report SR and feasibility (jerk/limit) per task, over short *and* 8 s episodes, and on [[2508.16943|LHM-Humanoid]]'s continuous-episode no-reset suite (350 scenes) where the long-horizon instability surfaces — LHM-Humanoid is itself a distilled-monolith comparator (unified student 71.14% Success-All vs End-to-End RL 0.00%).
   - *Row*: SkillBlender (during control) vs MuJoCo MPC HumanoidBench (planner monolith) vs Experts-to-Generalist / HANDOFF (distilled monolith) vs LHM-Humanoid (distilled monolith, no-reset long-horizon).
   - *Falsifier*: a single-reward monolith (RL, MPC, or distilled) matches without hacking and stays stable over 8 s → composition buys nothing.
2. **H2 — Per-joint resolution is what earns the coordination.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]'s per-joint softmax beats scalar per-skill weighting most on tasks needing *different* primitive mixes at the same instant (reach-while-stepping, where arm and leg disagree), and ties on single-skill tasks.
   - *Test*: ablate per-joint vs scalar weights; stratify by simultaneous-mix demand.
   - *Row*: SkillBlender (during control).
   - *Falsifier*: scalar matches per-joint on mix-heavy tasks → joint resolution is not the lever.
3. **H3 — A bounded out-of-hull residual extends coverage before hacking returns.**
   - *Prediction*: a learned residual outside the convex hull raises novel-skill coverage with reward hacking re-appearing only past a measurable residual budget — i.e., there is a non-trivial off-hull margin where feasibility still holds. [[2606.23680|CoorDex]]'s coordinated latent residual over frozen body+hand priors is the external existence proof a bounded residual works (0% without it on WalkGrab), and its monolithic-vs-coordinated-residual gap (action rate 0.22 vs 0.40) is the over-budget-residual failure this hypothesis bounds.
   - *Test*: extend SkillBlender's softmax ablation by adding a norm-bounded residual; sweep the bound and watch the jerk/limit collapse threshold, with [[2606.23680|CoorDex]]'s coordinated-vs-monolithic residual as the matched external control.
   - *Row*: SkillBlender (during control) / CoorDex (coordinated latent residual over frozen priors).
   - *Falsifier*: any off-hull residual immediately collapses feasibility → frozen primitives are a hard ceiling.
4. **H4 — Upstream feasible prior beats during-control blending, and beats post-hoc generation-filtering, as an RL reference.**
   - *Prediction*: using [[2604.00202|DreamControl-v2]]'s upstream robot-space diffusion prior (or [[2606.03536|Bionic-Whole-Body-Control]]'s physics-regularized prior) as the downstream-RL reference lifts valid-trajectory rate toward 68% (vs 8%) and RL success toward 0.925 (vs 0.101) *more* than SkillBlender's during-control blend and *more* than [[2606.10340|OMG]]'s generate-then-sim-filter feasibility on the same skills — guaranteeing feasibility upstream beats curating it after generation.
   - *Test*: head-to-head, same skill set, same RL loop, swap only the feasibility source (upstream prior / during-control blend / OMG-style post-hoc sim-filter).
   - *Row*: DreamControl-v2 / Bionic (upstream) vs SkillBlender (during control) vs OMG (generation-filtered).
   - *Falsifier*: during-control blend or post-hoc filtering matches the upstream prior → pipeline location doesn't matter.
5. **H5 — Primitive-library size amortizes across tasks (coverage per primitive beats reward tuning).**
   - *Prediction*: sweeping frozen-primitive count beyond SkillBlender's 4 raises composable-task breadth sub-linearly to a saturation point, and a learned primitive set covers more tasks-per-primitive than a hand-designed one.
   - *Test*: vary primitive count + learned-vs-handcrafted; report tasks-per-primitive-set, not single-task SR.
   - *Row*: SkillBlender (during control) / MotionBricks (latent primitive set).
   - *Falsifier*: coverage scales linearly with primitives → composition just re-hides the engineering cost.
6. **H6 — A planner-discovered feasible motion is recyclable, not just per-task.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2606.06139|MotionDisco]]'s LLM-evolutionary contact plans, when distilled into a reusable feasible library, transfer to *unseen* long-horizon tasks without re-running the per-task search — closing its named "per-task search, no reusable library" gap.
   - *Test*: distill MotionDisco plans into primitives, evaluate zero-shot on [[2508.16943|LHM-Humanoid]]'s 66-task OOD held-out long-horizon split (61.60% Success-All unseen) vs re-searching per task.
   - *Row*: MotionDisco (from a planner) / LHM-Humanoid (66-task OOD long-horizon transfer).
   - *Falsifier*: distilled plans don't transfer → planner feasibility is irreducibly per-task.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Frozen primitives cap the achievable behavior** — a behavior outside the primitive hull is unreachable by pure blending. → Allow a learned residual outside the convex hull for novel skills (couples to A1's coupling residual); H3 reports how large that residual can grow before reward hacking returns, and the fraction of tasks needing residual vs pure blend.
> - **Primitive library design is itself engineering** — picking the right primitives replaces reward tuning with primitive design. → H5 tests learned vs hand-designed primitives; the win is amortization across many tasks, so report tasks-per-primitive-set, not single-task SR.
> - **Sim-to-real gap on the blend** — SkillBlender's own limitation is state-based observation, no real deployment. → Borrow the diffusion-prior anchors' hardware recipe ([[2606.03536|Bionic-Whole-Body-Control]] runs 125 real trials); report real-robot feasibility, not just sim accuracy.
> - **Blending may produce discontinuous transitions** — switching weights mid-motion can jerk. → [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]'s softmax smooths weights; report motion-quality (jerk/limit) diagnostics per [[2603.20147|AGILE]], treat smoothness as first-class.

### A3 — World-Frame End-Effector Tracking with the Base as an Active DoF

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | A — Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation |
| **Thesis** | Formulate loco-manipulation as world-frame end-effector tracking where the base is an *actively-commanded* transport DoF, not a robot-centric controller that drifts. The reason it must work: a task target lives in the world frame, but a robot-centric policy folds base motion into cumulative drift, so long-horizon precision needs the loop closed in world coordinates and the base used to extend reach. The field assumes body-centric control suffices for task-space precision. The bet is in First-principles below — world-frame closure plus active base transport holds millimetre precision where a matched body-centric policy drifts with distance. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] (method), [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (benchmark), [[2603.20147\|AGILE]] (benchmark), [[2603.03279\|ULTRA]] (method), [[2503.05652\|BRS]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] 12.4 mm sim / 12–15 mm real world-frame end-effector RMSE, Kinematic-Manifold-Prior halves hand error (25.2 mm w/o), active base + waist modulation via a tunable α to trade reachability against balance; [[2603.03279\|ULTRA]] 73% dense-reference tracking + 50–90% sparse-goal real G1, RL finetuning +200% (tripling) OOD position-only; [[2503.05652\|BRS]] 88% sub-task via autoregressive base→torso→arm decoding as the drift-mitigation precedent; [[2411.06782\|QuadWBG]] GORM expands workspace +54% volume / +33% area, 89% one-time real grasp |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: when a humanoid places a hand precisely while walking, the target is in the *world frame*, but most controllers are *robot-centric*, which [[2602.06341|HiWET]] shows "leads to cumulative world-frame drift and high-frequency oscillations" as the base walks. Error accumulates with distance, not capacity.
- **Today's answers**: world-frame closure through base motion is now emerging consensus *on quadrupeds* — [[2407.10353|UMI-on-Legs]] makes the task-frame the interface and is robust to random base transport (80% zero-shot cup rearrangement); [[2409.16048|WB-EE-Pose-Tracking]] expands the workspace "by moving the body" (2.03 cm flat / 2.64 cm stairs on ALMA); [[2507.08656|Multi-Critic-Twist-Tracking]] holds a world-frame EE pose while walking; [[2412.03012|Omni-WBLM]] tracks 6-DoF EE on a wheeled-quad (0.022 m error). On *bipedal humanoids*, [[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s tunable α makes the base-as-DoF idea operational (full HiWET 12.4 mm, removing its feasibility prior doubles error to 25.2 mm) and [[2602.16705|HERO-Humanoid-EE-Control]] reaches 2.44 cm via learned residual FK + odometry; [[2603.03279|ULTRA]] shows the command interface need not fix granularity (73% dense / 50–90% sparse, +200% OOD). The frame distinction is real, but mostly proven on quadrupeds.
- **The opening**: no one has isolated the *frame* as the lever *on a bipedal humanoid with a walking gait*: a world-frame vs body-centric controller, error tracked against locomotion distance (H1), with a *learned continuous α-schedule* (H2) rather than a tuned constant or discrete trigger — exactly the package the quadruped precedents leave open.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A manipulation target lives in the world frame; a robot-centric controller folds the floating base's motion into the end-effector estimate, so error accumulates with locomotion distance. World-frame closure is the only formulation where precision is decoupled from how far the base has walked — and the mobile base is the DoF that brings out-of-static-reach targets into reach. [[2602.06341|HiWET]] measures this directly: the world-frame formulation holds 12.4 mm, and its KMP-removal ablation doubles hand error to 25.2 mm.
- **Assumption being challenged**: That body-centric whole-body control suffices for task-space precision, and that base motion is a disturbance to reject — and the narrower convention the quadruped precedents leave intact: that the reach-vs-balance trade is a *fixed/tuned* parameter ([[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s constant α) or a *discrete* go/no-go reach-extender ([[2409.16048|WB-EE-Pose-Tracking]], [[2605.21133|Spatial-Brain-Cerebellum]]), never a *learned continuous schedule over the task*. [[2602.06341|HiWET]] beats body-centric baselines, and the drift it measures is a *frame* problem, not a capacity problem — scaling the body-centric policy cannot fix it. [[2503.05652|BRS]] confirms the same drift from the *factoring* side (autoregressive base→torso→arm vs naive joint, 13×/21× over DP3/RGB-DP), and HiWET's α shows the base is better *scheduled* than suppressed — but as a tuned constant, not learned.
- **The bet**: On a *bipedal humanoid with a walking gait*, a world-frame Commander/Tracker hierarchy with a *learned continuous α-schedule* holds [[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s 12.4 mm sim / 12–15 mm real error over long horizons, where (i) a matched body-centric controller's error grows monotonically with locomotion distance while the world-frame one stays flat (H1, the falsifier no quadruped precedent runs), and (ii) the learned α reaches more out-of-static-range targets than HiWET's tuned-constant α and [[2605.21133|Spatial-Brain-Cerebellum]]'s discrete trigger (H2). Falsifiable: if a body-centric baseline stays flat with distance, or a tuned-constant α ties the learned schedule, the frame/schedule is not the lever and closure buys nothing.

**Related research papers.**

Twenty-four systems that differ on *how the base is treated* and *which frame closes the loop* — the axis the direction tests (note: the world-frame-through-base-motion precedents are mostly *quadrupeds*, not bipedal humanoids):

| System | Base treatment | Frame | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] | active transport DoF, scheduled by tunable α | world-frame | 12.4 mm sim / 12–15 mm real; KMP halves hand error (25.2 mm without) | α is tuned/fixed, not a learned schedule over the task |
| [[2603.03279\|ULTRA]] | physics-retargeted demos, masked command interface | world-frame (retarget) | 73% dense / 50–90% sparse real G1; RL finetune +200% OOD position-only | a flexible interface, but no explicit base-as-transport scheduling |
| [[2407.10353\|UMI-on-Legs]] | random base transport, task-frame manipulation interface | task-frame | 80% zero-shot cup rearrangement on a quadruped, 90% kettlebell push, iPhone odometry | *quadruped*, no bipedal gait; world-frame robustness shown but no learned reach-vs-balance schedule, no error-vs-distance curve |
| [[2409.16048\|WB-EE-Pose-Tracking]] | base actively expands the workspace "by moving the body" | world-frame | 2.03 cm flat / 2.64 cm stairs on ALMA; beats fine-tuned MPC (6.43 cm) | *quadruped* (ALMA); reach extension is implicit in tracking, not a learned continuous α |

| [[2606.25706\|ASYNC-3PT]] | tracks *asynchronous + structurally-incomplete* sparse upper-body task-space references via time-indexed conditioning over the whole future trajectory + a sliding-window global reward, with a kinematic-MPC reference-completion and self-guidance RL preserving natural lower-body motion | task-space (humanoid, sparse/asynchronous command) | **99.50%** asynchronous-tracking SR; **6.90** cm / **6.83°** asynchronous-interval error; safe OOD adaptation with no joint-limit violations, real G1 (waving / deep-squat / forward-walk) | the *asynchronous-sparse-command* face of the command interface — addresses the frame/rate mismatch between low-frequency task references and high-frequency control that [[2603.03279\|ULTRA]]'s masked dense/sparse interface and [[2606.18772\|HALOMI]]'s sparse head/hand targets leave implicit, sharpening A3's H4 (command-granularity earns OOD) — but it tracks the *upper-body task-space* target without the base as a scheduled reach-extending transport DoF and runs no world-frame error-vs-distance curve |
