| [[2607.11041\|PAKE]] | a hierarchical Kinematic-Normalizing-Flow (KNF) over redundant torso+arm solutions; a high-level controller navigates the KNF's latent space to pick a redundancy-aware reference, decoupling *kinematic reference generation* from *dynamic execution* rather than scheduling a single base-transport DoF | body-frame (redundancy-exploiting reference selection) | feasible workspace **2.3867 m³** (pos<0.1 m, orient<0.2 rad) vs [[2407.10353\|UMI-on-Legs]] **0.0174 m³** and VBC **0.4228 m³**; sim EE error **0.0402 m** / **0.0609 rad**; real quadruped-manipulator across **8** loco-manip tasks, **0.0449 m** / **0.1437 rad** | a direct head-to-head against a *table-cited* quadruped precedent (UMI-on-Legs) with a ~137× workspace-volume margin — but the redundancy it exploits is torso+arm kinematic multiplicity, not a *world-frame* closure or a *learned continuous reach-vs-balance α*; no error-vs-locomotion-distance curve is run (H1), so it sharpens H2's comparator set (QuadWBG's GORM / HiWET's tuned-α / Spatial-Brain-Cerebellum's discrete solver) without settling the frame question |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (world-frame closure + active base transport holds precision where body-centric drifts), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Body-centric error grows with locomotion distance; world-frame stays flat — the curve no precedent has run on a bipedal gait.**
   - *Prediction*: tracking end-effector RMSE against cumulative base-travel distance, a [[2602.06341|HiWET]]/[[2602.16705|HERO-Humanoid-EE-Control]]-class world-frame humanoid controller stays near 12.4 mm while a matched body-centric baseline's error rises monotonically with distance — the controlled isolation the *quadruped* precedents ([[2407.10353|UMI-on-Legs]], [[2409.16048|WB-EE-Pose-Tracking]]) demonstrate the frame but never plot.
   - *Test*: on a bipedal humanoid, same tasks + policy capacity, vary walk distance to the target; plot RMSE vs distance for both frames.
   - *Row*: HiWET / HERO (world-frame, humanoid) vs BRS (robot-centric); UMI-on-Legs / WB-EE Pose Tracking (world-frame, quadruped) as the precedents that show the frame but omit the curve.
   - *Falsifier*: body-centric stays flat with distance → the frame isn't the lever and closure buys nothing.
2. **H2 — A learned α-schedule extends reachable workspace beyond fixed/discrete base transport.**
   - *Prediction*: a policy over [[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s α (lower to extend reach when the target is out of static range, raise during aggressive locomotion) reaches more out-of-static-range targets than HiWET's fixed/ablated α *and* than [[2605.21133|Spatial-Brain-Cerebellum]]'s discrete go/no-go trigger, with the gain concentrated on reach-extension tasks.
   - *Test*: compare scheduled-α vs fixed-α vs discrete-solver on reachable-workspace coverage; report which task classes are load-bearing.
   - *Row*: HiWET (world-frame) / Spatial Brain Cerebellum (solver).
   - *Falsifier*: scheduled α ties fixed α → the schedule adds nothing over a tuned constant.
3. **H3 — The Kinematic Manifold Prior is the precision mechanism and transfers across embodiments.**
   - *Prediction*: constraining the action to [[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s feasible kinematic manifold is what halves hand error (12.4 vs 25.2 mm) and accelerates RL, and the same KMP-style prior carries the precision gain to a second embodiment.
   - *Test*: KMP-ablation on the home platform + KMP-transfer to a different humanoid; report precision + RL sample-efficiency delta.
   - *Row*: HiWET (world-frame).
   - *Falsifier*: KMP-removal leaves precision intact, or the prior fails to transfer → feasibility-manifold constraint isn't the mechanism.
4. **H4 — Mixing dense + sparse commands at train time earns the OOD-tripling.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2603.03279|ULTRA]]'s +200% OOD position-only gain comes from training the *one* controller on both dense-reference (73%) and sparse-goal (50–90%) commands, not from either alone — a single-granularity controller underperforms the mixed one OOD.
   - *Test*: train dense-only, sparse-only, and mixed; evaluate OOD position-only.
   - *Row*: ULTRA (world-frame retarget).
   - *Falsifier*: a single-granularity controller matches the mixed one OOD → the interface mix isn't the lever.
5. **H5 — World-frame closure shifts the bottleneck into base-state estimation.**
   - *Prediction*: under world-frame closure, end-effector error becomes dominated by base-state-estimation error — degrading the estimator degrades precision roughly linearly, and [[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s reported ~10 mm cost from removing the estimator is recoverable by a better one.
   - *Test*: inject controlled base-state-estimation noise; plot EE error vs estimation error; swap estimators.
   - *Row*: HiWET (world-frame).
   - *Falsifier*: EE error is insensitive to estimation noise → estimation is not the new bottleneck.
6. **H6 — Active base transport degrades manipulation precision unless coupling-compensated.**
   - *Prediction*: moving the base mid-reach raises tracking error *during* transport phases vs between them, and stacking A1's coupling-aware predictor under the tracker closes that during-vs-between gap.
   - *Test*: report RMSE during vs between base-transport phases, with and without the coupling predictor.
   - *Row*: HiWET (world-frame) / HEX (robot-centric, the coupled-dynamics layer).
   - *Falsifier*: no during-vs-between gap exists → base transport is not itself a disturbance and coupling compensation is unnecessary.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **World-frame closure needs accurate base state estimation** — drift moves from the controller into the estimator. → [[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s own ablation shows removing the state estimator costs ~10 mm; H5 reports end-effector error vs state-estimation error, treating estimation as a first-class dependency.
> - **Active base transport can destabilize manipulation** — moving the base mid-reach is itself a disturbance (couples to A1). → Use A1's coupling-aware predictor under the tracker (H6); schedule α (H2) so reach-extension and balance don't fight; report tracking error during vs between base-transport phases.
> - **12.4 mm may not generalize across tasks/embodiments** — one platform, specific tasks. → H1/H3 test over horizon and across embodiments; report error by task class and locomotion distance, not a single average.

### A4 — Unified-Latent Policy for Joint Loco-Manipulation Commands

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | A — Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation |
| **Thesis** | Build a single policy that emits *joint* locomotion + manipulation latent actions, not a manipulation policy stuck in a fixed workspace plus a separate locomotion stack. The reason it must work: manipulation and locomotion have *different visual dynamics*, so they need distinct latent action models, yet must be predicted jointly or the intent and the controller fall out of sync. The field assumes the policy is a manipulation model and locomotion is a downstream controller. The bet is in First-principles below — a unified-latent policy beats the best decoupled stack head-to-head on the same tasks, with the gain traced to specific ablation deltas. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2511.05936\|10-VLA-Challenges]] (survey), [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (benchmark), [[2508.16943\|LHM-Humanoid]] (benchmark), [[2603.20147\|AGILE]] (benchmark), [[2512.11047\|WholeBodyVLA]] (method), [[2506.13751\|LeVERB]] (method), [[2606.09215\|MotionWAM]] (method), [[2606.17833\|HumanoidArena]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2512.11047\|WholeBodyVLA]] 78.0% (Bag/Box/Cart) beating modular + end-to-end policies and baselines given the *same* LMO controller (GR00T-w-LMO 42.0%, OpenVLA-OFT-w-LMO 56.7%), +38.7% latent pretraining, +24.0% LMO-RL over velocity-RL; [[2506.13751\|LeVERB]] 58.5% on 10 task categories = 7.8× over naive hierarchical policy (7.5%), zero-shot sim-to-real G1; [[2606.09215\|MotionWAM]] 76.1% over 9 real tasks (+32 pp over GR00T-N1.7) at 4.9 Hz from a single unified motion latent; [[2603.12263\|Psi0]] >40% over GR00T N1.6 on 8 long-horizon loco-manip tasks, 800 h human + 30 h robot data; [[2606.22174\|OpenHLM]] 89% avg over 8 tasks from 40 demos and 87.5% long-horizon Fruit-Arrangement vs Ψ0 48.8% / GR00T N1.6 57.5% at <half the demo time — the 2026 unified-whole-body-VLA recipe whose head-to-head against Ψ0/GR00T is the strongest external decoupled-vs-unified comparator H3 needs; [[2508.16943\|LHM-Humanoid]] unified distilled student 71.14% Success-All vs End-to-End RL 0.00% (the unified-vs-monolithic head-to-head), 61.60% on 66 unseen tasks (seen/unseen OOD split), 18.07% five-object long-horizon, 63.71% egocentric-RGB VLA variant across 350 scenes; [[2606.18772\|HALOMI]] 90% (Bag Transfer, real G1) from a single manifold-constrained whole-body latent, head-to-head with WholeBodyVLA's Bag/Box/Cart 78.0% — the unified-manifold-latent real-humanoid comparator; [[2606.16696\|VENOM]] RMSE ≈ 0.42–0.58 rad matching RL experts across 5 humanoids with one shared central transformer (no body-part decoupling) — the reproducible one-LAM low-level negative control for H1; [[2606.17833\|HumanoidArena]] cross-GMT transfer drop 39.9% (TWIST2→SONIC) with relative retention reaching 0.0% — the latent↔tracker-interface eval harness H1/H3 need; [[2608.06375\|ω-0]] 81.8% SR / 90.3% task progress on 11 real household loco-manip tasks vs a video-generation WAM baseline (DiT4DiT) 43.6% and an arm-centric humanoid VLA 44.5%, future-visual-latent ablation costs the largest single drop (79.1%→64.5%): the reconstruction-free-future-latent evidence for a shared, unified action interface |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: most policies are implicitly *manipulation* models, "confined to limited workspaces" because they "lack integrated humanoid locomotion," as [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]] puts it; bolting a velocity controller underneath causes "decision-execution misalignment," where high-level intent and the controller disagree.
- **Today's answers**: "unified beats decoupled" is now the consensus headline. [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]] splits the *action* latent by visual dynamics (separate manip/loco VQ-VAE models, 78.0% beating same-LMO baselines that top out at 56.7%); [[2506.13751|LeVERB]] makes the *command* latent a kinematically-grounded "verb" (58.5%, 7.8× over naive hierarchical); [[2512.20188|Fast-Slow-WB-VLA]] gives the *unified-action-token* face (an RVQ-VAE whole-body action tokenizer, 90% scoop at 32.3 Hz, ~3× synchronous baselines, tokenizer-ablation 83.3%→0%); [[2603.05410|PhysiFlow]] gives the *semantic-latent* face at whole-body scale (74.9% on 9 tasks, +9.9 pp over LeVERB); and [[2606.22174|OpenHLM]] supplies the 2026 *empirical-recipe* face — a π0.5-init whole-body VLA over a joint-based teleop interface beating Ψ0 (48.8%) and GR00T N1.6 (57.5%) at 87.5% long-horizon with <half the demo time, but attributing the win to the teleop interface + co-training rather than any latent-architecture split. The reasoning↔control interface being a *learned latent* is no longer the contribution.
- **The opening**: the still-non-consensus part is that the *visual statistics force the architecture* — that manipulation video (near-static camera) and locomotion video (gait-moving camera) need *separate* latent models, not one shared latent. [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]] does run a two-LAM-vs-one-LAM ablation *internally* (separate-LAM 78.0% vs its own mixed-data `shared-LAM` 66.0%, a 12.0 pp gap) — but it explicitly hedges that decoupling is "beneficial but *not the primary factor*," ranking the split *third* behind action-free pretraining (+38.7%, the primary driver) and the LMO policy (+24.0%), and it provides *no stratification of the margin by camera-motion magnitude* and *no external unified-latent control* (its shared-LAM is an internal variant of its own architecture, not a [[2606.09215|MotionWAM]]-style external negative). So the *unrun* claim is narrower than "split vs shared": it is whether the split is the *lever* once isolated by camera-motion stratification against an external unified latent — the variable [[2512.20188|Fast-Slow-WB-VLA]]'s *one unified* whole-body action token and [[2606.09215|MotionWAM]]'s *one shared* motion latent take the opposite bet on but no one stratifies (H1).

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: Manipulation and locomotion have different visual dynamics (local-contact, near-static camera vs global-terrain, moving camera), so a single shared latent model underfits one — yet the next whole-body action is a *joint* over both, and predicting them through separate stacks re-introduces decision-execution misalignment. The right structure is distinct latent models with a joint prediction interface. [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]] supplies the evidence: separate VQ-VAE latents beat a shared model, +38.7% from action-free video pretraining and +24.0% from the loco-oriented RL policy, with same-LMO baselines (GR00T 42.0%, OpenVLA-OFT 56.7%) trailing its 78.0%.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "unified beats decoupled" (now consensus, [[2512.20188|Fast-Slow-WB-VLA]] / [[2603.05410|PhysiFlow]] both unify) and not even "split beats shared" in the abstract (now run *internally* by [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]], 78.0% vs 66.0%), but the sharper claim that WholeBodyVLA's *aggregate* ablation ranking settles which mechanism is load-bearing. It ranks the split *third* behind action-free pretraining (+38.7%) and the LMO command interface (+24.0%) and calls decoupling "beneficial but not the primary factor," yet both the +24.0% interface gain and the split are reported only as *aggregates*, never stratified by the variable (subgoal precision for the interface, camera-motion magnitude for the split) that should decide where each is load-bearing. [[2606.09215|MotionWAM]]'s single shared motion latent and [[2512.20188|Fast-Slow-WB-VLA]]'s single unified action token deny the split entirely; the open questions are whether the command interface earns its +24.0% mostly on precise-locomotion subgoals, and whether the split survives as a *measurable, camera-motion-stratified* effect against an *external* unified latent, not whether unification or an internal aggregate helps.
- **The bet**: The load-bearing test isolates the *command interface*, not the codebook split. A unified loco+manip latent policy beats the strongest decoupled stack, a [[2504.16054|π0.5]] / GR00T-class manipulation policy bolted onto a separate velocity controller, on the *same* Bag/Box/Cart tasks at a matched low-level controller: ≥78.0% unified vs ≤56.7% same-LMO decoupled, clearing [[2506.13751|LeVERB]]'s 58.5% (7.8× over naive hierarchical) on its 10-task suite (H3). The mechanism carrying that win is [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]]'s second-ranked +24.0% learned-LMO-over-velocity driver, and the sharper bet is that this +24.0% concentrates on tasks with precise locomotion subgoals (squat-to-place), where a continuous velocity controller induces decision-execution misalignment, and falls near-zero on coarse locomotion, a subgoal-precision stratification WholeBodyVLA never runs (H2). The visual-dynamics *split* is a secondary probe, not the headline: WholeBodyVLA ranks it third and calls it "beneficial but not the primary factor," but that verdict rests on an *aggregate* 12.0 pp margin (78.0% vs 66.0%) that averages a loco-heavy regime, where a gait-moving camera should force the split, with a manip-heavy near-static-camera regime where the split is near-zero, so the aggregate can hide a loco-heavy regime in which the split is load-bearing, the exact camera-motion stratification against [[2606.09215|MotionWAM]]'s *external* unified latent that could overturn the "not primary" ranking (H1). Falsifiable: if the decoupled stack ties the unified policy at a matched controller, or the +24.0% LMO gain is uniform across subgoal precision, the interface is not the lever; if an external shared latent ([[2606.09215|MotionWAM]]-style) also ties the split on the loco-heavy bins, the visual-dynamics split adds nothing beyond unification.

**Related research papers.**

Twenty-seven systems that differ on *which latent* carries the reasoning↔control interface — the axis the direction tests (action latent / unified action token / semantic verb / split-by-dynamics / shared skill-latent / arbitrary-keypoint latent / coordinated body+hand latent / contact-flow command / unified command surface / MoE policy / symbol-level / reconstruction-free future-visual latent / none):

| System | Latent type | What it splits / unifies | Key result | What's missing |

| [[2506.13751\|LeVERB]] | CVAE *semantic* "verb" latent (kinematically regularized) | bridges 10 Hz reasoner ↔ 50 Hz WBC | 58.5% on 10 tasks = 7.8× over naive hierarchical, zero-shot sim-to-real G1 | one verb latent, not split by manip-vs-loco visual dynamics |

Its *arbitrary-masked-keypoint* command surface generalizes [[2606.18772\|HALOMI]]'s fixed head+hand targets, but it is never split by manip-vs-loco visual dynamics, so it cannot isolate the split as the lever |
