**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (a unified loco+manip latent beats the decoupled stack on the same tasks), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Visual-dynamics split beats a shared latent, and the gap scales with camera motion — the stratification WholeBodyVLA's aggregate internal ablation omits.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]]'s two VQ-VAE LAMs beat a single shared LAM — using [[2606.09215|MotionWAM]]'s *external unified* whole-body motion latent as the negative, not WholeBodyVLA's own internal mixed-data shared-LAM — and, beyond its reported aggregate 12.0 pp (78.0% vs 66.0%) split→shared gap, the separate-over-shared margin grows with how much the camera moves (largest on loco-heavy splits, near-zero on manip-heavy ones). WholeBodyVLA's third-place "not the primary factor" verdict is itself an aggregation artifact: averaging the loco-heavy regime (split load-bearing) with the manip-heavy regime (split near-zero) dilutes the margin, so the stratified curve can overturn that ranking exactly where the camera moves.
   - *Test*: ablate two-LAM vs one-LAM (the MotionWAM-style unified latent) at matched capacity; stratify tasks by camera-motion magnitude on [[2508.16943|LHM-Humanoid]]'s standardized 350-scene seen/unseen suite, where the unified distilled student (71.14% Success-All) is the reproducible 'one-LAM' control.
   - *Row*: WholeBodyVLA (split by dynamics) vs MotionWAM (unified latent) / LHM-Humanoid (unified-policy substrate).
   - *Falsifier*: shared matches separate, or the gap is flat across camera-motion bins → the visual-dynamics argument doesn't hold quantitatively, and MotionWAM's unified latent is sufficient.
2. **H2 — A learned loco command interface beats a velocity controller, most on precise locomotion subgoals.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]]'s +24.0% LMO gain is largest on tasks with precise locomotion subgoals (squat-to-place) where a continuous velocity controller induces decision-execution misalignment, and near-zero on coarse-locomotion tasks.
   - *Test*: isolate LMO-RL vs velocity-RL; stratify by subgoal precision.
   - *Row*: WholeBodyVLA (split by dynamics) vs the velocity-controller baseline.
   - *Falsifier*: the LMO gain is uniform across subgoal precision → the interface isn't honoring fine intent.
3. **H3 — A unified latent beats the decoupled π0.5 + velocity stack on the same tasks.**
   - *Prediction*: the unified-latent policy beats a [[2504.16054|π0.5]] / GR00T-class manipulation policy plus a separate velocity controller — the strongest modular baseline — on the *same* Bag/Box/Cart tasks (target ≥ WholeBodyVLA's 78.0% vs the modular stack's ≤56.7% with the same LMO).
   - *Test*: head-to-head, unified vs modular, identical tasks + low-level controller, on [[2508.16943|LHM-Humanoid]]'s named task set — its unified DAgger-distilled student (71.14% Success-All) vs End-to-End RL (0.00%) is the reproducible unified-vs-monolithic protocol, with graceful-vs-collapse on 66 unseen tasks.
   - *Row*: π0.5 (decoupled comparator) / LHM-Humanoid (unified-vs-E2E-RL head-to-head).
   - *Falsifier*: the decoupled stack matches the unified policy → the interface is not the lever.
4. **H4 — Action-free human-video pretraining replaces a measurable fraction of teleop data, capped by the embodiment gap.**
   - *Prediction*: the +38.7% from [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]]'s action-free egocentric pretraining traces a data-efficiency curve where latent pretraining replaces a quantifiable share of teleop demos (toward [[2603.12263|Psi0]]'s 30 h robot / 800 h human ratio), with the human→humanoid embodiment gap setting the ceiling.
   - *Test*: sweep teleop-data fraction with vs without action-free pretraining; report SR-vs-teleop-hours.
   - *Row*: Psi0 (manip latent, scaled).
   - *Falsifier*: pretraining yield is flat in teleop data → the human-video substrate doesn't amortize teleop (couples to D1).
5. **H5 — A semantic verb keeps executable up to a capacity ceiling before collapsing to a discrete vocabulary.**
   - *Prediction*: sweeping [[2506.13751|LeVERB]]'s verb-latent dimension against its 10-task semantic coverage, the kinematic-reconstruction regularizer keeps the verb executable up to a capacity beyond which it degenerates toward a discrete action vocabulary (the failure WholeBodyVLA's separate codebooks risk).
   - *Test*: vary verb capacity with/without the kinematic regularizer; report 10-task coverage + executability.
   - *Row*: LeVERB (semantic verb).
   - *Falsifier*: executability is independent of capacity → there's no collapse boundary and the regularizer is inert.
6. **H6 — Joint prediction entangles the modalities unless the head is factored.**
   - *Prediction*: a shared prediction head over the two latents leaks locomotion into manipulation (per-modality accuracy drops vs the split prediction), and factoring the head recovers it — i.e., the dynamics coupling is real but the *representation* should stay split (couples to A1's non-separable-value insight).
   - *Test*: shared-head vs factored-head; report per-modality accuracy.
   - *Row*: WholeBodyVLA (split by dynamics) / HEX (dynamics-coupling layer).
   - *Falsifier*: the shared head shows no per-modality leakage → joint prediction doesn't entangle and factoring is unnecessary.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Two latent models double the training complexity** — separate manip/loco LAMs need their own data. → H4's data-efficiency curve via action-free human video amortizes the cost ([[2603.12263|Psi0]] runs on 30 h robot data); report teleop-data replaced per latent model.
> - **Joint prediction can entangle the modalities it separated** — a shared head may leak loco into manip. → H6 tests separate-vs-shared; if the joint head entangles, factor the prediction (couples to A1's non-separable-value insight — the dynamics coupling is real, the *representation* should still be split) and report per-modality accuracy.
> - **Latent action vocabularies can collapse** — VQ-VAE codebooks may under-utilize, and the verb may degenerate to a discrete vocabulary (the failure LeVERB's expressive latent targets). → [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]]'s separate codebooks and [[2506.13751|LeVERB]]'s kinematic-reconstruction regularizer mitigate (H5); report codebook utilization and the loco-vs-manip split, not just aggregate SR.

---

## Cluster B — Mobile Manipulation (Nav↔Manip Coupling)

*Jointly controlling a moving base and an arm, where the base velocity is itself a manipulation degree of freedom — repositioning extends the reachable workspace mid-task, not before it. The cluster reads the moving base as one coupling that surfaces in three places: the order base and arm actions are factored, what the moving camera keeps in view, and what scene state persists once the base turns away.*

### B1 — Active-Perception Coupling in Mobile Manipulation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | B — Mobile Manipulation (Nav↔Manip Coupling) |
| **Thesis** | Treat *where to look* as an action the policy takes — actively steering gaze to keep the manipulation target and contact in view while the base moves — rather than reading a fixed forward camera. The first principle: a moving base changes the camera's viewpoint continuously, so observability is a controllable function of the base and head action, not a given; an unobserved state is unrecoverable by any policy capacity. The field assumes mobile-manipulation perception is a passive fixed-camera stream that the action policy consumes. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2603.03243\|HoMMI]] (method), [[2411.04999\|DynaMem]] (method), [[2604.08534\|ActiveGlasses]] (method), [[2405.07991\|SPIN-Mobile-Manip]] (method), [[2605.07943\|TAVIS]] (benchmark), [[2306.11565\|HomeRobot]] (benchmark), [[2407.07788\|BiGym]] (benchmark), [[2606.18772\|HALOMI]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | [[2603.03243\|HoMMI]] 90% laundry / 85% delivery / 80% tablescaping, active head control via a predicted "3D look-at point" critical for active search, precise placement, and observability; [[2411.04999\|DynaMem]] 70% dynamic pick-drop vs 30% static (2×), locate-failure 6.7% vs 53.3%, 74.5% DynaBench hybrid-query; [[2405.07991\|SPIN-Mobile-Manip]] single-RL-policy learned gaze coupled to a moving base, 90–96% hard-nav sim vs <26% fixed-camera and 0.8/0.6 static/dynamic real — the learned-mobile-base foil H1/H2 must beat; [[2306.11565\|HomeRobot]] per-task SR 5–15% (GT perception) → 0.4–0.6% (real DETIC detector), 20% real overall — the perception-bottleneck floor; [[2606.18772\|HALOMI]] 90% Bag-Transfer / 85% / 80%, active perception disable-ablation 90%→30% on a moving-body humanoid coupled through a manifold-constrained whole-body controller; [[2505.06182\|APPLE-Active-Perception]] RL-learned gaze objective (joint policy-gradient + differentiable prediction loss), 89% TactileMNIST, transfers across envs without re-tuning — the learned-objective foil to the imitation-learned gaze rows |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: most mobile-manipulation stacks read a fixed forward camera, but a moving base continuously changes what is visible, so the target, the contact, and the next subgoal can leave the frame exactly when they matter — and an unobserved state cannot be acted on, no matter how good the policy.
- **Today's answers**: "active gaze beats fixed camera" is now consensus, contested on two fronts. *Planners* couple gaze to base motion at runtime: [[2605.02487|Visibility-Aware-Mobile-Grasping]] jointly optimizes whole-body trajectory *and* gaze on a real moving-base Fetch (68.8% / 58.0%, +18.0% over decoupled nav-then-manip, collision 6.3% vs 17.5%); [[2310.00433|ActPerMoMa]] jointly plans base + gaze (92.6% sim, 80% real TIAGo++). *Learned* active-gaze policies abound but mostly fixed-base — [[2602.04600|Act-Sense-Act]] learns active perception on a wheeled humanoid (83.3% SR vs OpenVLA-OFT 12.7%), while [[2510.01607|ActiveUMI]] (70%, +44% over fixed head), [[2506.15666|Vision-in-Action]] (+45%), [[2602.22461|EgoAVFlow]] (up to 4× over human-viewpoint imitation), [[2511.00153|EgoMI]] and [[2410.18964|DISaM]] all learn where-to-look but on a static base. [[2603.03243|HoMMI]]'s "3D look-at point" head and [[2411.04999|DynaMem]]'s exploration-driven re-observation (70% dynamic vs 30% static) close the loop on a moving base, and [[2405.07991|SPIN-Mobile-Manip]] goes furthest — a single RL policy that learns gaze *while* moving the whole body (90–96% hard-nav sim vs <26% fixed-camera) — but its gaze is an emergent by-product of a monolithic policy, never a *separable* look-at *conditioned on the base trajectory the autoregressive base→torso→arm policy ([[2503.05652|BRS]]) chooses* and never decomposed gaze-vs-exploration.
- **The opening**: the surviving lever is the *learned look-at coupled to the chosen base trajectory* plus the attribution none of these run — gaze-vs-exploration on in-view-vs-out-of-view relocations (H3) and the during-vs-between manipulation-stability decomposition (H5). [[2306.11565|HomeRobot]]'s 5–15%→0.4–0.6% collapse (GT → real DETIC) is the headroom an active-gaze policy reaches that a passive camera cannot.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A mobile base changes the camera's viewpoint continuously, so what is observable is a function of the base + head action — a controllable quantity, not a given. To keep the target and contact in view the policy must *act* (gaze, head, base orientation), and an unobserved state is unrecoverable from the current frame by any policy capacity. [[2603.03243|HoMMI]]'s "3D look-at point" makes this operational — and it represents gaze as a *relaxed* head action precisely because directly copying human 6-DoF head poses yields kinematically infeasible robot motion, so observability must be an explicitly-controlled DoF.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "active gaze beats passive" (now consensus, instantiated fixed-base by [[2510.01607|ActiveUMI]] / [[2506.15666|Vision-in-Action]] / [[2602.22461|EgoAVFlow]] and as a moving-base planner by [[2605.02487|Visibility-Aware-Mobile-Grasping]]) but the un-run conjunction: a *learned* look-at policy *conditioned on the base trajectory the action policy chooses*, with the perception-coupling slice isolated from the action-coupling slice. The planners ([[2605.02487|Visibility-Aware-Mobile-Grasping]], [[2310.00433|ActPerMoMa]]) couple gaze to base but as receding-horizon search with a see-for-safety objective, not a learned demo policy, and never decompose gaze-vs-exploration or during-vs-between stability.
- **The bet**: A *learned* active-gaze policy that predicts a look-at point *conditioned on the base trajectory [[2503.05652|BRS]]'s autoregressive base→torso→arm policy chooses* recovers the perception slice of [[2306.11565|HomeRobot]]'s 5–15%→0.4–0.6% collapse with the gain largest exactly when the base is moving (near-zero stationary), matches [[2605.02487|Visibility-Aware-Mobile-Grasping]]'s +18.0%-over-decoupled while beating its planner on demo tasks, and recovers dynamic-object relocations to [[2411.04999|DynaMem]]'s 70% (vs 30% static, locate-failure 53.3%→6.7%) *for in-view relocations where gaze beats re-driving* (H3). Falsifiable: if a fixed forward camera matches learned gaze on moving-base tasks, or independently-predicted gaze matches base-coupled gaze, observability is not coupled to the base action and the planner suffices.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *where and whether gaze is actively directed* (predicted look-at head / object-centric head prediction / exploration-driven dynamic re-observation / VLM active observer / passive fixed camera), plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | Gaze / observability mechanism | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2603.03243\|HoMMI]] | predicted "3D look-at point" actively controlling the head (relaxed head action) | 90% / 85% / 80%; active head "critical" for search + placement + observability | the look-at is predicted from human demos but not *coupled to the base motion* [[2503.05652\|BRS]]'s autoregressive policy chooses — gaze and base are not jointly optimized |
| [[2605.02487\|Visibility-Aware-Mobile-Grasping]] | receding-horizon joint optimization of whole-body trajectory *and* gaze on a moving base | 68.8% / 58.0% real Fetch, +18.0% over decoupled nav-then-manip, collision 6.3% vs 17.5% | a *planner* with a see-for-safety objective, not a learned look-at policy from demos; no gaze-vs-exploration or during-vs-between decomposition (the topThreat that narrows B1's core) |
| [[2310.00433\|ActPerMoMa]] | jointly plans base trajectory + gaze for grasp executability | 92.6% sim / 80% real TIAGo++, lowest abort rate; 61.8% on hard-grasp-only scenes | also a planner — couples gaze to base but hand-engineered receding-horizon, not a learned policy conditioned on the action policy's base motion |
| [[2405.07991\|SPIN-Mobile-Manip]] | a *single RL policy* on a mobile manipulator that jointly emits base+arm control **and predicts where the ego-camera should look each step** while moving the whole body, from ego-vision with no map (RL-with-scandots → ego-depth student distill) | **90–96%** hard-nav sim vs **<26%** fixed-camera; **0.8** static / **0.6** dynamic real vs classical mapping **0.6** / **0.0–0.2** | the missing *learned-RL mobile-base* row — gaze is learned *and* coupled to the base trajectory the same policy chooses (the exact H1/H2 lever every other row lacks: fixed-base-learned or moving-base-*planner*), so it is the strongest existing foil the bet must beat; but its gaze is an emergent by-product of a monolithic RL policy, never *decomposed* gaze-vs-exploration (H3) or during-vs-between stability (H5), and it learns by RL-from-scratch rather than a look-at policy from demos conditioned on a *separable* base action from [[2503.05652\|BRS]]'s autoregressive policy |
| [[2602.04600\|Act-Sense-Act]] | learned active perception coupled to action on a wheeled humanoid (cognition + dual-track memory) | 83.3% SR vs OpenVLA-OFT 12.7%; removing cognition → 22.7%, removing memory → 40.7% | *learned* and on a mobile humanoid, but gaze is coupled to a high-level cognition module, not to the low-level base *trajectory* — the coupling target is the planner, not [[2503.05652\|BRS]]'s base action |
| [[2510.01607\|ActiveUMI]] | learned active head policy from portable bimanual capture | 70% over 6 tasks, +44% over fixed head / +63% over wrist-only; 56% in novel envs | fixed-base — proves learned gaze beats fixed camera but never couples to a moving base (the learned-fixed-base band B1 must extend to mobile) |
| [[2604.08534\|ActiveGlasses]] | object-centric point-cloud policy jointly predicting manipulation + head movement | zero-shot transfer with active vision, generalizes across 2 robot platforms, beats fixed-camera baselines under occlusion | fixed-base manipulation only — no moving base to couple gaze to, so the nav↔gaze coupling is untested |
| [[2411.04999\|DynaMem]] | dynamic 3D voxel memory + value-based exploration to re-observe moved objects | 70% dynamic vs 30% static, locate-failure 53.3%→6.7%, 74.5% DynaBench | re-observation is *exploration*-driven (where to drive), not *gaze*-driven (where to look) — the head is not an action (feeds B2) |
| [[2605.21133\|Spatial-Brain-Cerebellum]] | VLM "Active Spatial Brain" actively perceiving + decomposing the scene | 98.84% spatial understanding, 94.11% active localization, 60.0% vs 0% OOD-hard | active perception is a VLM planner over a reachable-space solver, real-robot-data-free — no learned gaze policy coupled to control |
| [[2503.05652\|BRS]] | multimodal observation attention over a colored point cloud (passive whole-body view) | 88% sub-task / 58% entire-task, near-zero safety | attends over the observation but the camera is passive — no head/gaze action to maintain observability under base motion |
| [[2511.18112\|EchoVLA]] | coarse/fine cross-attention over scene + episodic memory (passive sensing) | 0.44 real vs π0.5 0.33; ablation-critical scene + episodic memory | retrieves from memory but does not *act* to perceive — the memory channel active gaze should write into (feeds B2) |
| [[2306.11565\|HomeRobot]] | fixed-camera open-vocab detection (DETIC), no active gaze | per-task 5–15% (GT) → 0.4–0.6% (real detector), 20% real overall | the passive-perception failure floor — quantifies the cost active gaze must recover |
| [[2504.16054\|π0.5]] | fixed forward camera, heterogeneous co-trained VLA | 10–15 min multi-stage tasks in unseen homes | the strong passive-perception baseline active gaze must beat — observability is a given, not an action |
| [[2606.18772\|HALOMI]] | learned gaze shifts + active-neck head movements from human demos, coupled to a moving humanoid body through a manifold-constrained whole-body controller (BFM-Zero latent) | 90% Bag-Transfer / 85% Pick-Bread / 80% Towel-to-Basket; disabling active perception collapses Bag-Transfer 90%→30% even where a static view seems sufficient | the gaze is coupled to the *whole-body controller*, not to a base *trajectory* chosen the way [[2503.05652\|BRS]]'s autoregressive policy chooses one — it learns where to look while the body moves but never conditions the look-at on the navigation action that policy selects, and the coupling is demo-imitated, never decomposed gaze-vs-exploration or during-vs-between (the moving-body case Act-Sense-Act leaves to a cognition module and ActiveUMI leaves fixed-base) |
| [[2603.12193\|SaPaVe]] | learned *continuous* camera-control policy with a Camera-Adapter (LoRA) and *separate* action decoders for camera vs manipulation, built to resolve the unified-action-space camera-vs-manipulation interference | 85.00% real active manipulation vs π0 45.00% / GR00T-N1 53.75%; 85–90% on unseen objects in occluded pick-and-place | fixed-base manipulation only — it solves the action-decoupling that H2/H5 raise (the conflict ActiveUMI's single learned head ignores) but with no moving base to couple the camera action to, so the nav↔gaze coupling B1 turns on is untested |
| [[2508.05186\|TVVE]] | dynamic virtual-viewpoint *selection* (re-rendered camera poses maximizing target visibility) + a Task-aware MoE that decouples the visual encoder to remove active-view co-training interference | 86.6% RLBench (18 tasks) / 67.0% RLBench-OG under occlusion + camera-pose shift; 88.0% / 78.0% real on two arms | the viewpoint is *selected over re-rendered virtual views*, not a physical head/base action, and the platform is fixed-base — it adds the co-training-interference fix (TaskMoE) that no current row carries, but cannot test gaze as a control DoF coupled to base motion (the gap ActiveGlasses' object-centric head also leaves) |
| [[2601.08325\|ActiveVLA]] | coarse-to-fine active viewpoint *selection* by generating and evaluating candidate cameras on visibility/distance/diversity, plus active 3D zoom-in re-rendering crucial regions | 91.8% RLBench / 65.9% COLOSSEUM SOTA under visual generalization; 51.3% GemBench long-horizon; real occlusion-heavy tasks on KINOVA GEN2 | a candidate-generate-and-evaluate viewpoint family (distinct from HoMMI's look-at regression and ActiveGlasses' object-centric head), but fixed-base — viewpoint candidates are evaluated geometrically, not predicted as a head action conditioned on a moving base, so it never couples gaze to the navigation action |
| [[2505.06182\|APPLE-Active-Perception]] | *RL-learned* where-to-look policy — active perception as a POMDP optimizing a joint policy-gradient + differentiable supervised-prediction loss (learned objective, not imitation) | 89% TactileMNIST classification, 1.9cm/13° Toolbox pose; transfers to new envs without hyperparameter re-tuning, 53% faster training (CrossQ) | the learned-*objective* comparator the table lacks — every other row (HoMMI, ActiveUMI, ActiveGlasses, HALOMI) learns gaze by *imitation*, APPLE learns it by RL; but it is demonstrated on *tactile* sensing with no moving base and no visual head/look-at action, so it sets the training-objective foil, not a mobile-manipulation gaze policy |
| [[2506.10968\|EyeRobot]] | *RL-learned* visual gaze — a gimbal-mounted mechanical eyeball trained by a BC-RL loop (the eye is rewarded when the BC hand policy predicts accurate actions), over a Foveal Robot Transformer + real-to-sim EyeGym | **87%** towel-search SR over a **210°** workspace, 1.8 s avg search; emergent gaze-switching + dynamic-target tracking; beats static + wrist cameras under large workspace / perturbation | the *visual* instance of the learned-RL-objective gaze cell APPLE only fills on tactile — gaze emerges purely from task reward (no imitation), closing the gap the APPLE row flags; but fixed-base, so it sets the learned-RL-visual-gaze training-objective foil, not yet a base-coupled mobile gaze policy (the moving-base coupling H1/H2 turn on remains open) |
| [[2505.12278\|Emergent-Active-Perception-Dexterity]] | *emergent head movement* (active visual search) learned purely from egocentric visual RL on a whole-body simulated humanoid while the body reaches/stabilizes | active visual search for unseen objects by head motion + whole-body reach/stability coordination on grasp/drawer-open; stereo > RGB/RGBD; generalizes to layouts + novel sub-task combos | sits exactly where [[2405.07991\|SPIN-Mobile-Manip]] sits (gaze emergent from a monolithic policy while the whole body moves) but on a *legged/whole-body humanoid* not a wheeled mobile manipulator — the whole-body-humanoid foil for H1/H2; gaze is emergent end-to-end RL, never a separable look-at conditioned on a chosen base trajectory, no gaze-vs-exploration or during-vs-between decomposition |
| [[2507.15833\|Look,-Focus,-Act]] | end-to-end *learned* gaze prediction (Fov-Act) + foveated ViT tokenization on AV-ALOHA (fixed-base), carrying the efficiency/footprint angle | ~**7×** training / **3×** inference speedup, **5×** GPU-memory cut, **94%** token reduction; **92%** ThreadNeedle / PourTestTube; improved robustness under distractors | supplies the H6-style footprint datapoint (does active gaze pay for its compute?) and a learned-end-to-end gaze comparator distinct from [[2510.01607\|ActiveUMI]]'s head-control-SR-only — but fixed-base tabletop, so it never couples gaze to a moving base |
| [[2503.01439\|AVR]] | active camera control via a Diffusion Policy predicting gimbal angles AND optical *zoom* (focal length), fixed-base bimanual | **+25%** real precision SR over static views, 5–**17%** sim; ablation confirms both viewpoint and zoom contribute, zoom marked for precision tasks | adds the focal-length / zoom DoF as an observability lever no cited row carries (look-at direction only), extending "gaze as a controllable DoF" to dynamic zoom for fine-grained perception/occlusion — but fixed-base, so the nav-gaze coupling B1 turns on stays untested |
| [[2511.15279\|EyeVLA]] | a real motorized pan-tilt-*zoom* camera (the "Robotic Eyeball") driven end-to-end by a VLA: pan/tilt/zoom jointly hierarchically-tokenized into one autoregressive action, GRPO-refined on real demonstrations | **96%** task completion / 50 real scenes, 2.04° pan / 1.68° tilt error; direct VLM prompting only **12%**, hard-coded geometric mapping **70%** | the sharpest external confirmation that direction-plus-zoom is already a solved *joint, tokenized* action space (found via first-principles archaeology, not previously in this corpus) — but the PTZ mount is a fixed physical pedestal, not embodied on a moving mobile-manipulator head, so it still never conditions the look-at-plus-zoom action on a *base trajectory* the way B1's bet requires |
| [[2503.12609\|VISO-Grasp]] | VLM-informed target-guided velocity-field Next-Best-View *planner* + uncertainty multi-view grasp fusion, handling occluded/invisible targets (fixed-base) | **87.5%** AFSR vs **12.5%** reconstruction-NBV / **52.5–60%** static; 3.10 avg grasp attempts; fusion lifts AFSR 70.0%→87.5% | complements the ActiveVLA/TVVE candidate-viewpoint-selection family with a *target-driven* (not reconstruction-driven) NBV under heavy clutter — a planner-foil the bet must beat on observability-for-grasping, but fixed-base, so it never preempts the learned base-coupled conjunction |
| [[2608.02257\|PanoVLA]] | *passive omnidirectional* sensing: a dedicated panorama expert (MTPano-encoded 360° equirectangular view) fused via the transformer KV-cache into a mobile-manipulation MoT policy, no head/gaze action at all | **91.3%** stage completion / **73.4%** end-to-end SR across 4 real mobile-bimanual tasks vs local-view π0.5 **58.6%**/**30.0%**; MTPano lifts a hard task by **>40 pp** over a general-purpose (SigLIP) panoramic encoder | the table's only *zero-gaze-action* row: instead of choosing where to look, it observes everywhere at once on a wheeled mobile base, sidestepping H1's active-vs-passive framing entirely. A genuine falsifier candidate for the "observability must be action-controlled" first principle, but never run head-to-head against a learned base-coupled gaze policy on the same tasks, and panoramic sensing is itself a hardware/bandwidth cost the bet's framing doesn't price |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (active gaze coupled to base motion beats a fixed camera), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — A learned base-coupled gaze policy beats the gaze-as-planner baseline, and the gap opens with base motion.**
   - *Prediction*: a learned look-at policy conditioned on the base trajectory matches or beats [[2605.02487|Visibility-Aware-Mobile-Grasping]]'s planner (+18.0% over decoupled) on its real moving-base tasks, with the learned-over-planner gap largest during base-motion phases (where the target leaves a fixed frame) and near-zero on stationary phases; ablating to a fixed forward camera collapses both.
   - *Test*: learned base-coupled gaze vs the receding-horizon planner vs frozen head; stratify SR and target-in-frame rate by base-motion phase, using [[2605.07943|TAVIS]]'s paired headcam-vs-fixedcam protocol on identical demos as the canonical fixed-vs-active control (its fixed-base version of this comparison: GR1T2 headcam 47.1% vs fixedcam 38.8%, up to +45 pp on conditional-pick).
   - *Row*: Visibility-Aware Mobile Grasping (planner, gaze+base) / HoMMI (learned look-at) / TAVIS (headcam-vs-fixedcam control) vs HomeRobot (fixed camera).
   - *Falsifier*: a fixed camera matches active gaze on moving-base tasks, or the planner matches the learned policy → observability is not action-controlled and the planner suffices.
2. **H2 — Coupling gaze to the base action beats independent head control.**
   - *Prediction*: jointly predicting look-at *conditioned on* the base motion [[2503.05652|BRS]]'s autoregressive base→torso→arm policy chooses keeps the target in frame more reliably while repositioning than an independently-predicted head, raising SR on reach-extension tasks where base and gaze must agree.
   - *Test*: gaze-conditioned-on-base vs independent-head on the autoregressive policy; report target-in-frame rate and SR during repositioning.
   - *Row*: HoMMI (predicted look-at) — coupled to BRS's autoregressive base.
   - *Falsifier*: independent head matches base-coupled gaze → the nav↔gaze coupling is inert and gaze can be optimized separately.
3. **H3 — Gaze-driven re-observation beats exploration-driven re-observation for moving targets.**
   - *Prediction*: adding active gaze (where to *look*) on top of [[2411.04999|DynaMem]]'s exploration (where to *drive*) recovers the 53.3%→6.7% locate-failure faster on objects that move *within* the current viewpoint, where re-driving is wasteful.
   - *Test*: DynaMem exploration alone vs exploration + active gaze on in-view vs out-of-view object relocations; report locate-failure and re-acquisition time.
   - *Row*: DynaMem (exploration-driven).
   - *Falsifier*: gaze adds nothing over exploration → head motion is redundant given base re-positioning.
4. **H4 — Object-centric gaze prediction transfers from human demos to the moving-base setting.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2604.08534|ActiveGlasses]]'s object-centric joint manipulation+head prediction, retrained with a moving base, retains its occlusion-handling zero-shot-transfer advantage — the object-centric representation is what makes the gaze policy embodiment-portable.
   - *Test*: object-centric vs ego-frame gaze head, fixed-base vs moving-base; report occlusion-task SR and cross-platform transfer on [[2602.01939|EFM-10]]'s 10 occlusion/exploration tasks (active vision Cup-Hang 26.7%→90.0%), the named occlusion-active-perception benchmark for the object-centric-gaze-under-occlusion test.
   - *Row*: ActiveGlasses (object-centric head prediction) / EFM-10 (occlusion benchmark).
   - *Falsifier*: object-centric gaze loses its advantage with a moving base → the precedent does not extend to mobile manipulation.
5. **H5 — Active gaze destabilizes manipulation unless coupling-compensated.**
   - *Prediction*: moving the head/base to look raises end-effector tracking error *during* active-look maneuvers vs between them, and stacking A1's coupling-aware predictor under the controller closes that during-vs-between gap.
   - *Test*: report manipulation precision during vs between active-look maneuvers, with and without the A1 coupling predictor.
   - *Row*: HoMMI (predicted look-at) — coupled to A1 (HEX, coupled dynamics).
   - *Falsifier*: no during-vs-between gap → active gaze is not a balance disturbance and coupling compensation is unnecessary.
6. **H6 — The perception coupling is separable from the action coupling, and decomposing them localizes the gain.**
   - *Prediction*: running [[2503.05652|BRS]]'s autoregressive base→torso→arm policy with {ground-truth, active-gaze, passive} perception shows active gaze recovers a measurable, *additive* slice of the [[2306.11565|HomeRobot]] 5–15%→0.4–0.6% perception gap, distinct from that policy's action-coupling slice.
   - *Test*: 2×3 grid (autoregressive/flat factoring × GT/active/passive perception); attribute SR to action-coupling vs perception-coupling, holding perception as a controlled variable via [[2605.07943|TAVIS]]'s headcam-vs-fixedcam (active-vs-passive) axis paired with [[2306.11565|HomeRobot]]'s GT-vs-real-detector axis so the full GT/active/passive triad is benchmark-backed.
   - *Row*: HomeRobot (GT-vs-real) / TAVIS (active-vs-passive) vs HoMMI (active) — coupled to BRS's autoregressive base.
   - *Falsifier*: active vs passive perception makes no SR difference at fixed action coupling → the perception coupling is not a separate lever.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Active gaze can destabilize manipulation** — moving the head/base to look couples back into balance (A1) and shifts the manipulation frame. → H5 reports manipulation precision during vs between active-look maneuvers; couple to A1's coupling-aware control so the gaze maneuver is balance-compensated, not a disturbance the arm fights.
> - **Predicting where to look needs supervision** — the look-at target is ambiguous, and copying human head poses is kinematically infeasible. → [[2603.03243|HoMMI]] learns a *relaxed* 3D look-at point from demos (sidestepping the 6-DoF infeasibility); report look-at prediction accuracy vs downstream SR and where predicted gaze diverges from the optimal.
> - **Active perception adds a control loop** — gaze + base + arm is more to coordinate, and the loop may not pay for itself on every task. → Bound the gaze-control frequency to the perception need; H6's perception-vs-action decomposition tells you which task classes the added loop earns its cost on.
> - **The gain may be a re-observation artifact, not gaze** — DynaMem already recovers moved objects by re-driving. → H3 separates gaze-driven from exploration-driven re-observation on in-view vs out-of-view relocations; if gaze only helps out-of-view (where re-driving also works), its marginal value over B2's memory + exploration is small.
> - **Passive omnidirectional sensing may make active gaze unnecessary (the regime-boundary contradiction the bet has not run)** — the whole first principle assumes observability must be *action*-controlled (gaze, head, base), but [[2608.02257|PanoVLA]] gets a comparable gap-closing gain (91.3% SCR / 73.4% SR vs local-view π0.5's 58.6% / 30.0%) from a *passive* 360° panorama expert with no head/gaze action at all, on a wheeled mobile-bimanual platform. → Do **not** assume active gaze is the only route to observability: run panoramic (passive, see-everything) sensing head-to-head against a learned base-coupled gaze policy on the same coupled tasks before asserting H1's "observability is action-controlled" premise; if panoramic sensing matches active gaze on a legged humanoid too, price the trade as compute/bandwidth cost vs the gaze policy's control-loop cost, not as a settled first principle.

### B2 — Large-Workspace Memory for Mobile Manipulation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | B — Mobile Manipulation (Nav↔Manip Coupling) |
| **Thesis** | Equip a mobile manipulator with persistent spatio-semantic memory of objects and scene state it can no longer see, rather than a Markovian policy that re-perceives from scratch each step. The first principle: the task-relevant workspace exceeds the instantaneous field of view, so the current frame is not a sufficient statistic — relevant state is partly *outside* the observation and must be carried in memory. The field assumes the current camera frame (or a short recent window) is a sufficient statistic for the next action. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2511.18112\|EchoVLA]] (method), [[2411.04999\|DynaMem]] (method), [[2605.21133\|Spatial-Brain-Cerebellum]] (method), [[2603.04639\|RoboMME]] (benchmark), [[2407.07788\|BiGym]] (benchmark), [[2306.11565\|HomeRobot]] (benchmark), [[2603.01229\|RMBench]] (benchmark), [[2511.11478\|LIBERO-Mem]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2511.18112\|EchoVLA]] 0.44 real (TidyBot++, 6 tasks) vs π0.5 0.33 / WB-VIMA 0.11, 0.31 sim vs π0.5 0.20, 0.10 on a long-horizon task where baselines largely fail, scene + episodic memory both ablation-critical; [[2411.04999\|DynaMem]] relocated-object locate-failure 6.7% vs 53.3% memoryless, 70% dynamic vs 30% static, 74.5% DynaBench; [[2407.07788\|BiGym]] long-horizon multi-object (`stack_blocks`, long sequences) near-0% for memoryless IL + demo-RL — the long-horizon floor; [[2603.04639\|RoboMME]] 16 history-dependent tasks under a temporal/spatial/object/procedural memory taxonomy with 14 memory-augmented π0.5 variants, memory-required floor 44.51% best (perceptual FrameSamp+Modul) / 84.08% symbolic-Oracle vs 90.5% human — the standardized memory-taxonomy + comparator-pool substrate; [[2606.17463\|WeaveLA]] RoboMME 19.0%→24.7% (6-task) / 17.3%→23.3% (16-task), SWINGXTIMES 0%→47.8% |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: a mobile manipulator's relevant workspace is far larger than its field of view — it walks between rooms, sets an object down, turns away, and must return — so a Markovian policy re-perceiving from the current frame has no record of where it left the basket or that the object has since moved.
- **Today's answers**: the literature has cleaved into three camps, none combining all of B2's elements. *Scene-memory-for-mobile* is now its own consensus: [[2510.03885|3D-Latent-Mapping]] keeps a persistent 3D-latent map as policy state "for mobile manipulation," extending perception beyond the FOV and beating image-based baselines (0.31 vs 0.29 Map-BC, 1.00 vs 0.75 sequential-RL OOD), and [[2509.20297|mindmap]] reconstructs out-of-view objects for the policy (76% vs 20% memoryless). *Episodic-for-tabletop*: [[2511.18112|EchoVLA]]'s dual declarative memory (0.44 real, both types ablation-critical) and [[2603.18494|MemoAct]]'s long-short-term consolidation (96.5% MemoryRTBench, boosts DP3 26%→76.5%). *Modular-dynamic-maps*: [[2411.04999|DynaMem]] (70% relocated vs 30% static, 53.3%→6.7% locate-failure) and [[2410.11989|DovSG]] (93–95% scene-change detection, 35% long-term vs Ok-Robot's 5%). The scene-memory-beats-memoryless half is taken; no one combines *scene + episodic in one learned policy*, makes that in-policy memory *dynamic* (relocated-object-aware), and sweeps *multi-room* scale.
- **The opening**: [[2407.07788|BiGym]]'s near-0% memoryless floor is the existence proof that the gap is memory not capacity — but the surviving lever is the *multi-room scaling law* ([[2510.03885|3D-Latent-Mapping]] and [[2509.20297|mindmap]] are single-scene; [[2511.18112|EchoVLA]]'s scene map is *static*), which no external paper sweeps.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: The task-relevant state spans a workspace larger than the instantaneous field of view — at any moment, relevant objects and scene structure are partly outside the current observation. So the current frame is *not* a sufficient statistic, and a Markovian policy is structurally unable to return to an out-of-view target. [[2511.18112|EchoVLA]] demonstrates the consequence: its 0.11→0.44 gap over memoryless WB-VIMA, and its 0.10 on a long-horizon task where baselines fail, both vanish under memory ablation.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "memory beats memoryless" (now taken — [[2510.03885|3D-Latent-Mapping]] and [[2509.20297|mindmap]] already beat memoryless scene-side, [[2603.18494|MemoAct]] episodic-side) but the un-built combination: that a *static* scene-memory policy ([[2510.03885|3D-Latent-Mapping]], [[2511.18112|EchoVLA]]) or a *modular* dynamic map ([[2411.04999|DynaMem]], [[2410.11989|DovSG]]) is enough — when the object moves *between* visits, the static map confidently returns the stale location and the modular map sits outside the policy with no episodic task history. The Markovian [[2504.16054|π0.5]]/WB-VIMA baselines are the floor all three camps clear.
- **The bet**: An *in-policy* memory fusing persistent scene + episodic task-history *and* dynamic relocated-object purging beats *both* static-scene-memory policies ([[2510.03885|3D-Latent-Mapping]] 0.31, [[2509.20297|mindmap]] 76%) *and* modular dynamic maps ([[2411.04999|DynaMem]] 70% relocated, [[2410.11989|DovSG]] 35% long-term) on *multi-room relocated-object* tasks, with the margin scaling monotonically with the number of out-of-view returns (near-zero single-room, maximal multi-room — the H3 lever no external paper sweeps) and dynamic purging specifically required where the object moves between visits. Falsifiable: if a static-scene-memory policy holds on relocated objects, or a memoryless long-window policy matches in-policy memory on multi-room tasks, the dynamic/in-policy/multi-room combination is not the lever.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *what state persists out of view* (persistent voxel scene map / time-indexed episodic experience / dynamically-purged changing world / VLM-maintained spatial state / Markovian none), plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | Out-of-view state retained | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2511.18112\|EchoVLA]] | persistent voxel scene map + time-indexed episodic token buffer (dual declarative) | 0.44 real vs WB-VIMA 0.11 / π0.5 0.33; 0.10 long-horizon where baselines fail; both memories ablation-critical | the scene map is *static* — it remembers where things *were*, not that they moved (DynaMem's gap, inverted) |
| [[2510.03885\|3D-Latent-Mapping]] | persistent 3D-latent scene map as policy state "for mobile manipulation" (SBP) | 0.31 vs 0.29 Image-BC (OOD); 1.00 vs 0.75 sequential-RL OOD; zero-shot to real xArm6 | the strongest scene-memory-for-mobile precedent (clears B2's H4), but *single-scene* and *static* (no relocated-object purge) and *scene-map-only* (no episodic channel) — the topThreat that takes the scene half |
| [[2509.20297\|mindmap]] | reconstructed out-of-view scene fed to a diffusion policy | 76% over 4 spatial-memory tasks vs 20% 3D-Diffuser-Actor; 97% Mug-in-Drawer, attends to out-of-view objects | scene reconstruction beats memoryless (clears H4) but *static* and single-scene — no dynamic purge, no episodic memory, no multi-room sweep |
| [[2603.18494\|MemoAct]] | long-short-term episodic memory consolidation (plug-and-play) | 96.5% MemoryRTBench vs MVMP 72%; boosts DP3 26%→76.5%; 87.5% real | the *episodic* half — pure task-history memory with no allocentric scene map, so it can't re-acquire an out-of-view *spatial* target |
| [[2410.11989\|DovSG]] | modular dynamic 3D scene graph that detects + updates scene change | 93–95% scene-change detection, 88% scene-graph accuracy, 35% long-term vs Ok-Robot 5%, 13× less memory | the *modular* dynamic-map half (like DynaMem) — outside the policy, no episodic task history, the map and the policy never co-train |
| [[2606.12956\|SERF]] | 4D spatiotemporal scene+robot-body feature map (neural points, in-policy via a map tokenizer) with *within-episode* SE(3) transform tracking — latents/decoder learned *before* execution and held fixed (map *not* co-trained with the policy) | 58.7% BEHAVIOR-1K vs 44.0% image-only / 54.0% static-map; +23% under relocated goals (28.0%→51.0% on unvisited regions); 95% object-drop re-grasp vs 65%; *3 single-large-room* tasks (4.4×3.0 / 5.5×9.0 / 7.6×8.8 m), no multi-room | its numbers land only H2's *within-paper dynamic-vs-static* control: there is *no episodic / task-history channel* (spatial reconstruction only), relocated-object handling is *within-episode SE(3) transform tracking* ($\hat R\,p+\hat t$ via keypoint correspondence), **not** ray-cast purge or *cross-visit re-ID*; the map is **not co-trained** with the policy; and it evaluates *single-room only* with *no scaling law* — leaving the scene+episodic-fused, dynamically-purged, multi-room *in-policy-learned* combination the bet turns on unbuilt |
| [[2411.04999\|DynaMem]] | dynamic 3D voxel map, ray-cast-purges moved objects + value-based exploration | 70% dynamic vs 30% static, locate-failure 53.3%→6.7%, 74.5% DynaBench | a modular map outside the policy, no *episodic* task experience — remembers the world, not the task history (B1's exploration overlaps) |
| [[2605.21133\|Spatial-Brain-Cerebellum]] | VLM-maintained spatial state across base motion (active spatial brain) | 60.0% vs 0% OOD-hard, 98.84% spatial understanding, 94.11% active localization | spatial state is VLM-reasoned, real-robot-data-free — not a learned in-policy memory the controller reads (active perception feeds B1) |
| [[2503.05652\|BRS]] | none beyond the current whole-body observation (Markovian) | 88% sub-task / 58% entire-task across an *extensive* reachable workspace | the large-workspace substrate, but Markovian — succeeds within view, supplies no out-of-view memory |
| [[2512.24653\|RoboMIND-2.0]] | dual-system planner state (MIND-2-VLM high-level) over 20K mobile-manip trajectories | up to 1.0 multi-robot collaborative SR across 6 embodiments | planner-level state for hand-off, not a spatial memory of where objects are — the multi-room data substrate memory trains on |
| [[2306.11565\|HomeRobot]] | modular semantic map of unseen objects (open-world) | 20% real overall; 5–15%→0.4–0.6% with real perception | open-world map limited by perception quality — bad detection writes bad memory, stressing the write-quality axis |
| [[2407.07788\|BiGym]] | benchmark, no memory mechanism (50 human demos/task) | IL + demo-RL near-0% on long-horizon multi-object | measures the memoryless floor on 40 tasks but supplies no memory — the long-horizon difficulty gradient B2 is scored on |
| [[2504.16054\|π0.5]] | short recent-window VLA, no persistent spatial memory | 10–15 min multi-stage tasks in unseen homes | the memoryless cross-embodiment baseline EchoVLA beats — long horizon within a window, no out-of-view recall |
| [[2510.20328\|MemER]] | retrieved task-relevant *visual experience* — a VLM nominates keyframes, 1D single-linkage clustering compacts them into a long-horizon episodic memory on top of π0.5 (B2's own baseline) | 59/60 Object Search retrievals, 1 wrong scoop in Counting Scoops, on-par with Human-HL across 3 long-horizon partially-observable tasks; ~1Hz HL / ~2Hz LL | an *episodic retrieval* memory (like MemoAct's, but built by VLM keyframe selection) with no allocentric scene map — it recalls *what was seen* minutes ago, not *where* an out-of-view spatial target now is; single-scene, no multi-room sweep |
| [[2508.19236\|MemoryVLA]] | in-policy dual *episodic* memory — Perceptual–Cognitive Memory Bank (perceptual/working + cognitive/episodic, gate-fused, redundancy-aware consolidation, memory-conditioned diffusion action expert), time-indexed experience with no allocentric scene map | **71.9%** SimplerEnv-Bridge (+14.6 pp over CogACT-Large); **+26** pp on real long-horizon temporal tasks (+43 on Seq. Push Buttons); consolidation adds only **+3.6%** latency / **+0.8 GB** | the canonical in-policy *episodic-only* SOTA — the pure-episodic counterpart to [[2511.18112\|EchoVLA]]'s scene+episodic dual and the cleanest H1 comparator (does in-policy episodic-only match dual scene+episodic on the long horizon?); overlaps [[2603.18494\|MemoAct]]'s lane but is the more-prominent named memory-VLA baseline and uniquely supplies the H6 footprint datapoint (consolidation cost), yet carries no scene map and no relocated-object purge |
| [[2410.06237\|BUMBLE]] | VLM-maintained *dual-layered modular memory* (execution history + valuable-experience store) operating building-wide over a navigation/manipulation/infrastructure skill library — building-scale spatial+experience state | **47.1%** over 70 real trials across 3 buildings (vs COME 36.2% / Inner-Monologue 17.4%), up to **80.2%** with GPT-4o; sequences ≤12 skills / 15 min per trial; long-term memory ablates +10.9% | the building-scale multi-room precedent the lane lacked — every cited scene-memory (3D Latent Mapping, mindmap, EchoVLA's static map) is single-scene, so BUMBLE supplies the upper-extent comparator H3's multi-room scaling-law sweep needs; but its memory is *VLM-maintained modular* (the cited [[2605.21133\|Spatial-Brain-Cerebellum]] / [[2410.11989\|DovSG]] / [[2411.04999\|DynaMem]] lane), not the in-policy learned memory the bet proposes — the building-scale modular baseline, not the mechanism |
| [[2603.01229\|RMBench]] | benchmark — Task Memory Complexity (TMC) quantifies the minimum past information a task requires, binning M(1) vs M(n) across 9 memory-dependent dual-arm tasks (graded memory-*amount* axis), with a persistent-anchor-vs-sliding-memory ablation (Mem-0 policy) | Mem-0 **52.8%** M(1) (+38.4) / **28.5%** M(n) (+21.2); removing anchor memory collapses SR; Markovian floor ACT **0.0%** / Pi0.5 **5.83%** | the graded memory-amount axis [[2603.04639\|RoboMME]] (type taxonomy) and [[2407.07788\|BiGym]] (raw difficulty) do not supply — it substrates H3 (bin tasks by required out-of-view returns) and H4 (a long window cannot substitute); its anchor-memory ablation lands on H1's scene-vs-episodic contribution test, and its Markovian floor reinforces the BiGym/RoboMME memoryless-floor proof |
| [[2511.11478\|LIBERO-Mem]] | benchmark — 10 non-Markovian object-permanence tasks stress-testing object-instance tracking through *occlusion* with visually-ambiguous objects + sequenced interactions; method = Embodied-SlotSSM (slot-SSM memory), the out-of-view-via-occlusion case | Embodied-SlotSSM **14.8%** LIBERO-Mem subgoal vs OpenVLA/SlotVLA-h8 **5.0%** / SlotVLA-h1 **0%**; **83.0%** general LIBERO-Goal; tracks the same instance through occlusion | backs H2 (object-identity through occlusion — the out-of-view-via-occlusion case of the static-vs-dynamic test), H4 (cues hidden in object-specific histories a window cannot substitute), and H6 (VLA token-scaling becomes intractable past a few hundred frames — the footprint problem); its slot-SSM is a distinct in-policy memory mechanism not in the table, and its memory-required floor reinforces the memoryless-floor proof; but tabletop-occlusion, not multi-room travel |
| [[2510.07134\|TrackVLA++]] | an *in-policy* confidence-gated long-term identity memory (Target Identification Memory) built INTO a VLA — freezes target identity during occlusion and re-acquires on confident re-detection (dynamically-purged identity) | **74.0%** Distracted-Tracking SR (4-view) vs NavFoM **62.0%** with less data; **87.5%** SYNTH-PEDES re-ID; real Unitree GO2 **+7–17%** over TrackVLA | a distinct in-policy memory mechanism (confidence-gated freeze/update) not yet in the table, substantiating H2 (dynamic memory required when the target moves/occludes; a static representation fails) on a *mobile quadruped* — complementary to the *tabletop* slot-SSM occlusion of [[2511.11478\|LIBERO-Mem]]; but single-target visual *tracking*, not multi-object mobile manipulation, so it adds the dynamic-identity sub-claim, not the full bet |
| [[2403.08231\|OPF]] | a classical particle filter (not learned, not in-policy) embedding object-permanence rules: on occlusion, a dynamics module or Bhattacharyya-distance occluder module supplies a virtual measurement while covariance is *exponentially inflated* with occlusion duration/velocity, giving an explicit, growing uncertainty signal rather than a frozen or purged belief | translation error **0.06289→0.01138**, rotation **0.3870→0.06869** vs standard PF; **8/10** blindfolded real sugar-dropping trials; validated across AprilTag/MediaPipe/BundleSDF/mocap | found via first-principles archaeology (outside this vault, pre-dates typical ingestion) — the sharpest *formal Bayesian* occlusion-belief mechanism in the table, distinct from TrackVLA++'s confidence-gated freeze/re-acquire and LIBERO-Mem's slot-SSM: uncertainty is a continuous, principled quantity, not a discrete gate; but tabletop/fixed-arm HRI single/multi-object *tracking*, not multi-room allocentric scene memory — it sharpens H2's dynamic-vs-static test with a formal uncertainty model, without touching B2's actual multi-room in-policy bet |
| [[2607.18016\|POT-VLA]] | *in-policy* role-indexed 3D object memory (Persistent Object Tokens) fed directly into the action-head self-attention *and* re-read by a geometric-predicate supervisor, refreshed after every action chunk (ground–act–refresh–verify–recover loop) | **71/80** vs direct-baseline **39/80** across 8 humanoid loco-manip task families; ablation isolates the gain to *tokens* (31/40) with verification adding a further slice (34/40 full); mid-execution-perturbation SR **8/10** vs **4/10** baseline, novel-instance **9/10** vs **6/10** | the closest external instance of B2's *in-policy* half — object state is both actionable (drives the action head) and dynamically refreshed (re-grounds after relocation) — but persistence is *within one subtask/episode* on a humanoid office loop, not a multi-room scene+episodic fusion, and it carries no allocentric scene map or across-episode task history, so it substantiates H1's token-conditioning-vs-verification split and H2's dynamic-vs-static test without settling H3's multi-room scaling law |
| [[2605.22283\|SOMA]] | a *movable head camera* actively scanning to build a persistent 3D spatial-semantic memory (VGGT+YOLO+DINOv3), dynamically refined online and read via cross-attention — the first in-corpus row combining *active viewing* with *persistent memory-write*, the H5 compound no other cited row instantiates | Fixed-Head-Camera ablation consistently failed when targets or goals left the field of view; **52.0%** avg SR on RoboCasa Tabletop GR1 (**48.3%** from only 30 demos) | the head runs a *predefined scanning strategy*, not a learned look-at policy — doesn't touch B1's learned-gaze-conditioned-on-base-trajectory bet; dual-arm tabletop platform (RoboCasa GR1) — desk-scale out-of-frame occlusion, not multi-room out-of-view, so it does **not** satisfy H3's multi-room-scaling requirement |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (persistent out-of-view memory beats a Markovian policy, scaling with task length), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Both scene and episodic memory contribute, and episodic carries the long horizon.**
   - *Prediction*: ablating [[2511.18112|EchoVLA]]'s two memory types each drops the 0.11→0.44 gain, and removing *episodic* memory specifically collapses the 0.10 long-horizon result where the static scene map alone cannot track task progress.
   - *Test*: 2×2 ablation (scene on/off × episodic on/off); report SR overall and on the long-horizon task, with [[2603.04639|RoboMME]]'s temporal/spatial/object/procedural taxonomy + 14-variant π0.5 pool as the standardized cross-policy grading of whether scene-type vs episodic-type memory carries each demand (its within-policy version of H1's ablation).
   - *Row*: EchoVLA (dual declarative) / RoboMME (memory-taxonomy comparator pool).
   - *Falsifier*: scene-only matches dual memory on long-horizon → episodic memory is redundant and a static map suffices.
2. **H2 — Dynamic memory is required when objects move; a static map fails.**
   - *Prediction*: on objects relocated *between* visits, [[2411.04999|DynaMem]]'s ray-cast-purged dynamic map recovers the 53.3%→6.7% locate-failure while [[2511.18112|EchoVLA]]'s *static* scene map degrades (it remembers the stale location), and the gap is zero on stationary objects — matching [[2606.12956|SERF]]'s within-paper dynamic-vs-static control (58.7% vs 54.0% static-map, +23% under relocated goals).
   - *Test*: static scene map vs dynamic map on stationary vs relocated objects; report locate-failure per condition, using SERF's static-map baseline as the matched control.
   - *Row*: DynaMem (dynamic) / SERF (dynamic-vs-static control) vs EchoVLA (static scene map).
   - *Falsifier*: the static map matches the dynamic map on relocated objects → dynamic purging is unnecessary.
3. **H3 — The in-policy dynamic scene+episodic advantage scales with multi-room extent, where single-scene memories saturate.**
   - *Prediction*: sweeping task extent (single-room → multi-room), the in-policy dynamic scene+episodic margin over *single-scene* static memories ([[2510.03885|3D-Latent-Mapping]], [[2509.20297|mindmap]]) and *modular* dynamic maps ([[2410.11989|DovSG]], [[2411.04999|DynaMem]]) is near-zero single-room and grows monotonically with out-of-view returns — the multi-room scaling law no external paper sweeps, matching [[2407.07788|BiGym]]'s near-0% memoryless floor on the longest tasks.
   - *Test*: bin tasks by required out-of-view returns + rooms; plot the in-policy-memory margin vs single-scene and modular baselines against task extent.
   - *Row*: 3D Latent Mapping / mindmap (single-scene static) vs DovSG (modular dynamic) vs EchoVLA (in-policy declarative); BiGym (difficulty gradient).
   - *Falsifier*: the margin is flat across extent, or single-scene memory holds multi-room → multi-room is not the lever and the existing camps suffice.
4. **H4 — A long observation window cannot substitute for persistent memory.**
   - *Prediction*: a memoryless policy with a long stacked-frame window stays below declarative memory on multi-room tasks, because stacking recent frames cannot recover a target seen many rooms ago — the sufficient-statistic assumption fails even with a large window.
   - *Test*: sweep observation-window length on a memoryless policy vs EchoVLA on multi-room tasks and on [[2603.04639|RoboMME]]'s memory-required floor (built so policies cannot succeed from immediate observations), where a long window provably cannot substitute; report SR-vs-window-length.
   - *Row*: π0.5 (recent-window) vs EchoVLA (dual declarative) / RoboMME (memory-required floor).
   - *Falsifier*: a long-enough window matches declarative memory → the frame window *is* a sufficient statistic and explicit memory is avoidable.
5. **H5 — Memory and active perception compound; writing active observations dominates either alone.**
   - *Prediction*: feeding B1's active-gaze observations into B2's persistent memory beats both passive-perception-into-memory and active-gaze-without-memory on long-horizon mobile manipulation, because active gaze improves *write* quality and memory improves *recall*. [[2605.22283|SOMA]] is now the first in-corpus row that partially instantiates the compound (a movable head camera writing into a persistent scene memory), but its scan is a *predefined* strategy, not a learned look-at policy, so it shows *some* active viewing helps write quality without yet testing whether a *learned*, base-trajectory-conditioned gaze compounds further.
   - *Test*: 2×2 (active/passive perception × memory/memoryless) on long-horizon multi-room tasks; report SR per cell.
   - *Row*: EchoVLA (dual declarative) / SOMA (movable-camera scene memory, predefined scan) — coupled to B1 (HoMMI / DynaMem).
   - *Falsifier*: active-into-memory matches passive-into-memory → write quality does not gate memory value and B1/B2 are independent.
6. **H6 — Memory footprint can be pruned to task-relevant state without SR loss.**
   - *Prediction*: pruning the persistent map to task-relevant objects/regions holds SR while bounding the memory footprint as the workspace grows — multi-room state need not blow up linearly if irrelevant structure is forgotten.
   - *Test*: sweep memory-pruning aggressiveness; report the memory-footprint-vs-SR curve across single→multi-room and across [[2603.04639|RoboMME]]'s four memory types (temporal/spatial/object/procedural), so pruning aggressiveness is measured against which memory demand is load-bearing.
   - *Row*: EchoVLA (dual declarative) / DynaMem (dynamic) / RoboMME (four-type taxonomy).
   - *Falsifier*: any pruning drops SR → all retained state is load-bearing and memory must scale with the full workspace.
7. **H7 — Scene memory that survives a genuine session restart retains coverage a from-scratch rebuild cannot match.** None of this direction's own cited systems has been tested across a process boundary: [[2410.11989|DovSG]]'s own framing is explicit — "Enabling True Long-term Autonomy" means operating "without manual resets," i.e. within one continuous deployment, never across one — and [[2410.06237|BUMBLE]]'s "long-term memory" is a small offline-curated failure-exemplar set gathered *before* its 70-trial run and frozen throughout, not live-accumulated across separately-launched sessions. [[2511.18112|EchoVLA]]'s own Limitations section names the unsolved case directly: a "cold-start problem in novel environments where the 3D memory is initially sparse" — the exact condition a restart produces.
   - *Prediction*: a scene memory checkpointed to disk and reloaded after a genuine process restart (kill → reload → relaunch), with 20% of tracked objects relocated during the gap, retains ≥90% of its same-session multi-room object-localization accuracy; a from-scratch rebuild under the same conditions needs ≥3× the exploration steps to reach equivalent task-relevant map coverage.
   - *Test*: extend [[2511.18112|EchoVLA]]'s scene+episodic memory or [[2411.04999|DynaMem]]'s ray-cast-purged map with disk checkpointing; kill the policy process between a build phase and a query phase, reload from disk, relocate 20% of tracked objects during the gap; compare localization accuracy and exploration-steps-to-coverage against a from-scratch-rebuilt baseline under identical relocation.
   - *Row*: EchoVLA (scene+episodic substrate to extend) / DynaMem (ray-cast purge substrate to extend) / DovSG ("without manual resets" — the framing to invert and test directly).
   - *Falsifier*: the from-scratch rebuild matches persisted-memory accuracy within a few rounds of exploration → the restart boundary is not load-bearing, and B2's existing within-deployment multi-room framing (H1–H6) already captures what matters.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **No cited system has ever been tested across a process restart** — checked directly: [[2410.11989|DovSG]] states its persistence works "without manual resets," and [[2410.06237|BUMBLE]]'s long-term memory is gathered and frozen *before* evaluation, not live-accumulated across sessions. → H7 is the first test of this boundary; stage it behind H1–H3 (confirm the within-deployment scene+episodic result first, since the restart harness is real infrastructure work with no existing protocol to reuse).
> - **Memory can accumulate stale/wrong state** — a remembered object location goes invalid when the world changes, and a static scene map will confidently return the wrong place. → H2 tests static vs dynamic memory on relocated objects; adopt [[2411.04999|DynaMem]]'s online ray-cast purging and report SR on relocated objects as a measured failure mode.
> - **Memory scales with workspace** — multi-room state can blow up linearly with area. → H6 tests memory-footprint vs SR under pruning; prune to task-relevant state and report the footprint–SR curve rather than retaining everything.
> - **Memory and perception interact** — bad active perception (B1) writes bad memory, so a memory gain can be a perception artifact. → H5's 2×2 separates write-quality (active vs passive perception) from the memory mechanism; report SR with active-vs-passive perception feeding memory.
> - **A long observation window may be the cheaper baseline** — if stacking frames suffices, explicit memory is over-engineering. → H4 sweeps window length against declarative memory on multi-room tasks; the claim only holds if a long window stays below memory, which is the falsifier to clear first.

---

## Cluster C — Force-Adaptive Coordination Under Load

*Whole-body control under external wrench, payload, and contact force — where a force at the hand propagates through the kinematic chain to the support polygon, the legs compensate for what the arms feel, and the standard task reward silently omits the force. The three directions split the load problem along its three irreducible faces: making the policy perform under a force it must model and anticipate (C1), certifying that it cannot fall or collide when the load drifts out of distribution (C2), and letting the policy itself choose how compliant to be, region by region, instead of treating stiffness as a value that arrives from outside it (C3).*

### C1 — Force-Adaptive Whole-Body Control Under Unknown Wrench

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | C — Force-Adaptive Coordination Under Load |
| **Thesis** | Build a whole-body policy that *anticipates* the unknown external hand wrench and the base/leg reaction it induces, instead of a stiff tracker that treats force as a disturbance to reject. The reason it must work: an external wrench at the hand propagates through the kinematic chain to every joint, including the support legs, so force adaptation is a whole-body equilibrium problem, not an arm problem. The field assumes whole-body controllers can be force-agnostic. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] (method), [[2603.03751\|IO-WBC]] (method), [[2606.03297\|SplitAdapter]] (method), [[2502.10894\|UAN]] (method), [[2512.01061\|Sim-to-Real-Door]] (method), [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (benchmark), [[2607.06052\|ThorArena]] (benchmark), [[2606.16542\|ADAPT-Locomotion]] (method), [[2308.14636\|Linear-Impactor]] (benchmark), [[2404.19173\|Single-Contact++-RL]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] 0.37 vs 0.60 upper-body tracking error under large force (~2×), 100 N cart-pull + 1.2 kg/hand, 0–100 N (up to 30% body weight) torque-limit-aware 3D force curriculum; [[2603.03751\|IO-WBC]] 80% success (4/5) carrying an 18 kg tire vs 0% for a SOTA WBC baseline, pushes a 65 kg crate where the baseline fails at 50 kg — no F/T sensor, the largest load envelope in the cluster; [[2502.10894\|UAN]] real 113 N cart-drag (10 m) + 8 kg lift + 20 m throw via unsupervised actuator net (no torque sensors); [[2606.03297\|SplitAdapter]] 26/27 sim-real full-task vs 16/27 base + 86/90 sim under OOD 6 kg loads; [[2512.01061\|Sim-to-Real-Door]] 83% real door-opening (RGB-only, contact-rich) + 23.8% faster than human teleop |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: a humanoid opening a heavy door or carrying a variable payload meets "significant, dynamic, and multi-directional 3D end-effector forces," but the standard stack either solves the upper body with a kinematic IK that compensates force late, or trains one monolith that never models force at all — the wrench is left as an unmodelled disturbance.
- **Today's answers**: the humanoid force-adaptive cluster is *reactive* — [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] gets implicit compensation from a torque-limit-aware 3D force curriculum (0.37 under large force, ~2× over a 0.60 monolith, 100 N cart); [[2511.20275|HAFO]] follows upper-body angles under 10–50 N hand disturbances; [[2510.26280|Thor]] earns 167.7 N pull via a force-augmented reward; [[2603.14308|Load-Aware-Loco-Manipulation]] estimates load from history for a 5 kg carry; [[2606.03297|SplitAdapter]] factorizes the load context from history (26/27 vs 16/27 under 6 kg). All *estimate-then-compensate from the past*. The one paper that *anticipates* — [[2201.03871|ALMA-Wrench-Prediction]] — predicts the wrench and feeds it forward to the legs 0–0.8 s ahead (208% over reactive, leans against a 150 N push), but only for the *self-induced known* wrench from the robot's own arm plan, on a *quadruped*, with a *model-based* predictor.
- **The opening**: ALMA proves anticipatory feedforward beats reactive — but explicitly *not* for unknown external environmental forces (cart/door/payload), and not on a humanoid support polygon. The unexplored delta is estimating an *unknown external* wrench first, then *learning-forward-modeling* its whole-body reaction on a *humanoid* — exactly where FALCON's "kinematically limited force compensation" diagnosis and ALMA's self-induced-only scope leave the gap.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A force at the hand — an external wrench $F_{\text{ext}}$ — does not stay at the hand. It propagates through the kinematic chain via $J_{\text{ext}}^{\top}$ to every joint, including the support legs that keep the centre of mass over the feet. So force adaptation is a whole-body balance problem: the legs must compensate for what the arms feel, while every joint torque stays within limits and the centre of mass stays over the support polygon. It is not a local arm-stiffness problem, regardless of the training data. [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] demonstrates the structure is load-bearing: its torque-limit-aware force curriculum — applying only *physically feasible* forces that respect joint limits via inverse dynamics — is what earns the 0.60→0.37 tracking gain and the stable exploration a naive (limit-blind) force schedule cannot.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "anticipation beats reactive for legged force" (taken — [[2201.03871|ALMA-Wrench-Prediction]] proved it, 208% over reactive) but the narrower scope ALMA explicitly excludes: that anticipation only works for the *self-induced known* wrench, with a *model-based* predictor, on a quadruped. The reactive humanoid trackers ([[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]], [[2511.20275|HAFO]], [[2510.26280|Thor]], [[2603.14308|Load-Aware-Loco-Manipulation]]) all leave the *unknown external* wrench un-anticipated, and [[2606.03297|SplitAdapter]] sharpens it from the adaptation side — a unified latent conflating load with dynamics mismatch loses robustness under heavy load, so the load needs a dedicated representation that history estimation provides only *after* the load lands.
- **The bet**: A humanoid policy that estimates an *unknown external* wrench then *learns to forward-model* its whole-body reaction (a learned residual through $J_{\text{ext}}^{\top}$ feeding pre-emptive leg compensation) holds balance under a *step-applied* load with a measurable advantage in the first ~100 ms after onset where [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s reactive compensation lags — the sudden-onset CoM-excursion advantage at matched force is the headline metric, not FALCON's borrowed 0.37 — while sustaining [[2502.10894|UAN]]-class loads (113 N cart, 8 kg lift), and extends [[2201.03871|ALMA-Wrench-Prediction]]'s anticipatory mechanism from self-induced-known to unknown-external on a tight support polygon. Falsifiable: if anticipation matches reactive at the same step load on a humanoid, or the wrench is too noisy to forward-model, force is adequately handled reactively.

*Cross-references (already cited elsewhere in this doc, not re-tabled here):* [[2511.21169\|Kinematics-Aware-Multi-Policy]] (cited under A1) is also a real C1 data point — its force-based curriculum trains the lower-body policy to proactively support a 112.8 kg cart pushed/pulled by the hands, plus a delta-command policy compensating end-effector drift induced by leg motion.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how the force / load signal is acquired and how it enters the policy* (implicit from a force curriculum / factorized context from history / learned actuator model / vision-only implicit / explicit anticipatory prediction) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| [[2606.03297\|SplitAdapter]] | factorized object/load + dynamics-mismatch contexts via FiLM, GRL-separated | privileged context from history | 26/27 sim-real vs 16/27 base, 86/90 sim under OOD 6 kg; clean mass-organized latent | estimates load from *past* history (lagging), not an anticipatory model of the reaction the next action induces |
| [[2201.03871\|ALMA-Wrench-Prediction]] | predicts the *self-induced known* arm wrench, feeds it forward to the legs 0–0.8 s ahead | anticipatory (model-based, known wrench) | 208% over reactive/naive; leans against a 150 N push, zero-shot across manipulator weights, real ALMA | the anticipatory *mechanism* C1 borrows — but only the *self-induced known* wrench, on a *quadruped*, with a *model-based* predictor; explicitly not unknown external forces (the topThreat that narrows the general claim) |

| [[2506.13432\|Kalman-sysID]] | Kalman-filter online identification of a quadruped's mass/CoM shift, covariance-thresholded update gating, feeding an adaptive MPC+WBC stack | classical Kalman filter — a distinct estimator family from every cited RL-teacher-student or analytic-momentum-observer row | reliable mass/CoM estimates gated by a quantified covariance-reliability threshold, absorbed by the adaptive MPC+WBC stack | a *base-mounted/rigidly-attached* payload (mass on the floating base), not a hand-applied external wrench propagating through an arm — a narrower slice of C1's problem than [[2606.16542\|ADAPT-Locomotion]]'s hand-load estimate, but a genuinely new estimator family worth tracking as C1's mechanism space widens |
| [[2508.09846\|SATYRR-Teleop]] | multi-stage online 3D object-inertial-parameter estimation (vision + VLM prior + parallel-sim CEM) drives automatic lean/equilibrium-point compensation for payload-induced disturbance on a wheeled humanoid under haptic teleoperation | vision + estimated object params (no F/T sensor on the robot) | keeps haptic feedback and balance meaningful under payload disturbance during teleoperated lift/carry | weakest of the seven new rows — a wheeled base, not legs (C1's stated propagation target), and a *human teleoperator* is in the anticipation loop rather than an autonomous policy |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (anticipating the wrench's whole-body reaction beats reactive force handling), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Anticipating an unknown external wrench beats reactive compensation in the first ~100 ms, where ALMA's self-induced-only mechanism does not reach.**
   - *Prediction*: a learned forward model that estimates an *unknown external* step load and pre-compensates the legs holds balance with a measurable CoM-excursion advantage in the first ~100 ms after onset where [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s reactive compensation lags — extending [[2201.03871|ALMA-Wrench-Prediction]]'s self-induced-known anticipation (208% over reactive) to the unknown-external regime ALMA excludes, on a humanoid support polygon.
   - *Test*: apply *unknown* step force impulses at the end-effector; compare CoM excursion and tracking error in the first 100 ms for the learned anticipatory model vs FALCON's reactive compensation vs ALMA's model-based self-induced predictor, at matched force magnitude.
   - *Row*: FALCON (curriculum-shaped, reactive) vs ALMA (anticipatory, self-induced known).
   - *Falsifier*: anticipation matches reactive at the same step load on a humanoid → reactive compensation already absorbs the reaction and prediction is redundant.
2. **H2 — The torque-limit-aware curriculum, not raw force exposure, earns the gain.**
   - *Prediction*: isolating [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s torque-feasibility constraint (forces respect joint limits via inverse dynamics) delivers the 0.60→0.37 drop and stable exploration, while a limit-blind force schedule of equal magnitude degrades both — the feasibility envelope is the lever, not the force amount.
   - *Test*: ablate torque-limit-aware vs limit-blind 3D force curriculum at matched force magnitudes; report tracking error and exploration stability.
   - *Row*: FALCON (curriculum-shaped, reactive).
   - *Falsifier*: limit-blind force training matches → the torque envelope is incidental and any force curriculum suffices.
3. **H3 — Factorized load context beats a unified latent specifically under heavy OOD load.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2606.03297|SplitAdapter]]'s split object/load vs dynamics contexts deliver the 16/27→26/27 robustness *concentrated* in the heavy-load bins (6 kg OOD), with the gap over a unified adapter near-zero at light loads where the conflation does not yet hurt.
   - *Test*: ablate factorized vs unified adapter, stratify success by payload mass (2 / 4 / 6 kg); report the per-mass margin.
   - *Row*: SplitAdapter (privileged context from history).
   - *Falsifier*: a unified latent matches across all masses → factorization is not the lever under load.
4. **H4 — Explicit force sensing adds little once the curriculum and load context are in place.**
   - *Prediction*: adding a measured force/torque channel — e.g. [[2606.13232|WT-UMI]]'s wearable distributed-tactile force (1.05 N RMSE) + admittance regulation — on top of [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s curriculum + [[2606.03297|SplitAdapter]]'s load context yields a *sub-linear* gain on tasks the curriculum already covers, but a *super-linear* gain on bulky/deformable contact-rich tasks where WT-UMI's explicit force is load-bearing (Bucket 60%→80%), so the value of sensing is concentrated by contact complexity, not uniform — consistent with [[2512.01061|Sim-to-Real-Door]] still reaching 83% on rigid doors with RGB only.
   - *Test*: add WT-UMI's tactile-force channel to the curriculum+context policy; stratify the marginal SR/error gain by contact complexity (rigid vs deformable/bulky) against the sensing cost.
   - *Row*: WT-UMI (directly sensed) / Sim-to-Real Door (vision-only) vs FALCON (curriculum-shaped).
   - *Falsifier*: explicit sensing's gain is flat across contact complexity → implicit handling suffices everywhere and the tactile channel is incidental.
5. **H5 — A shared predictor carries more force-adaptation than a shared observation.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s "mutual awareness" (legs and arms share proprioception) recovers less of the under-force tracking gain than a shared *explicit* wrench-reaction predictor at equal capacity — predicting the reaction beats merely observing the other agent.
   - *Test*: ablate FALCON's shared observation vs a shared reaction predictor on the N/M/L-force tracking task at matched parameters.
   - *Row*: FALCON (curriculum-shaped, reactive).
   - *Falsifier*: shared observation matches the shared predictor → emergent mutual awareness is sufficient.
6. **H6 — The actuator model is what unlocks the high-load regime, separable from hand-force adaptation.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2502.10894|UAN]]'s unsupervised actuator net is necessary for the 113 N / 8 kg loads to transfer, and stacking it under a hand-force-adaptive policy raises the *deployable* load ceiling beyond either alone — actuator fidelity and wrench adaptation are orthogonal contributors.
   - *Test*: cross factorial {UAN actuator net on/off} × {hand-force adaptation on/off}; report the maximum sim-to-real-stable load per cell.
   - *Row*: UAN (learned actuator model) vs FALCON (curriculum-shaped).
   - *Falsifier*: the actuator net and force adaptation are non-additive → one subsumes the other and the high-load ceiling has a single cause.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Anticipation needs an accurate inertia/torque model** — the predicted reaction depends on the URDF + actuator model. → Treat the wrench-reaction predictor as a learned residual over a nominal analytic model; lean on [[2502.10894|UAN]]'s unsupervised actuator net where the model is uncertain (H6), and report sim-vs-real reaction-prediction error.
> - **Force adaptation can trade off precision** — compliant force-following may drift the end-effector. → Report the force-magnitude vs tracking-error Pareto front, per [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s under-force error curve, not a single number.
