> - **Large loads stress hardware + balance** — 113 N / 8 kg push the support polygon to its limit. → Bound load claims to validated platforms; couple to C2's certified filter for the balance guarantee under the loads C1 reaches.
> - **Implicit handling may already suffice** — FALCON's curriculum and SplitAdapter's context capture force without an explicit term. → This is the bet's falsifier (H1/H4): run anticipatory vs implicit head-to-head at matched load; if implicit wins, report it and keep the curriculum.
> - **The first-100 ms headline is hostage to the *composed* loop latency, not any one component** — the metric is a transient advantage measured *across* the wrench-estimate → forward-model → leg-torque loop, so it must be priced against the whole onboard budget, not against [[2606.13232|WT-UMI]]'s 68 ms force-prediction-head lag (a demo-data number, not a control-loop latency). → Report the *summed* deployed budget — momentum-observer settling + policy inference + actuator bandwidth — as a distribution and check it clears the closed-loop ceiling (perception-bound loops run far slower than proprioceptive-only loops); if the composed loop consumes most of the 100 ms window, the transient advantage is unmeasurable as designed and the headline must move to a steady-state load-ceiling number.

### C2 — Safety-Bounded Whole-Body Control Under Load

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | C — Force-Adaptive Coordination Under Load |
| **Thesis** | Wrap whole-body loco-manipulation in a *certified* safety layer — a barrier or learned-manifold projection that provably keeps balance and collision-freeness, or a predictive monitor that fires before a fall — instead of hoping a learned policy stays safe when load and geometry drift out of distribution. The reason it must work: a learned policy optimizes an expectation and carries no per-step guarantee under OOD wrench/geometry, while balance and collision are hard physical boundaries a violation cannot undo. The field assumes domain randomization and reward shaping yield safe-enough behavior. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2604.07457\|CMP]] (method), [[2605.25546\|ISSf-CBF-WBC]] (method), [[2602.01515\|RAPT]] (method), [[2310.12567\|Safety-Gymnasium]] (benchmark), [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (benchmark), [[2502.03132\|SPARK]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2604.07457\|CMP]] OOD-survival 46.9% vs 4.7% (geometry, ~10×) / 40.3% vs 6.9% (sensor noise, ~6×) sim, real 100% in-dist / 93.3% moderate / 86.7% extreme OOD, 2.99 ms latency, best-effort projection to the closest feasible intention; [[2605.25546\|ISSf-CBF-WBC]] 20% mass mismatch → collision-free vs standard CBF / eCBF ~50% collision, real TOCABI single-leg balance + teleop collision-avoidance; [[2602.01515\|RAPT]] AUROC 0.92 sim / 75% real-anomaly recall at zero nominal false positives, 1.63 ms, 75% Top-1 root-cause (87.5% with vision) across 16 real failures |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: a policy that works in-distribution gives no guarantee when geometry, payload, or sensor noise drifts OOD — and under load the failure modes are *physical*: a fall, a self-collision, a workspace-boundary violation, each unrecoverable. The standard answer is domain randomization plus reward shaping, which buys robustness *in expectation* but certifies nothing.
- **Today's answers**: the "certified barrier over a black-box learned policy on a real humanoid" mechanism is no longer novel in itself — [[2505.11494|SHIELD-Humanoid]] already ships a probabilistic certified barrier over a learned RL policy on a real Unitree G1, and [[2510.14959|CBF-RL]] internalizes the CBF into the policy (99.0% vs 38.7% filter-only) — but *both for locomotion / obstacle-avoidance, with no payload or external-wrench term*. The on-axis under-load systems split into certified and reactive: [[2604.07457|CMP]] lifts unshielded 4.7%→46.9% sim / 86.7% extreme-OOD real via learned manifold projection (2.99 ms); [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]] stays collision-free under 20% mass mismatch (vs ~50% standard CBF) on a real TOCABI; [[2603.02443|Safe-WBLM]] adds a wrench-aware certified governor but *on a quadruped* with no humanoid survival numbers; [[2602.01515|RAPT]] flags OOD (AUROC 0.92) and fires a predefined stop. The robustness-by-training / detect-then-stop baselines ([[2503.00923|HWC]], [[2603.22703|Safe-Stoppability-Monitor]], [[2511.18509|SafeFall]]) are the falsifier targets. No one compares certified vs reward-shaping *under load*.
- **The opening**: CMP's reduction makes a certified check O(1) (2.99 ms) — but the surviving novelty is not the barrier-over-learned-policy mechanism (taken by SHIELD); it is the *payload/wrench-aware barrier* (H2) and the *certified-vs-reward head-to-head under load* (H1), neither of which any humanoid safety paper runs. A full-corpus sweep of every CBF-adjacent note in the vault (39 candidates checked) turned up zero further genuine C2 fits beyond what's cited above — a confirmed field-level gap in the literature, not a scan miss.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: Balance — the centre of mass inside the support polygon — and collision-freeness are *hard constraints* with no soft trade-off: a violation is a fall or a crash, not a small loss. A learned policy optimizes an expectation over training conditions and carries no per-step guarantee once load or geometry drifts OOD; a barrier or projection filter enforces the constraint set as a hard boundary, projecting any unsafe command to the closest feasible one. [[2604.07457|CMP]] measures the necessity directly: unshielded whole-body tracking survives only 4.7% of OOD-geometry tasks, and its key insight — separating "out-of-*training*-distribution" from "out-of-*safe*-distribution" so a never-mastered but in-distribution command is also caught — is what a reward penalty cannot express.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "a certified barrier over a learned policy is novel" ([[2505.11494|SHIELD-Humanoid]] / [[2510.14959|CBF-RL]] already do it on a real humanoid) but the under-load gap they leave: that a *kinematics-only* barrier (no payload/wrench term) and *robustness-by-training* ([[2503.00923|HWC]]) suffice when the load drifts OOD. The DR + reward-shaping policies CMP and ISSf-CBF beat bet on this; [[2604.07457|CMP]]'s 4.7% OOD-geometry survival and [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s ~50% baseline collision under mass mismatch mark the boundary — a soft reward cannot *guarantee* a hard constraint under shift, and a wrench-blind barrier has no support-polygon term once the payload pulls the CoM. [[2602.01515|RAPT]] adds the silent-failure evidence: sim-robust policies "execute confidently in OOD states," exactly what reward shaping leaves uncaught.
- **The bet**: A *wrench-aware* certified layer — a barrier whose safe set carries the external-payload equilibrium term — holds collision-free single-leg balance under an unknown payload where [[2505.11494|SHIELD-Humanoid]]'s and [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s kinematics-only barriers (tuned without a force term) violate the support polygon, and in a *certified-vs-reward head-to-head under load* lifts OOD whole-body survival to [[2604.07457|CMP]]'s 86.7% extreme / 93.3% moderate real (at 2.99 ms) where a reward-penalty-only policy of equal capacity ([[2503.00923|HWC]]-class robustness-by-training) cannot. Falsifiable: if a kinematics-only barrier holds balance under load, or reward shaping matches the certified layer on OOD survival and collision under mass mismatch, the wrench term and the guarantee buy nothing over robustness-by-training.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how the safety guarantee is enforced and when it acts* (formal forward-invariance barrier / learned-manifold projection / predictive detect-then-respond / soft reward-or-feasibility penalty) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | Guarantee mechanism | When it acts | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [[2605.25546\|ISSf-CBF-WBC]] | input-to-state-safe control barrier function (forward invariance of an enlarged safe set) | *before* execution, formal, model-based | collision-free under 20% mass mismatch vs ~50% standard/eCBF collision, real TOCABI | the guarantee needs an accurate dynamics model + conservative tuning; no payload-wrench term in the barrier |
| [[2505.11494\|SHIELD-Humanoid]] | probabilistic certified barrier layered over a black-box learned RL policy | *before* execution, formal, on a real humanoid | adaptive conservativeness keeps collision below target probability; real G1 obstacle + pedestrian avoidance | the exact "certified barrier over a learned policy on a real humanoid" mechanism — but *locomotion / obstacle-avoidance*, no payload/wrench term, no OOD-load survival (the topThreat that takes the mechanism, leaves the under-load axis) |
| [[2510.14959\|CBF-RL]] | CBF internalized into the policy during training (no runtime filter) | *during* training, formal-then-internalized | 99.0% vs 38.7% filter-only; zero-shot G1 obstacle avoidance + 0.3 m stairs | internalizes the barrier but for *locomotion/navigation* — no external-wrench term, so no certified guarantee once a payload drifts OOD |
| [[2603.02443\|Safe-WBLM]] | reference-governor QP enforcing position/velocity/kinematic limits on a force-tracking WBC | *before* execution, formal, model-based | EE velocity MSE ≤0.005 m²/s²; recursive feasibility, compliant external-wrench tracking | a *wrench-aware* certified governor — but on a *quadruped* with no humanoid support-polygon survival numbers (the under-load mechanism C2 ports to a humanoid) |
| [[2604.07457\|CMP]] | learned competence-manifold projection to the closest feasible intention (O(1)) | *every frame*, best-effort, learned | 46.9% vs 4.7% OOD-geometry sim, 86.7% extreme-OOD real, 2.99 ms | empirical "best-effort," no formal proof; the manifold is learned per-deployment, not certified under load |
| [[2503.00923\|HWC]] | robustness-by-training (curriculum + disturbance exposure), no run-time shield | *during* training, no run-time guarantee | 95.88% under low-freq forces, 81.27% under high-impulse; real G1 push/kick recovery | the robustness-by-training baseline the certified-vs-reward head-to-head (H1) must beat — buys robustness in expectation, certifies nothing under shift |
| [[2603.22703\|Safe-Stoppability-Monitor]] | predictive monitor characterizing the safe-stoppable boundary, fires a stop | *on detection*, discrete (stop) | 94.1% unsafe-prediction accuracy with 40% less trajectory data, real G1 | a detect-then-stop monitor like RAPT — *interrupts* rather than continuing, no payload-aware certified continuation under load |
| [[2602.01515\|RAPT]] | predictive OOD detection + LLM root-cause diagnosis, fires a predefined response | *on detection*, discrete (stop / fall / recover) | AUROC 0.92 sim, 75% real recall at 0 nominal FP, 1.63 ms, 75% root-cause | *interrupts* the task rather than keeping it running — detection + diagnosis, but no continuation policy |
| [[2605.21935\|MIF]] | perception-grounded interaction-pose-safety check: on-demand watertight-mesh recovery (flow-matching) under gait-distorted observation, verified *before* the interaction | *before interaction*, geometry-grounded | 94% IPS / 0% collision vs point-cloud 62%/32% and static-mesh 78%/14%; 98% under object relocation; 1.6 s, <4 GB | certifies the *pose*, not the *trajectory* — a pre-interaction geometry check, not an in-loop barrier on every control step (the observability-quality complement, couples to B1) |
| [[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] | torque-limit-aware force curriculum (a *soft* feasibility constraint baked into training) | *during* training, no run-time guarantee | 0.37 vs 0.60 under force, 100 N cart, 1.2 kg/hand | feasibility is learned, not enforced — gives no per-step boundary under OOD load (the C1 policy a hard filter wraps) |
| [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] | Kinematic Manifold Prior constraining action to feasible configurations | *during* control, soft (prior) | 12.4 mm world-frame EE error; KMP halves hand error | a kinematic-feasibility *prior*, not a certified safe set — constrains configuration, not collision/balance under disturbance (feeds A3) |
| [[2603.03279\|ULTRA]] | robustness by RL finetuning over OOD goals (no explicit shield) | *during* training, no run-time guarantee | 73% dense / 50–90% sparse, +200% OOD position-only | robustness-by-training — the baseline a certified shield must beat, with no hard guarantee under shift |
| [[2604.07993\|HEX]] | coupling-aware policy with no safety layer | *no shield* | 79.8 ID / 61.8 OOD vs GR00T N1.5 70.2 / 41.0 | the strong unshielded policy a safety filter would wrap — its OOD survival is bounded by training coverage (feeds A1) |
| [[2512.01061\|Sim-to-Real-Door]] | unshielded contact-rich policy (massive randomization only) | *no shield* | 83% real door-opening, RGB-only | robustness from randomization breadth — the unshielded contact-rich baseline a filter would bound (feeds C1) |
| [[2506.11033\|Adaptive-Shielding]] | online adaptive shield: a function-encoder infers hidden mass/friction online, an Adaptive-Conformal-Prediction safety margin filters unsafe actions, plus training-time Safety-Regularized Optimization | *every frame*, formal-probabilistic, online-adapting | lower cost-rate with competitive return and OOD-dynamics generalization under unseen mass/friction at modest overhead (3.94 s → 5.05 s, Car-Goal) | the missing adaptive-shield-vs-static-barrier cell: neither [[2605.25546\|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s fixed enlarged safe set nor [[2604.07457\|CMP]]'s per-deployment manifold adapts the shield online to the payload/mass shift — but SafetyGym-class, not a humanoid support polygon under load (the C2 mass-mismatch axis, H1/H4) |
| [[2505.00779\|Uncertainty-Latent-Safety-Filter]] | learned latent-space Hamilton-Jacobi reachability filter augmented with epistemic-uncertainty (ensemble Jensen-Rényi divergence) + conformal-calibrated OOD detection, penalizing high-uncertainty states in the safety value | *every frame*, formal-reachability, uncertainty-gated | real-world Jenga failure 0.08 vs 0.82 LatentSafe; OOD-classification balanced-accuracy 0.94; 0.20 sim block-plucking failure vs 0.30 | directly the 'world-model overconfident in OOD → catastrophic' concern C2 names — a reachability barrier that gates on percept fidelity (H7) — but a visual-manipulation arm, not a whole-body wrench/balance term, and the safe set is learned not modeled |
| [[2511.06385\|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]] | reachability-based filter that keeps the intended PATH and modulates only speed/acceleration (temporal reparameterization), explicitly avoiding the OOD states reactive filters induce by altering the path | *every frame*, formal, model-based | 68% higher SR than reactive CBF; 79% vs 80% unprotected real HRI at 0% violation / 80% Safe-Success; 0.20 ms per step, +14% task speed | a distinct 'when/how it acts' cell — temporal reparameterization vs [[2604.07457\|CMP]]'s spatial command projection, serving best-effort continuation over a hard stop (H3) and the over-constrain Risk + latency (H5) — but dynamic-HRI diffusion/VLA, no payload-wrench or support-polygon term under load |
| [[2508.11129\|Poisson-CBF-Humanoid]] | a *predictive* CBF-constrained nonlinear MPC whose CBF constraints are synthesized *directly from perception* via extended (time-varying) Poisson safety functions, geometry-aware through Minkowski-lifted robot occupancy, on a real humanoid + quadruped | *every frame*, formal, predictive (MPC-horizon), perception-derived | real G1 humanoid navigates a narrow corridor by reorienting an oblong payload to fit; Poisson safety function stays positive throughout; PDE solve **20–100 ms**, MPC at **100 Hz** | the predictive-formal-CBF-*from-perception* cell none of [[2505.11494\|SHIELD-Humanoid]] (reactive probabilistic barrier), [[2511.06385\|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]] (temporal reparameterization), or [[2605.21935\|MIF]] (pre-*interaction* pose check) fill — it certifies against perception-*derived* geometry on every MPC step, extending the certified spectrum from per-frame projection to an MPC-horizon predictive filter and giving H7 a formal-barrier instance (geometry fidelity gates the guarantee); but obstacle/collision-avoidance geometry, no payload-wrench/support-polygon equilibrium term under load (the C2 axis it complements, not closes) |
| [[2601.07821\|FARL]] | world-model safety critic (latent dynamics + constraint-prediction head) that rolls out future steps; risky actions are swapped for a *pre-trained recovery policy* — detect-then-RECOVER rather than stop | *on prediction*, learned, continuation (recover) | 43.6% avg failure-episode reduction over 4 FailureBench sim tasks; real Franka Disturbed-Push 10 → 2 failures; +800% return vs PPO-Lagrangian / P3O / CPO | the detect-then-recover cell H6 needs that no current row fills — [[2602.01515\|RAPT]] and [[2603.22703\|Safe-Stoppability-Monitor]] STOP, [[2604.07457\|CMP]] projects continuously — but a Franka tabletop offline-to-online substrate (recovery half only, no humanoid balance-under-load numbers) |
| [[2502.02858\|p-SSA]] | Projected Safe Set Algorithm on a real G1 — decouples constraint relaxation from task optimization (first minimize multi-constraint violation, then optimize the task under the minimally-relaxed limb-level geometry constraints), *guaranteeing* feasible safe control under conflicting infeasible constraints | *before* execution, formal, principled-relaxation | reduces collisions + control-constraint violation vs naive SSA; outperforms relaxed r-SSA with no per-task tuning; safe single-leg balancing *with hand control* on real G1, generalizes across tasks zero parameter-tuning | the projected-safe-set + principled-relaxation cell between formal barrier and learned projection — it directly answers the *over-constrain / block-valid-actions* Risk no current row addresses ([[2604.07457\|CMP]] projects to the closest feasible but does not *resolve* multi-constraint conflict; [[2605.25546\|ISSf-CBF-WBC]] is a single barrier), and demonstrates the C2 H2 single-leg-balance-under-hand-control scenario; same G1 safe-set/SSA lineage as [[2502.03132\|SPARK]] |
| [[2502.03132\|SPARK]] | benchmark + modular toolbox of SOTA safe-control algorithms (5 energy-function safe controllers: SSA/CBF/SSS/PFM/SMA) with simulation benchmarks comparing safety approaches across environments/tasks/robot-models under one configurable safety-cost framework, real G1 deployment | *measures* the certified-vs-reward head-to-head (shared safety-cost framework) | SSS best for whole-body motion / SMA best for fixed-base; dynamic obstacles + density degrade arm-tracking + safety scores; real-time 0.011–0.015 s control loop on real G1 | the humanoid-specific safe-control comparison substrate H1's certified-vs-reward head-to-head needs — [[2310.12567\|Safety-Gymnasium]] is generic MuJoCo per-step-cost (no humanoid support-polygon-under-load) and HumanoidBench carries no safety/cost/violation term; SPARK lets the comparison run under one shared safety-cost framework on the real humanoid, but it scores end-controllers and ships no payload/wrench-equilibrium safe set of its own |
| [[2508.07611\|End-to-End]] | CBF forward-invariance translated into instantaneous CMDP costs trained with P3O — a direct *certified-vs-reward-shaping* head-to-head on a real humanoid | *during* training, formal-as-cost-then-internalized | **53%** unsafe-zone reduction vs PPO-RewardShaping; narrow-passage **60%** vs **33%** (P3O); dynamic-agent **86%** vs **70%**; sim-to-real G1 | a third locomotion-certified-vs-reward instance (beside [[2505.11494\|SHIELD-Humanoid]] / [[2510.14959\|CBF-RL]]) whose key insight is exactly H1's thesis ("CBF-as-cost overcomes reactive reward shaping") — but carries *no* payload/wrench/balance term (collision + HRI-comfort only), so it leaves the under-load gap C2 turns on, sharpening both H1's framing and the "no one runs the comparison under load" claim (P3O-CBF) |
| [[2607.14488\|Acc-CBF-QP]] | an *acceleration*-based real-time QP with CBF constraints (joint position/velocity/torque + self-collision) as a runtime post-processing filter over an *unmodified* RL policy, plus a Generalized-Momentum-Observer + Tilt-Observer disturbance estimate closing the CBF's model-mismatch gap | *every frame*, formal, runtime-filter over a black-box policy (no training-time coupling) | **92%** hardware constraint-violation reduction on real Unitree H1 (**10.04**→**0.80** violations/s) vs a Safe-RL (CaT) baseline; **0.348 ms**/cycle at 400 Hz; **~20%** longer median survival under aggressive commands, converting unsafe violations into physically-plausible falls | a fourth locomotion-certified instance (beside [[2505.11494\|SHIELD-Humanoid]] / [[2510.14959\|CBF-RL]] / End-to-End) but the *runtime-filter-over-frozen-policy* mechanism — no retraining, validated against a Safe-RL (not reward-shaping) baseline, and the only row reporting the composed disturbance-observer settling + filter latency explicitly — still *no* payload/wrench/balance term under load, so it sharpens H1's certified-vs-reward comparator set without closing the under-load gap |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (a certified safety layer beats reward-shaped robustness under OOD load/geometry), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — A certified layer beats reward-shaped safety *under load*, where SHIELD/CBF-RL's locomotion-only certification leaves the gap.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2604.07457|CMP]]'s projection or [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s barrier delivers the 4.7%→46.9% OOD-geometry survival / ~50%→0% collision under mass mismatch that a reward-penalty-only / robustness-by-training policy ([[2503.00923|HWC]]-class) of equal capacity cannot — and the certified advantage is *concentrated under payload OOD*, where [[2505.11494|SHIELD-Humanoid]]'s and [[2510.14959|CBF-RL]]'s locomotion-only certification has no term.
   - *Test*: compare {certified filter} vs {reward-penalty / HWC robustness-by-training} vs {SHIELD/CBF-RL locomotion-certified} on OOD geometry + 20% mass mismatch + payload OOD at matched capacity; report survival and collision per condition, running the constrained-vs-unconstrained head-to-head on [[2310.12567|Safety-Gymnasium]]'s standardized per-step violation-cost protocol (PPO-Lag 98% cost reduction on its MuJoCo-Humanoid velocity-constraint task) so the certified-vs-reward comparison uses a shared cost metric, not stitched method self-numbers.
   - *Row*: CMP (learned projection) / ISSf-CBF WBC (formal barrier) / Safety-Gymnasium (constrained-vs-unconstrained protocol) vs HWC (robustness-by-training) / SHIELD-Humanoid (locomotion-certified).
   - *Falsifier*: reward shaping or locomotion-only certification matches the under-load certified layer → robustness-by-training suffices and the guarantee is redundant.
2. **H2 — A wrench-aware barrier holds balance under unknown load where a kinematics-only barrier fails.**
   - *Prediction*: extending [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s barrier with the external-wrench equilibrium constraint (the C1 coupling) maintains collision-free single-leg balance under an unknown payload where the kinematics-only barrier — tuned without a force term — violates the support-polygon constraint.
   - *Test*: add a payload-wrench term to the ISSf-CBF safe set; compare balance-violation rate with vs without it under stepped end-effector loads.
   - *Row*: ISSf-CBF WBC (formal barrier).
   - *Falsifier*: the kinematics-only barrier holds balance under load → the wrench term is unnecessary and kinematic safety transfers for free.
3. **H3 — Best-effort projection preserves more task success than a hard stop.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2604.07457|CMP]]'s graceful projection to the closest feasible intention completes more of the OOD task than [[2602.01515|RAPT]]'s detect-then-stop response on the *same* OOD episodes, because continuation along the competence boundary recovers partial success a hard stop forfeits.
   - *Test*: run both shields on identical OOD-geometry episodes; report task-completion-under-shield and the survival/completion trade.
   - *Row*: CMP (learned projection) vs RAPT (detect-then-respond).
   - *Falsifier*: detect-then-stop matches best-effort projection on completion → graceful degradation adds nothing over a clean interrupt.
4. **H4 — Formal forward-invariance degrades more gracefully than a learned manifold under growing mismatch.**
   - *Prediction*: as the model/payload mismatch grows past the training range, [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s input-to-state-safe guarantee (deviation bounded by disturbance magnitude) degrades smoothly while [[2604.07457|CMP]]'s learned manifold, fit per-deployment, fails more abruptly once mismatch exceeds its training distribution.
   - *Test*: sweep mass mismatch from 0% upward; report collision/survival for the formal barrier vs the learned projection and locate each method's cliff.
   - *Row*: ISSf-CBF WBC (formal barrier) vs CMP (learned projection).
   - *Falsifier*: the learned manifold degrades as smoothly as the formal barrier → the formal guarantee buys no extra robustness margin.
5. **H5 — Latency, not coverage, is the binding constraint for an in-the-loop shield.**
   - *Prediction*: sweeping the shield's computational budget, OOD coverage saturates well below the [[2604.07457|CMP]] 2.99 ms / [[2602.01515|RAPT]] 1.63 ms class — beyond a few-millisecond budget extra computation does not improve survival, so a certified layer is feasible *because* its per-step check is O(1), not because it is cheap to train.
   - *Test*: profile survival vs per-step latency for the projection and detection shields; locate the latency at which coverage plateaus relative to the control-loop budget.
   - *Row*: CMP (learned projection) / RAPT (detect-then-respond).
   - *Falsifier*: coverage keeps rising with latency past the loop budget → no fixed-budget certified shield can both cover OOD and run in the loop.
6. **H6 — Predictive detection plus a recovery policy beats either a shield or a stop alone.**
   - *Prediction*: pairing [[2602.01515|RAPT]]'s OOD detection + root-cause diagnosis with a [[2604.07457|CMP]]-style projected continuation (detect → diagnose → project rather than detect → stop) recovers more episodes than RAPT's predefined stop and catches more silent failures than CMP's continuous projection alone.
   - *Test*: compose {RAPT detection → CMP projection} vs RAPT-stop vs CMP-only on the same OOD failures; report recovered-episode fraction and missed-failure rate.
   - *Row*: RAPT (detect-then-respond) + CMP (learned projection).
   - *Falsifier*: the composition matches the better single method → detection and projection are redundant and one shield suffices.
7. **H7 — Geometry fidelity gates the safety guarantee; a degraded percept makes a certified check unsafe.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2605.21935|MIF]]'s recovered-watertight-mesh interaction-pose check lifts IPS from a point-cloud / static-mesh baseline (62% / 78%) to 94% at 0% collision *because* the geometry it certifies against is higher-fidelity — so degrading the percept (gait-blur, stale mesh) degrades the certified survival roughly monotonically even with the safety logic fixed.
   - *Test*: hold the IPS check fixed, vary only the geometry source (raw point cloud / static mesh / on-demand recovered mesh) under graded gait-induced observation noise; report IPS survival and collision rate vs geometry fidelity; run the same fidelity-sweep on [[2508.11129|Poisson-CBF-Humanoid]]'s perception-derived CBF constraints (the formal-barrier instance of the same H7 dependency — its safe set is synthesized from the 2D occupancy map, so percept noise propagates straight into the certified set).
   - *Row*: MIF (perception-grounded geometry check) / Poisson-CBF Humanoid (perception-derived formal barrier).
   - *Falsifier*: IPS survival is flat across geometry fidelity → the guarantee is independent of percept quality and a cheap point cloud suffices.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **A safety filter can over-constrain and block valid actions** — an aggressive projection may refuse feasible commands. → [[2604.07457|CMP]]'s best-effort projection to the closest feasible intention (not a hard stop) preserves continuation; report task-completion-under-shield (H3), not safety alone.
> - **A safety check is only as good as the geometry it sees** — under gait-distorted perception a stale or noisy percept silently certifies an unsafe pose. → [[2605.21935|MIF]]'s confidence-aware 3DGS + on-demand mesh recovery raises geometry fidelity before the IPS check (H7); report safety survival vs percept fidelity, not against ground-truth geometry alone.
> - **A formal barrier needs an accurate model** — the guarantee depends on dynamics fidelity and conservative tuning. → [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s input-to-state-safe formulation tolerates a 20% mass mismatch by bounding the deviation; report the model-mismatch level the guarantee survives (H4).
> - **Latency can break the control loop** — a slow shield defeats real-time whole-body control. → H5's latency-vs-coverage sweep is the feasibility gate; bound to the 2.99 ms ([[2604.07457|CMP]]) / 1.63 ms ([[2602.01515|RAPT]]) class.
> - **Detection without continuation just stops the robot** — a binary stop forfeits the task and is unsafe mid-motion. → Pair predictive detection with a projected continuation (H6); report recovered-episode fraction, not just detection TPR.
> - **The force-observability tax: who pays for the wrench input the certified safe set needs** — a *wrench-aware* barrier (H2) needs the external payload wrench as an input, and that input is not free. It is either a measured F/T sensor (capital cost, calibration, mounting, failure modes) or a sensor-free proprioceptive estimate — [[2603.08961|FAME]] confirms the sensor-free route — it "estimates interaction forces from the robot dynamics ... without wrist force/torque sensors" (abstract verified) — though the specific momentum-observer form $F_{\text{ext}} = -(J^{\top})^{\dagger}(\tau - \tau_g)$ is the standard such estimator and is *not* spelled out in FAME's abstract, so treat the exact equation as illustrative rather than FAME's stated formula. → Charge for force sensing before recommending the certified safe set: the sensor-free estimator carries its own noise floor (the same lag/noise C1's bet rests on), so run an H7-style fidelity sweep on the *force* channel (not only the geometry channel) — does the barrier still certify when the wrench input is a noisy proprioceptive estimate rather than a calibrated F/T reading? If the certificate only holds with an expensive sensor, report the sensor-cost-vs-guarantee trade, not a guarantee priced as if the wrench were observed for free.

### C3 — Compliance Allocation as an Explicit Policy Action

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | C — Force-Adaptive Coordination Under Load |
| **Thesis** | Make joint-region impedance a channel the whole-body policy chooses, step by step, instead of a stiffness value the task or the operator sets from outside. The reason it must work: nothing in the equations of motion forces impedance to be fixed — a rigid-actuator low-level controller can take any admissible gain profile as an input, so a compliance channel fits the action space without contradicting the underlying dynamics. The field treats compliance as a dial to tune, not a decision to learn. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2511.04679\|GentleHumanoid]] (method), [[2510.17792\|SoftMimic]] (method), [[2512.14689\|CHIP]] (method), [[2505.20829\|Unified-Force-Position-Control]] (method), CoTaP (arXiv:2509.25443, method — not yet in the vault's KnowledgeHub), Variable-Stiffness-Quadruped (arXiv:2502.09436, method — not yet in the vault's KnowledgeHub), [[2508.00355\|TOP]] (method), [[2605.25546\|ISSf-CBF-WBC]] (method), [[2604.07457\|CMP]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | Variable-Stiffness-Quadruped (arXiv:2502.09436) PLS 85.65% vs P50 81.77% push-recovery success at the hardest tested force (<300 N) — this sweep is sim-only, though the same paper separately validates on real hardware (5 kg payload + outdoor-terrain walking) — with the legs opposite the push stiffening "way above the maximum stiffness of the high stiffness policy" while the legs toward the push relax; quadruped, no arm; [[2512.14689\|CHIP]] 1/k=0.04 → 80% multi-robot grasp SR vs 5% always-stiff / 40% non-perturbed; [[2511.04679\|GentleHumanoid]] peak hand force ~10 N vs >20 N rigid baseline; [[2508.00355\|TOP]] 95.30% SR vs Exbody 92.46% (the reference-modulation baseline C3's Pareto test is measured against) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: joint impedance never shows up as something a whole-body policy *decides* — every humanoid compliance system this doc has surveyed treats stiffness as a value that arrives from outside the policy: a task-designer's threshold, an operator's VR dial, or a constant baked into the impedance law. The action space has no channel for "how stiff to be, here, right now, given the load I feel."
- **Today's answers**: [[2511.04679|GentleHumanoid]]'s τ_safe (5–15 N) and [[2510.17792|SoftMimic]]'s K_cmd (40–1000 N/m) are both externally commanded inputs the policy conditions on, never chooses. [[2512.14689|CHIP]] gets closer — its continuous 1/k lets a VR teleoperator split compliance per hand online (1/k=0.001 holding a whiteboard rigid, 1/k=0.05 wiping it, in the same task) — but the split and the values are still set by a human, not inferred by the policy from the load itself. Where the field DOES shape the arm→base coupling at its source rather than compensating it downstream, it modulates the *reference* instead: [[2508.00355|TOP]] slows the whole arm by predicting a future timestamp, and [[2602.06341|HiWET]] absorbs the disturbance reactively through base and waist. Neither treats stiffness itself as the lever.
- **The opening**: the one system that makes stiffness a policy output at all is a quadruped with no arm. Variable-Stiffness-Quadruped (arXiv:2502.09436) puts per-joint/per-leg/hybrid stiffness groupings (PJS/PLS/HJLS) directly in the RL action space, and the policy discovers an asymmetric response with no reward term written for it — legs opposite a push stiffen, legs toward it relax — beating a fixed-gain baseline on push-recovery at the hardest tested force (85.65% vs 81.77%). It never touches an arm, a hand, or an object; the existence proof is real, but it stops at the leg-only boundary.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: Under a rigid-actuator low-level controller, joint impedance is a free parameter of the control law, not a quantity the dynamics fix. The equations of motion, $\ddot q = M(q)^{-1}(\tau - C(q,\dot q)\dot q - g(q) + J_{\text{ext}}^{\top}F_{\text{ext}})$, constrain what torque a commanded acceleration requires; they say nothing about how far a joint is *allowed to deviate* under an external load, which is exactly what a gain sets. So a compliance channel can sit in the action space without contradicting the coupling term A1 and C1 build on. Variable-Stiffness-Quadruped (arXiv:2502.09436) shows the structure is usable, not just permissible: given a per-joint/per-leg stiffness action channel and an ordinary task reward with no hand-written asymmetry term, an RL policy discovers direction-conditioned stiffening on its own.
- **Assumption being challenged**: That compliance is a design-time or task-time hyperparameter a human sets, not a decision the policy makes — the position held, without exception, by every humanoid compliance system in this doc. [[2511.04679|GentleHumanoid]]'s τ_safe is tunable but externally commanded; [[2510.17792|SoftMimic]]'s K_cmd is user-specified; [[2512.14689|CHIP]]'s 1/k is set online by a VR teleoperator; CoTaP (arXiv:2509.25443) states its task-space compliance "can be specified and integrated with other controllers"; [[2505.20829|Unified-Force-Position-Control]]'s K is a fixed constant written into the impedance law itself, one step further from a decision than a commanded dial. Five methodologically sophisticated, sim-to-real-validated systems, and in every one the stiffness number is an input, never an output.
- **The bet**: A humanoid policy that emits per-region impedance — a shoulder/elbow group and a hip/ankle group, the humanoid analog of Variable-Stiffness-Quadruped's PLS grouping — alongside the reach reference dominates [[2508.00355|TOP]]'s global speed-cap on the shared Pareto of peak CoM excursion in the first ~100 ms after reach onset vs EE tracking error, at matched task-completion time. This is not a claim that impedance is free: softening a joint does not change the commanded $M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$ term, only how far the joint is allowed to deviate under it — so the bet is that region-differentiated, load-reactive softening is a *different instrument on the same axis* as a coarse, pre-planned timing knob, with its own precision/attenuation trade-off, not that it strictly dominates for free. Falsifiable: if the impedance-allocation curve lies on or below TOP's speed-cap curve on that Pareto, per-region granularity buys nothing over the coarser global knob.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *who or what sets the compliance value, and at what granularity* — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | Compliance-value source | Granularity | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Variable-Stiffness-Quadruped (arXiv:2502.09436) | policy-chosen — RL action-space output (PJS/PLS/HJLS groupings) | per-joint / per-leg / hybrid | PLS 85.65% vs P50 81.77% push-recovery at <300 N max force; legs opposite the push autonomously stiffen, legs toward it relax; zero-shot sim-to-outdoor-terrain transfer | quadruped, legs only — no arm, no hand, no object contact; the mechanism this direction ports across the leg-only boundary (H2), not yet demonstrated for it |
| [[2511.04679\|GentleHumanoid]] | externally commanded (τ_safe sampled/set, fed to the policy as an observation) | whole upper-body, single tunable threshold | peak hand force ~10 N vs >20 N rigid baseline; posture-invariant compliance 5–15 N | the policy conditions on τ_safe but never chooses it — compliance is a task-designer's dial, the H3 ablation target |
| [[2510.17792\|SoftMimic]] | externally commanded (K_cmd, user-specified) | whole-body, single commanded stiffness (40–1000 N/m) | halves max contact force at low stiffness; generalizes to 20–50 cm objects | the same externally-commanded ceiling as GentleHumanoid — infers force from proprioception, but the stiffness value is an input, never chosen from the load |
| [[2512.14689\|CHIP]] | externally commanded (1/k, VR teleoperator, online) | per-end-effector (differs hand-to-hand) | 80% vs 5% always-stiff / 40% non-perturbed multi-robot grasp SR at 1/k=0.04; live bimanual hybrid compliance (stiff-hold + compliant-wipe) | the closest existing system to region-differentiated compliance — but the split and the values are chosen by a human operator in real time, not inferred by the policy from what it feels (H3) |
| CoTaP (arXiv:2509.25443) | externally specified (task-space compliance modulation) | per task-space direction | "adjustable task-space compliance can be specified and integrated with other controllers" (abstract) | specified, not learned — no reported mechanism for the policy to choose the value itself |
| [[2505.20829\|Unified-Force-Position-Control]] | fixed constant in the control law ($K$ in $F=K(x-x_{des})$) | EE + base (two regions, each a fixed gain — $K$ for the EE, $D$ for base velocity) | +39.5% contact-rich SR vs position-only; commanded-force error within 10 N | $K$/$D$ are hyperparameters of the impedance law itself, not even a commanded dial — the most rigid point on the axis this direction moves away from |
| [[2508.00355\|TOP]] | n/a — modulates the *reference*, not impedance (predicted-timestamp arm-speed cap) | one global knob (whole upper body) | 95.30% SR vs Exbody 92.46%; real base <2 mm / EE <6 mm tracking | shapes the coupling at its source by changing *when* the arm moves, not *how stiff* any region is — the reference-modulation alternative C3's central Pareto test (H1) is measured against |
| [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] | n/a — base/waist absorb the disturbance reactively | base + waist only | 12.4 mm world-frame EE error | reference/posture modulation, not impedance, and reactive rather than a chosen allocation |
| [[2511.21169\|Kinematics-Aware-Multi-Policy]] | n/a — part-wise decoupling fixed at design time | per-part (upper/lower), fixed architecture | 57% EE-height-oscillation cut under 4 kg payload | the part split is a design-time architectural decision, not a per-step policy choice, and it suppresses the reaction rather than choosing how compliant to be to it |
| [[2605.25546\|ISSf-CBF-WBC]] | n/a — formal barrier derived over a fixed-gain dynamics model | no impedance-state term | collision-free under 20% mass mismatch vs ~50% standard CBF | the safe set assumes a fixed gain profile; if impedance becomes a per-step policy output, the safe set itself becomes gain-dependent — the certified-layer re-derivation question H4 tests |
| [[2604.07457\|CMP]] | n/a — learned projection to the closest feasible intention, over a fixed policy | no impedance-state term | 46.9% vs 4.7% OOD-geometry survival | projects to feasibility under a fixed dynamics assumption; a variable-impedance policy changes what "feasible" means step to step, untested (H4) |
| [[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] | n/a — implicit compensation from a torque-limit-aware force curriculum, no impedance channel at all | none (reactive, curriculum-shaped) | 0.37 vs 0.60 upper-body error under force (~2×) | the zero-impedance-channel floor C1's anticipatory bet is measured against; C3 asks whether adding a compliance channel to it narrows C1's anticipation advantage (H5) |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (region-differentiated, policy-chosen impedance is a distinct and useful instrument, not a free lunch), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Per-region impedance allocation dominates a global speed cap on the CoM-excursion-vs-tracking-error Pareto.**
   - *Prediction*: a policy emitting per-region impedance (shoulder/elbow group vs hip/ankle group) alongside the reach reference produces a CoM-excursion-vs-EE-tracking-error Pareto frontier that strictly dominates [[2508.00355|TOP]]'s arm-speed-modulation frontier in the loaded-reach regime, at matched task-completion time.
   - *Test*: train the impedance-allocation policy and TOP's speed-cap baseline on the same loaded-reach task set; sweep both to trace their Pareto frontiers; compare peak CoM excursion in the first ~100 ms post-reach-onset against EE tracking error at matched completion time.
   - *Row*: TOP (reference modulation).
   - *Falsifier*: the impedance-allocation curve lies on or below TOP's curve → per-region granularity buys nothing over the coarser global timing knob.
2. **H2 — The quadruped PJS/PLS grouping mechanism transfers to the humanoid arm-base coupling.**
   - *Prediction*: a humanoid analog of Variable-Stiffness-Quadruped's PLS grouping (shoulder/elbow group vs hip/ankle group) reproduces the same asymmetric, load-direction-conditioned stiffening when the load is applied at the hand rather than the torso — the group opposite the load direction stiffens, the group toward it relaxes.
   - *Test*: apply the PJS/PLS/HJLS action-space recipe (arXiv:2502.09436) to a humanoid whole-body policy on [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s or [[2511.04679|GentleHumanoid]]'s force-curriculum tasks; measure whether the leg/base group stiffens opposite an arm-applied hand load.
   - *Row*: Variable-Stiffness-Quadruped (arXiv:2502.09436).
   - *Falsifier*: the humanoid policy converges to a single uniform stiffness regardless of grouping, or the stiffen/relax asymmetry does not track the load direction → the mechanism does not transfer across the leg-only-to-arm-base boundary.
3. **H3 — A context-conditioned, policy-chosen compliance value beats the best single externally-commanded setting.**
   - *Prediction*: swapping [[2511.04679|GentleHumanoid]]'s or [[2512.14689|CHIP]]'s externally-set compliance value for a load-conditioned, policy-chosen value reduces peak contact force at matched tracking error relative to the best single *fixed* setting swept over the same task distribution.
   - *Test*: ablate externally-set vs policy-chosen compliance on GentleHumanoid's hugging/contact tasks or CHIP's multi-robot grasp task, holding the rest of the architecture fixed; sweep the fixed setting to find its best operating point before comparing.
   - *Row*: GentleHumanoid (externally commanded) / CHIP (externally commanded, per-hand).
   - *Falsifier*: the best single fixed setting matches the policy-chosen value across the tested load range → the compliance value carries no situational information worth learning, and a dial suffices.
4. **H4 — Existing certified safe sets do not already have enough slack for a variable-impedance policy.**
   - *Prediction*: replaying [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s or [[2604.07457|CMP]]'s safe-margin computation under a swept range of commanded impedance gains (holding the policy fixed) drives the certificate's margin negative for gains still inside the training envelope — the certified layer needs an impedance-dependent safe set, not just re-validation at its default gain.
   - *Test*: sweep the commanded impedance gain across the trained range while holding the policy and task fixed; recompute the barrier/projection margin at each gain; report whether the margin ever crosses zero inside the envelope.
   - *Row*: ISSf-CBF-WBC (formal barrier) / CMP (learned projection).
   - *Falsifier*: the margin stays positive across the whole trained gain range → existing certificates already have enough slack, and only re-validation (not re-derivation) is needed.
5. **H5 — Compliance allocation substitutes for, rather than adds to, C1's anticipatory advantage.**
   - *Prediction*: adding a compliance channel to [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s reactive baseline absorbs part of the shock passively, so C1's first-~100 ms CoM-excursion advantage of anticipation over reactive compensation narrows once both conditions in that comparison have impedance allocation turned on.
   - *Test*: re-run C1's H1 test (anticipatory vs reactive at matched step load) with per-region impedance allocation added to both the anticipatory and the reactive condition; measure whether the anticipatory margin over FALCON narrows relative to the no-impedance-channel comparison.
   - *Row*: FALCON-Loco-Manipulation (zero-impedance-channel floor).
   - *Falsifier*: the anticipatory margin is unchanged with impedance allocation on for both conditions → the two mechanisms are additive and independent, not substitutes.
6. **H6 — Softening trades peak excursion for a longer settling tail; it does not eliminate the disturbance.**
   - *Prediction*: because a compliant joint does not change the commanded $\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$, only how far the joint deviates under load, region-differentiated softening lowers peak CoM excursion in the first ~100 ms at the cost of a *longer* settling tail relative to [[2508.00355|TOP]]'s speed cap — energy is dissipated more slowly through allowed deviation, not removed.
   - *Test*: report the full CoM-excursion trajectory, not just the peak, for the impedance-allocation policy vs TOP's speed-cap baseline at matched load; compare settling time to within a fixed tolerance band.
   - *Row*: TOP (reference modulation) / GentleHumanoid (externally commanded).
   - *Falsifier*: impedance allocation dominates on both peak excursion and settling time → it is a strict Pareto improvement over the speed cap in the tested regime, not merely a different instrument on the same axis, and the physics caveat is empirically moot there.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Softening does not shrink the commanded coupling term** — impedance changes how far a joint deviates under load, not the commanded $M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$ itself, so a naive read could oversell compliance as removing the disturbance rather than redistributing it. → H6 reports the full excursion trajectory, not just the peak, and the FP bet is stated as a different instrument on the same axis, not a free-lunch dominance claim; let H1/H6 falsify or confirm dominance empirically.
> - **A certified safety layer built for fixed gains may not cover a variable-impedance policy** — [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]], [[2604.07457|CMP]], and the rest of C2's safe sets are derived against a dynamics model that presumes a fixed gain profile; deploying a variable-impedance policy under an un-re-derived certificate risks silently violating the guarantee it claims. → H4 tests whether existing margins already have enough slack before committing to a full re-derivation; treat the certificate as unvalidated for a variable-impedance policy until H4 clears.
> - **Reusing this doc's exogenous-magnitude stratification silently mixes two different things** — A1's H2, C1's H1/H2, and several of C2's hypotheses stratify tests by arm-acceleration or load-magnitude bins, treating coupling magnitude as a property of the disturbance alone. If compliance allocation is real, magnitude is partly endogenous — the policy chose to let it stay large or made it small — so those bins conflate "the disturbance was large" with "the policy chose to let it stay large." → Any comparison across this doc that stratifies by coupling/disturbance magnitude and wants to combine with C3 must add a compliance-setting covariate to the bin definition, not assume magnitude is purely exogenous.
> - **The only existing precedent never crosses the leg-only boundary** — Variable-Stiffness-Quadruped (arXiv:2502.09436) has no arm, no hand, and never touches an object; the asymmetric leg-stiffening behavior might not transfer to a hand-applied wrench propagating through an arm at all. → H2 tests the transfer directly before committing further resources to the humanoid instantiation; if it fails, the direction needs a different mechanism, not a bigger backbone.
> - **No benchmark reports compliance as a controlled condition** — every force-adaptive suite in this doc (including [[2308.14636|Linear-Impactor]] and [[2404.19173|Single-Contact++-RL]]) treats stiffness as a fixed property of the platform, not a swept independent variable, so H1/H6's Pareto sweep has no standardized protocol to run on yet. → Reuse Linear-Impactor's / Single-Contact++-RL's step-impulse + recovery-success machinery as the substrate and add a compliance-setting covariate (the new Benchmark Gaps row below), rather than authoring a protocol from scratch.

---

## Cluster D — Whole-Body Teleoperation & Human-Motion Retargeting

*The whole-body data wall — demonstrations of coupled loco-manipulation are scarce, embodiment-mismatched, and per-platform. The three directions remove three different bricks from that wall, each a distinct verb on a distinct object: retarget the human motion that exists so its contact survives the morphology gap, or capture new coupled demos directly with a human still in the per-demo loop (both D1), transfer a trained policy onto a new body without retraining (D2), and synthesize coupled demos with the human removed from the per-demo loop entirely (D3). The sharpest line is D1 vs D3 — whether a person performs (or is retargeted from) each demonstration or a generator authors it (synthesis).*

### D1 — Interaction-Preserving Whole-Body Retargeting

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | D — Whole-Body Teleoperation & Human-Motion Retargeting |
| **Thesis** | Retarget human motion to a humanoid by preserving the *interaction* — object contact, scene constraints, foot–ground contact — as a hard objective, instead of minimizing joint-space pose error. The reason it must work: a loco-manipulation reference's value is in the contact relationships (hand-on-object, foot-on-floor), not the joint angles, so a pose-error-minimizing retarget can be joint-accurate yet contact-broken. The field assumes retargeting is kinematic pose matching. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2509.26633\|OmniRetarget]] (method), [[2606.03476\|Human2Humanoid]] (method), [[2603.22201\|NMR]] (method), [[2412.17730\|Mimicking-Bench]] (benchmark), [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2509.26633\|OmniRetarget]] downstream-RL 82.20–94.73% with near-zero penetration (0.00–0.01) + zero foot-skating (0 duration, 0 max-velocity), interaction-preserving (object + scene), 30 s parkour zero-shot G1 from 5 rewards; [[2606.03476\|Human2Humanoid]] 88.5% SR / 0.12 tracking-error / 0.05 cm penetration / 4.7% foot-skating via a morphology-invariant end-effector consistency loss; [[2603.22201\|NMR]] 54% self-collision reduction (0.87% frames) + zero joint jumps; [[2510.02252\|GMR]] the shared-suite retargeting→downstream-tracking protocol — compares 4 retargeters (PHC / ProtoMotions / Unitree closed-source / GMR) on a shared LAFAN1 subset under one fixed BeyondMimic training pipeline, isolating retargeting quality from training (GMR matches the closed-source baseline and holds tracking on dynamic 'Dance 1'/'Run (stop & go)' sequences where others hit 0% SR); [[2606.16272\|TopoRetarget]] 87.5% downstream RL with 7.71 mm object-relative displacement and zero-shot sim2real (hand-object contact-topology preservation, the H4 dexterous-contact face) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: loco-manipulation references come from human motion, but a human's loco-manipulation is defined by its *interactions* — hand on object, foot on floor, body against scene — and a retargeter that minimizes joint-space pose error can be joint-accurate yet contact-broken: the hand floats off the object, the foot skates, the reference penetrates the ground, and the policy inherits the violation.
- **Today's answers**: interaction-preservation is now *consensus*. [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]] preserves the interaction via an interaction-mesh Laplacian objective (penetration 0.00–0.01, zero foot-skating, 82.20–94.73% downstream RL); [[2606.03476|Human2Humanoid]] adds a *morphology-invariant end-effector consistency loss* (88.5% SR, 0.05 cm penetration); [[2603.22201|NMR]] adds feasibility-by-distribution (−54% self-collisions). And [[2602.15733|MeshMimic]] *extends* the OmniRetarget recipe past flat ground to large-scale terrain (−25.5% world MPJPE, −18.7% Chamfer vs VideoMimic), with [[2604.18557|SynAgent]] (multi-agent contact, 45% vs 7.26% InterMimic), [[2601.09518|Human-Humanoid-Interaction]] (PAIR contact-F1 0.841) and [[2602.06827|DynaRetarget]] (dynamically-feasible retarget, 97.09% downstream vs 79.41% raw) as PARTIAL follow-ons on the same mechanism. The interaction-preservation half is owned; what no one isolates is *which* contact relationship is load-bearing, or whether contact-fidelity *predicts* trainability monotonically rather than correlating.
- **The opening**: OmniRetarget's recipe ties reference quality to trainability, but two systems that reach overlapping contact failure modes through *different mechanisms* have never been trained as one loss: [[2603.22201|NMR]]'s learned-feasibility manifold (self-collision, joint-jumps, plus a fixed-corpus data-curation gate on gross foot-ground defects) and [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]]'s per-query interaction-mesh objective (broken contact, re-solved fresh for every new scene). Stacking them into a single composite retargeting loss is the surviving, unclaimed *mechanism* — neither paper, nor the follow-ons (MeshMimic, SynAgent, DynaRetarget), trains both terms jointly. The quantitative grading of any retargeter on this axis — the cross-method penetration/foot-skating → downstream-SR regression (slope + R²) — is a supporting measurement (H2), not the invention itself. (D1 owns both; D3 measures a parallel feasibility→SR slope on *synthesized*, not retargeted, data — the two cards don't overlap.)

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: The information in a loco-manipulation reference is the contact graph — which surfaces touch (hand–object, foot–floor, body–scene) and with what relative geometry — not the absolute joint angles, which are a morphology-specific realization. A pose-error-minimizing retarget optimizes the wrong quantity: it can match joints while breaking the contact that defines the task. [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]] demonstrates the structure is load-bearing — its interaction-mesh objective preserves contact where Laplacian-blind baselines foot-skate and penetrate, and that contact fidelity is what enables the 82.20–94.73% downstream RL from a minimal reward set.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "retargeting is pose matching" (interaction-preservation is now consensus — [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]], [[2606.03476|Human2Humanoid]], [[2602.15733|MeshMimic]] all preserve it), and not a clean split of failure modes either — [[2603.22201|NMR]]'s own CEPR pipeline already checks foot-ground contact: Step 1 discards source human motions with "insufficient foot-ground contact (floating or penetration)," and Step 2's "Floating Foot Rectification" discards kinematically-retargeted robot motions on the same defect, alongside a self-intersection reject threshold (>0.05 frames). So the orthogonality the original framing claimed — "NMR closes joint-jumps, OmniRetarget closes broken contact" — overstates the split; both systems touch foot-ground contact and self-collision. The real, defensible distinction is *mechanism*, not failure-mode ownership: NMR's contact handling is a discard-then-learn *gate* applied once to a fixed, morphology-specific training corpus — its Transformer's own L1 regression loss carries no explicit interaction term, so at inference on a *novel* motion, contact competence is whatever generalized statistically from the CEPR-clustered training set (the paper's own limitation: CEPR must be regenerated per morphology, and by extension per new object/scene distribution). [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]]'s interaction-mesh objective is a *per-query* SQP-solved hard constraint (`φ_j(q_t) ≥ 0` for every potential collision pair, foot-contact stability enforced per stance frame), re-solved fresh for each new demonstration and each augmented object/terrain with no retraining. Nobody has combined NMR's learned, smooth, joint-jump-free manifold with OmniRetarget's per-query hard-constraint guarantee into one pipeline, so whether the composite's failure modes are complementary (each closes what the other's *mechanism* structurally can't) or redundant (one already dominates) is untested.
- **The bet**: Stack [[2603.22201|NMR]]'s learned-feasibility-manifold regularizer *under* [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]]'s interaction-mesh Laplacian objective as one joint retargeting loss — a composite objective no system currently trains. The causal case for the composite pushing self-collision *below* NMR's solo 0.87% (not merely holding parity with it): NMR's residual self-collision concentrates where its Transformer generalizes off the CEPR-clustered training manifold (CEPR's VAE+K-Means clustering fits per-cluster-refined targets, so a query motion unlike any cluster is exactly where the network's statistical mapping degrades into self-intersection) — a *learned-generalization-gap* failure mode. OmniRetarget's `φ_j(q_t) ≥ 0` constraint is solved fresh per query by SQP, not learned from a training distribution, so it has no generalization-gap failure mode of that kind: the bound holds whether the query resembles training data or not. A composite that enforces OmniRetarget's per-query hard constraint on top of NMR's learned manifold should close specifically the out-of-distribution violations NMR's statistical mapping leaves — while NMR contributes what OmniRetarget's own mechanism doesn't supply: joint-space smoothness learned from physics-refined rollouts (zero joint jumps), versus OmniRetarget's only smoothness term, a soft `||q_t − q_{t-1}||²` penalty with no learned-manifold prior. Caveat: neither KH note reports OmniRetarget's own self-collision rate on a shared benchmark, so "below 0.87%" is a mechanism-level prediction, not a number extrapolated from a reported head-to-head — closing that missing measurement is exactly H5's job. Falsifiable: if OmniRetarget's constraint alone (no NMR manifold) already reaches ≤0.87% self-collision on the same benchmark, or an unweighted sum of the two losses reaches the same frontier with no cross-term, the mechanisms are redundant and stacking buys nothing beyond either paper's own number.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *what relationship the retarget preserves across the morphology gap* (full scene+object interaction / kinematics-only pose / morphology-invariant contact / feasibility-by-learned-distribution / physics-fidelity contact) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | What the retarget preserves | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2509.26633\|OmniRetarget]] | full agent–object–scene interaction via an interaction-mesh Laplacian objective | 82.20–94.73% downstream RL, penetration 0.00–0.01, zero foot-skating, 30 s parkour zero-shot G1 (5 rewards) | the interaction mesh needs the contact relationships specified; which relationship is load-bearing is never isolated |
| [[2606.03476\|Human2Humanoid]] | morphology-invariant end-effector contact via a consistency loss + physics constraints | 88.5% SR, 0.05 cm penetration, 4.7% foot-skating vs PHC/GMR/Unitree-Retarget | end-effector contact only — scene/body contact (climbing, leaning) not in the objective (D2's morphology-invariant face) |
| [[2603.22201\|NMR]] | feasibility-by-distribution: learns the feasible-motion manifold rather than optimizing per-frame IK | 54% self-collision reduction (0.87% frames), zero joint jumps, faster downstream convergence | CEPR's data curation *discards* motions with gross foot-ground/self-intersection defects, but that's a fixed-corpus accept/reject gate, not a per-query preservation objective — the trained network's own loss carries no explicit interaction term, so contact quality on a novel scene is whatever generalized statistically from training |
| [[2602.15733\|MeshMimic]] | interaction-mesh preservation *extended to large-scale terrain* (the OmniRetarget recipe past flat ground) | −25.5% world MPJPE, −18.7% Chamfer vs VideoMimic; higher real terrain-interaction SR | extends the consensus recipe to terrain but never isolates *which* contact relationship is load-bearing nor regresses fidelity → trainability (the surviving D1 core) |
| [[2604.18557\|SynAgent]] | multi-agent interaction-graph contact preservation + data refinement | 45.00% multi-agent SR vs 7.26% InterMimic; 19.16% cooperative transport vs CooHOI 7.28% | preserves *agent-agent* contact for cooperation — a contact face, but the fidelity→trainability predictor is untested |
| [[2601.09518\|Human-Humanoid-Interaction]] | contact-preserving human-humanoid interaction retarget (PAIR) + D-STAR policy | contact-F1 0.841 (+67.5% over SOTA), JPE 0.174 m; 75.4% across 6 interaction tasks, real G1 | optimizes contact for human-robot interaction; contact fidelity is reported but never tied to a monotone downstream-SR curve |
| [[2602.06827\|DynaRetarget]] | *dynamically-feasible* trajectory optimization (SBTO) preserving contact | 76.8% feasible-trajectory generation vs SPIDER 37.9%; 97.09% downstream tracking vs 79.41% raw kinematic | adds dynamic feasibility on top of contact, and reports a feasible→downstream gain — but doesn't regress the contact-fidelity → SR slope across methods (the D1 predictor) |
| [[2603.03279\|ULTRA]] | physics-driven contact fidelity (lowest foot-skating + minimal penetration) feeding a masked controller | 73% dense / 50–90% sparse real G1, +200% OOD from RL finetune | optimizes physics-fidelity for *tracking*, not interaction-preservation for arbitrary scene contact (feeds A3) |
| [[2604.00202\|DreamControl-v2]] | pre-retargeting feasibility: a diffusion prior in robot action space before any reference exists | 68% valid trajectories vs 8% inference-time, 0.925 vs 0.101 downstream RL | a feasibility *prior*, not a retarget of a given human motion — complements but does not preserve the human interaction (feeds A2) |
| [[2605.06593\|ReActor]] | physics-aware contact via an RL retargeting loop enforcing zero penetration | zero penetration, 97.45% downstream RL | an RL loop per reference, costlier than OmniRetarget's optimization; scene-interaction breadth untested (cross-ref [[Locomotion\|Locomotion]] A2) |
| [[2606.01851\|PHASOR]] | a phase-anchored universal representation the retarget maps into, embodiment-agnostic by construction | 1.62 mm next-frame, 90.3% R@1 cross-embodiment retrieval | a representation, not a contact objective — preserves *motion semantics* across bodies, not *interaction* with the scene (feeds D2) |
| [[2511.09484\|SPIDER]] | physics-informed contact at scale across hands + bodies (the scaled retargeting engine) | 2.4 M frames / 800 h across 5 hands + 4 humanoids, up to 100% SR | scales dexterous retargeting but the *whole-body scene*-interaction preservation OmniRetarget targets is not its objective (feeds D3) |
| [[2412.17730\|Mimicking-Bench]] | benchmark — measures humanoid-*scene* interaction quality via human mimicking | 54% best-combination SR over 6 household tasks, ~log-linear scaling, 20K synthetic + 3K real / 11K objects | scores scene-interaction fidelity on 6 tasks but supplies no retargeter — the scene-interaction yardstick D1 is measured against |
| [[2505.12748\|TeleOpBench]] | benchmark — standardizes how the source human motion is captured before any retarget | 30 bimanual tasks × 4 modalities × 3 humanoids, sim-real correlation | evaluates the *capture* interface upstream of retargeting; supplies no preservation objective — the source-quality substrate D1 consumes for both retargeting and the capture-isolation test (H7) |
| [[2606.16272\|TopoRetarget]] | local *hand–object* interaction topology via a Delaunay interaction-mesh + topology-aware Laplacian optimization (the dexterous-contact face of OmniRetarget's recipe) | 87.5% downstream RL on contact-rich pen-spinning (+40 pp over baselines), lowest object-relative displacement 7.71 mm / 15.67° orientation, zero-shot sim2real to Wuji Hand | preserves the *hand–object* contact face that OmniRetarget's body–scene and Human2Humanoid's end-effector terms don't isolate (D1's 'which contact term is load-bearing' H4) — but, like them, never regresses contact-fidelity → SR across methods |
| [[2603.09956\|Kinodynamic-Retargeting]] | multi-contact *dynamic* fidelity via whole-body trajectory optimization fed with human ground-reaction-force data — recovers heel-toe contact-event timing + GRF magnitude | eliminated foot-floating/penetration with strict adherence to contact windows; faster policy convergence + higher early episode reward in downstream IL vs mass-rescaled human data | adds a GRF/dynamics fidelity lever beyond DynaRetarget's pure SBTO and ULTRA's physics-fidelity, but reports the trainability gain only qualitatively (no downstream SR) — so it broadens H2's contact-fidelity spread without supplying a point on the fidelity → SR regression |
| [[2509.15443\|Implicit-Kinodynamic-Retargeting]] | dynamic feasibility via a *learned* two-stage neural retarget (kinematics-aware pretrain → dynamics-aware decoder fine-tune), avoiding physically-impossible motion | 5000 fps (~100× faster than numerical optimization), lower acceleration 29.15 rad/s² / jerk 435.37 rad/s³ than pretrained, real G1 deployment robust to source-motion noise | the cheap learning-based sibling of DynaRetarget — directly answers the 'RL-in-the-loop retargeter is costly' risk that ReActor raises — but learns dynamic feasibility without an explicit *interaction* term, so contact is implicit in the data, not preserved as an objective |
| [[2510.04353\|Stability-Aware]] | preserves whole-body multi-contact *stability* (an actuation-aware 2D centroidal stability margin) rather than pose error — the foot/body-against-scene balance face of interaction-preservation | +**27%** sim / +**7%** hardware stability-margin gain; stability-margin → impulse-resilience correlation **R=0.69**; real-time 79 µs region update | sits between OmniRetarget's scene-interaction term and ULTRA's physics-fidelity (the stability face) — its margin→robustness correlation is the closest existing *fidelity→outcome regression precedent* feeding H2's methodology, but it regresses margin→resilience, not the cross-method contact-fidelity→SR slope the bet designs against |
| [[2602.01632\|Closed-Form-Geometric-Retargeting-Solver]] | preserves limb-*orientation* alignment (a kinematic/pose quantity, not contact) via a closed-form geometric solver | ~**3×** higher downstream policy SR from retargeting fidelity; **0.587 ms** median inference; self-collision cut ~50%→1.3% with the safety filter | the pose-matching comparator H1 explicitly needs — a non-contact fidelity lever (orientation accuracy vs contact accuracy) the contact graph must *beat* in H1's falsifier, filling the kinematics-only-pose row with a strong recent baseline (SEW-Mimic), but it is the lever the bet sharpens against, not a solver of it |
| [[2508.00362\|Whole-Body-Motion-Imitation-Framework]] | a GNN retargeter with an explicit *foot-contact* objective feeding nonlinear centroidal MPC | **100%** SR with CoM RMSE **0.0170 m** vs 0.0400 m (LMPC+WBC) on full-size hardware (Wave/Yoga/Stride) | a DynaRetarget / Kinodynamic-Retargeting sibling on the "preserve foot-floor contact as an objective" face — broadens the method spread the contact-fidelity→SR regression (H2) is run across without duplicating an existing row's mechanism |
| [[2602.04851\|PDF-HR]] | a learned pose-plausibility *distance field* used as a differentiable retargeting regularizer (the feasibility-by-distribution face) | improves retargeting deployment SR **9/10** vs 6/10 sequences; higher RL sample efficiency, mastering skills baselines fail within 800 M samples | the differentiable plug-in-prior lever [[2603.22201\|NMR]] (manifold learning) and the table's feasibility row don't supply — the regularizer form H5 stacks *under* OmniRetarget's interaction objective, an NMR sibling on the differentiable-prior face |
| [[2606.29940\|WARP]] | whole-body *mobile-manipulation* retargeting preserving implicit base–arm coordination + *arbitrary-link* contact (not just end-effector) from offline human demos | palm position error cut **>150×** with lowest joint-limit-violation + self-collision rates; **+12%** downstream policy SR over optimization-based retargeting; real manipulation with arbitrary links (elbow push) | a whole-body *mobile* (base–arm-coordination-preserving) retarget reporting a retarget-quality → downstream-SR gain, feeding H2's contact-fidelity → trainability spread from the mobile-manip side that [[2509.26633\|OmniRetarget]]'s fixed-base scene interaction and [[2602.06827\|DynaRetarget]]'s SBTO leave open, yet, like them, never regresses the fidelity → SR slope/R² across methods (the D1 predictor) |
| [[2603.02856\|Rhythm]] | *robot–robot* interaction topology (not human–scene or human–robot) via decoupled Individual + Unified manifolds: an Interaction-Mesh Laplacian objective that resolves the kinematic conflict between preserving each robot's own self-motion and preserving the inter-agent contact geometry | **0%** inter-penetration, F1-Strict **0.932** (Intensive Contact) vs baseline OR **47.3%** IPR; downstream utility DSR **78.3%**; real dual-Unitree-G1 Hug **86.7%** SR vs single-agent-retarget baseline **26.7%**, robust to external pushes/kicks | the multi-agent contact axis [[2604.18557\|SynAgent]] (interaction-graph) and [[2601.09518\|Human-Humanoid-Interaction]] (human-robot PAIR) leave for *robot–robot* physical coupling — first real-hardware dual-humanoid deployment on the table, but preserves inter-agent contact topology, not the human–object–scene term OmniRetarget's fidelity→SR regression (H2) is designed against |
| [[2509.04443\|EMMA]] | bypasses retargeting entirely: trains directly on captured human motion (Project Aria head pose → synchronized base + hand pose) with no explicit retarget-and-preserve step — the coupling is inherent in the capture, not enforced by a retargeting objective | **82%** vs **52%** Handover-Wine (+30 pp) replacing 1 hr teleop with 1 hr human data on a wheeled mobile-manipulator; **54%** generalization in unseen scenes where Mobile ALOHA fully fails; **36%→82%** as human data scales 15→60 min | tests whether retargeting is even necessary, not which retarget preserves what — and demonstrated only on a *wheeled* base, so whether an explicit interaction-preserving retarget still beats direct-capture-and-train on a *legged* platform, where balance coupling exists to isolate, is the open contribution D1 now owns (H7) |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (interaction-preservation beats pose-matching, and contact-fidelity predicts trainability), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Interaction-preservation beats pose-matching downstream at equal joint accuracy.**
   - *Prediction*: retargeting the *same* human motions with [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]]'s interaction objective vs a pose-error-minimizing baseline of *matched* joint accuracy, the interaction-preserving references reach 82–94% downstream RL while the pose-matched ones drop on tasks where the broken contact matters (object transport, climbing).
   - *Test*: hold joint-tracking error fixed, vary only the interaction term; report downstream RL SR per task class.
   - *Row*: OmniRetarget (full interaction) vs NMR (feasibility-by-distribution, no interaction term).
   - *Falsifier*: pose-matching at equal joint accuracy reaches the same downstream SR → the contact graph is not the load-bearing quantity.
2. **H2 — Foot-skating / penetration predicts downstream SR monotonically (slope + R²).**
   - *Prediction*: across retargeting methods ([[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]], [[2606.03476|Human2Humanoid]], [[2603.22201|NMR]], [[2602.15733|MeshMimic]], [[2602.06827|DynaRetarget]], [[2603.03279|ULTRA]], [[2605.06593|ReActor]]), the penetration/foot-skating → downstream-RL-SR curve is monotone with a measurable slope and R² — lower contact violation predicts higher SR — so contact-fidelity is a *quantitative* trainability predictor, not a correlate.
   - *Test*: regress downstream RL SR on penetration + foot-skating across methods on [[2510.02252|GMR]]'s shared-motion-set / fixed-downstream-pipeline protocol (the controlled retarget→tracking comparison), controlling for intrinsic motion hardness with [[2512.07248|MDS]]'s Motion Difficulty Score (Pearson r up to 0.6478, Spearman ρ up to 0.8203 with MPJPE-G) and Difficulty-Stratified Joint Error so the fidelity→SR slope is unconfounded; report the slope, R², and monotonicity.
   - *Row*: OmniRetarget / Human2Humanoid / NMR / MeshMimic / DynaRetarget / ULTRA / ReActor (the contact-fidelity spread); GMR (shared-suite protocol) / MDS (difficulty covariate).
   - *Falsifier*: SR is flat or non-monotone in contact violation → fidelity does not predict trainability and the metric is decorative.
3. **H3 — The morphology-invariant end-effector loss is the mechanism, and it transfers across bodies.**
   - *Prediction*: isolating [[2606.03476|Human2Humanoid]]'s end-effector consistency loss delivers the 88.5% SR / 0.05 cm penetration, and the same loss carries the contact-preservation gain to a second humanoid morphology with no re-tuning.
   - *Test*: ablate the consistency loss on the home platform + transfer it to a different humanoid; report SR + penetration delta.
   - *Row*: Human2Humanoid (morphology-invariant contact).
   - *Falsifier*: removing the loss leaves contact fidelity intact, or it fails to transfer → end-effector consistency is not the mechanism.
4. **H4 — Scene-contact preservation is needed beyond object-contact for body-against-scene tasks.**
   - *Prediction*: on tasks where the body contacts the scene (climbing onto a platform while carrying a load), preserving body–scene contact (OmniRetarget's scene term) beats preserving only hand–object contact (Human2Humanoid's end-effector term), with the gap near-zero on tabletop object tasks.
   - *Test*: object-contact-only vs object+scene-contact retargets on [[2412.17730|Mimicking-Bench]]'s scene-interaction tasks vs tabletop tasks; report SR per task type.
   - *Row*: OmniRetarget (full scene interaction) vs Human2Humanoid (end-effector only).
   - *Falsifier*: object-contact-only matches full scene interaction on climbing/leaning → scene contact is not a separate lever.
5. **H5 — Feasibility-by-distribution and interaction-preservation are complementary, not redundant.**
   - *Prediction*: stacking [[2603.22201|NMR]]'s learned-feasibility manifold under [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]]'s interaction objective cuts self-collisions below NMR's 0.87% *and* holds OmniRetarget's contact fidelity, because NMR's residual violations are a learned-generalization-gap failure mode (concentrated off the CEPR-clustered training manifold) that OmniRetarget's per-query hard constraint — solved fresh regardless of training coverage — is structurally positioned to close; first measure OmniRetarget's own self-collision rate alone (currently unreported), since the composite must beat that number too, not just NMR's 0.87%.
   - *Test*: interaction-only vs feasibility-only vs both, on a shared motion set; report self-collision rate (split in-cluster vs out-of-cluster relative to NMR's CEPR training clusters), foot-skating, and downstream SR.
   - *Row*: NMR (feasibility) + OmniRetarget (interaction).
   - *Falsifier*: OmniRetarget alone already matches or beats the combination's self-collision rate, or an unweighted sum of both losses reaches the same frontier with no cross-term → the mechanisms are redundant.
6. **H6 — A diffusion feasibility prior reduces the human-reference budget the retargeter needs.**
   - *Prediction*: seeding the retargeter with [[2604.00202|DreamControl-v2]]'s pre-retargeting feasibility prior (68% vs 8% valid trajectories) lets interaction-preserving retargeting hit the 82–94% downstream band from fewer human references, because the prior pre-filters infeasible targets the interaction objective would otherwise have to repair.
   - *Test*: sweep human-reference count with vs without the feasibility prior; report downstream SR vs reference budget.
   - *Row*: DreamControl-v2 (pre-retargeting prior) + OmniRetarget (interaction).
   - *Falsifier*: the prior does not reduce the reference budget → feasibility-priming and interaction-preservation do not compound.
7. **H7 — Whole-body-captured demos beat fixed-base demos plus a controller on the balance-critical stratum of a *legged* platform (porting EMMA's wheeled isolation).**
