   - *Prediction*: on a bipedal humanoid, a policy trained on whole-body-captured demos ([[2401.02117|Mobile-ALOHA]] teleop, or [[2602.06643|HMI]] / [[2510.03022|HumanoidExo]] robot-free capture) beats one trained on fixed-base arm demos plus a separate locomotion controller, with the margin concentrated on tasks where arm motion perturbs gait and near-zero on fixed-base tasks — the controlled subtraction [[2509.04443|EMMA]] ran on wheels (+30 pp Handover-Wine, replacing 1 hr teleop with 1 hr human data) but no system paper runs on legs, where balance coupling exists to isolate.
   - *Test*: same task suite, two data sources (whole-body-captured vs fixed-base + controller) at matched demo count on a legged humanoid; stratify SR by balance-criticality on [[2407.07788|BiGym]]'s 40 mobile-bimanual tasks, whose whole-body-vs-bimanual action-mode switch is the coupling-present-vs-absent knob.
   - *Row*: EMMA (bypasses retargeting) vs Mobile ALOHA / HMI / HumanoidExo (whole-body capture) vs a fixed-base + controller baseline.
   - *Falsifier*: the margin on the balance-critical stratum is ~0 or reverses, or fixed-base + controller matches whole-body-captured demos there → EMMA's wheeled result is the ceiling, not a floor, and simultaneous capture buys nothing extra on legs.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Interaction preservation needs the contact graph specified** — the interaction-mesh objective requires knowing which surfaces touch. → [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]] infers contact from human–object–scene data; H1/H2 report retargeting quality vs contact-annotation quality, treating annotation as a measured dependency.
> - **Morphology gap can exceed what any retarget can bridge** — a very different humanoid may have no feasible interaction-preserving retarget. → [[2606.03476|Human2Humanoid]]'s physics-aware constraints bound infeasible targets; H3 reports the morphology range the consistency loss covers (couples to D2's transfer limit).
> - **Contact-fidelity metrics need not equal task success** — zero foot-skating does not guarantee task completion. → H2 ties contact-fidelity to downstream RL SR as a measured curve; report the trainability regression, not penetration/skating alone.
> - **An RL-in-the-loop retargeter is costly** — per-reference RL ([[2605.06593|ReActor]]) does not scale to large corpora. → Prefer optimization-based interaction-preservation (OmniRetarget) where the corpus is large; report retargeting cost-per-reference alongside fidelity.
> - **The capture isolation may not transfer to legs — EMMA's wheeled result may be the ceiling, not a floor** — a wheeled base has no balance coupling to isolate, so its +30 pp margin may not replicate where gait stability is at stake. → H7 runs the controlled subtraction on a bipedal platform directly; if the margin collapses on the balance-critical stratum, whole-body capture buys nothing beyond what fixed-base demos plus a controller already deliver on legs.

### D2 — Cross-Embodiment Whole-Body Policy Transfer

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | D — Whole-Body Teleoperation & Human-Motion Retargeting |
| **Thesis** | Transfer a whole-body loco-manipulation policy across humanoid morphologies via a phase-anchored or kinematically-aligned shared representation, instead of retraining a per-platform policy from scratch. The reason it must work: whole-body coordination has a morphology-invariant *structure* (phase-clocked balance + end-effector goals) separable from each body's joint realization. The field assumes every new humanoid needs its own whole-body-tracking policy. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2605.23733\|Any2Any]] (method), [[2606.01851\|PHASOR]] (method), [[2606.03985\|Humanoid-GPT]] (method), [[2606.03476\|Human2Humanoid]] (method), [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (benchmark), [[2606.16696\|VENOM]] (method), [[2506.09366\|SkillBlender]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2605.23733\|Any2Any]] adapts pretrained whole-body-tracking across LimX Oli / LimX Luna / G1 / H1 using ~1% of from-scratch compute + data, matching/beating specialists, LoRA into the dynamics-sensitive actor modules; [[2606.01851\|PHASOR]] 90.3% R@1 + 93.9% MRR human→robot retrieval, 1.62 mm next-frame, only method beating raw-kinematics teleop (64.75 mm); [[2606.03985\|Humanoid-GPT]] 92.58% tracking SR / 40.99 mm MPKPE (2 B frames), <1.5 ms inference; [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] the only standardized morphology-distance protocol — 18 morphologies (8 procedural + 10 real) with explicit interpolation / extrapolation / composition splits and defined train/test (ME agents drop to 0% on `Arms-push` extrapolation; symlog + discrete actions buy 4× multi-task / 2× zero-shot), but manipulation-only reach/push, no bipedal whole-body loco-manip body |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: whole-body-tracking policies are trained per-platform — a new humanoid (LimX Oli, LimX Luna, G1, H1) restarts training from scratch, paying the full large-scale data + compute bill each time, even though the *coordination* it learns is largely the same.
- **Today's answers**: the space has densely filled, splitting into *cheap transfer* and *generalist scale*. Cheap transfer: [[2605.23733|Any2Any]] PEFTs the dynamics-sensitive modules across 4 humanoids at ~1% compute; [[2606.01851|PHASOR]] makes motion's *periodicity* the invariant (90.3% R@1, only method beating raw-kinematics teleop at 64.75 mm); [[2604.19734|UniT]] / [[2511.07820|SONIC]] use shared token spaces; [[2601.15419|Unified-Latent-Cross-Embodiment]] integrates a new robot in ~15 min (sub-cm reach); [[2602.13764|MOTIF]] does 1-shot/5-shot transfer (67.5% real). But [[2512.00971|H-Zero]] executes the transfer-not-from-scratch thesis (81% on unseen robots, ~30 min few-shot) *only on locomotion*. Generalist scale (the opposite of cheap transfer): [[2602.05791|XHugWBC]] (100% survival on 19 sim + 7 real, ~85% of specialists, one policy) and [[2602.02960|Embodiment-Aware-Distillation]] (EAGLE, zero-shot across 5 humanoids) train *one* generalist; [[2505.05753|Embodiment-Scaling-Laws]] (817 embodiments) and [[2509.02815|URMAv2]] (50 robots) show scale buys generalization. Two camps, never run head-to-head on *whole-body loco-manipulation*.
- **The opening**: the two front-line gaps the literature leaves are (i) *whole-body loco-MANIPULATION* — H-Zero and the scaling-laws line stop at locomotion, exactly where D2's novelty lives — and (ii) the *transfer-vs-generalist-scale head-to-head* (cheap localized PEFT vs a one-time-trained XHugWBC/EAGLE generalist), now the thickest tension in the field. Any2Any's ablation localizes the lever (PEFT into the dynamics-sensitive pathway), so the structure is a specific pathway, not a coincidence of scale.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: Whole-body coordination decomposes into a morphology-invariant structure (a phase-clocked balance schedule + end-effector goals any humanoid shares) and a body-specific joint realization. The structure is the transferable invariant; the joint angles are the per-body projection. A representation anchored to the structure (phase, shared tokens, end-effector) transfers where a joint-space policy cannot. [[2606.01851|PHASOR]] demonstrates it directly — the phase manifold is "intrinsic to the behavior rather than to the body that produces it," and anchoring multiple robots to a shared human-pretrained manifold yields cross-embodiment retrieval a joint-space encoder cannot.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "per-platform retraining is avoidable" (consensus on *locomotion* — [[2512.00971|H-Zero]], [[2602.05791|XHugWBC]], [[2505.05753|Embodiment-Scaling-Laws]] all show it) but two sharper claims: that the result holds for *whole-body loco-MANIPULATION* (where H-Zero and the scaling line stop at locomotion), and that *cheap localized transfer* beats *one-time generalist scale* below a crossover — the live tension between [[2605.23733|Any2Any]]-style PEFT and [[2602.05791|XHugWBC]]/[[2602.02960|Embodiment-Aware-Distillation]] generalists that no one has resolved on manipulation-coupled tasks.
- **The bet**: On *whole-body loco-MANIPULATION* (not locomotion), a cross-embodiment adapter transfers a pretrained policy at [[2605.23733|Any2Any]]'s ~1% of from-scratch compute/data across 4 humanoids via a [[2606.01851|PHASOR]]-class phase-anchored representation (90.3% R@1, 1.62 mm next-frame), and — the promoted front-line falsifier — *beats a one-time-trained generalist* ([[2602.05791|XHugWBC]] ~85% of specialists / [[2602.02960|Embodiment-Aware-Distillation]]) on cost below a measurable crossover, with the transferable structure *localized* to the dynamics-sensitive pathway. Falsifiable: if joint-space transfer at equal budget matches the phase-anchored representation, or a one-time generalist beats cheap transfer at equal cost on loco-manipulation, neither the structure nor the cheap-transfer regime is the lever.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *what invariant the transfer rides on* (kinematic-align + PEFT on the policy / phase manifold / shared discrete token space / morphology-invariant end-effector objective / kinematic-feasibility prior) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | Transfer invariant | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2605.23733\|Any2Any]] | kinematic alignment + PEFT into dynamics-sensitive actor modules (transfers the *policy*) | ~1% compute/data across LimX Oli/Luna/G1/H1, matches/beats specialists, faster convergence | needs a pretrained source tracker; transfers a policy, not a representation — the invariant is implicit in the aligned weights |
| [[2606.01851\|PHASOR]] | FFT-parametric phase manifold + motion-semantic distillation (embodiment-agnostic by design) | 90.3% R@1, 93.9% MRR, 1.62 mm next-frame, only method beating raw-kinematics teleop (64.75 mm) | phase is natural for cyclic locomotion, weaker for non-periodic fine manipulation — the loco-heavy bias |
| [[2604.19734\|UniT]] | shared discrete token space via tri-branch cross-reconstruction (vision anchors kinematics) | 66.7% RoboCasa, 10× data efficiency, 60% zero-shot stacking, t-SNE-verified shared manifold | a discrete codebook can under-utilize; manipulation-centric validation, loco coupling lighter |
| [[2511.07820\|SONIC]] | universal token space from supersized motion tracking (scale *is* the transfer) | 99.6% OOD tracking (100 M frames / 21k GPU-h / 42 M params), 50/50 zero-shot real G1, 95% mobile-manip via GR00T N1.5 | reaches universality by *scaling from scratch*, not cheap transfer — the comparator that asks whether transfer beats scale (Q4) |
| [[2606.03985\|Humanoid-GPT]] | 2 B-frame scaled tracking yielding a zero-shot-transferable policy | 92.58% tracking SR, 40.99 mm MPKPE, <1.5 ms inference, real G1 | scaling-driven zero-shot, no explicit cross-embodiment alignment — transfer is emergent from scale, not engineered |
| [[2512.00971\|H-Zero]] | pretrain-then-few-shot transfer across embodiments (transfer-not-from-scratch) | 81% mean episode length on unseen robots; few-shot adapt in ~30 min vs from-scratch | executes the transfer thesis but only on *locomotion*, via generalist-pretrain not a localized invariant — leaves D2's whole-body loco-manip front line open |
| [[2602.05791\|XHugWBC]] | one generalist whole-body policy, zero-shot across platforms (no transfer step) | 100% survival on 19 sim + 7 real, ~85% of specialists; fine-tune +10% peak | the *generalist-scale* alternative — the opposite of cheap transfer, the promoted head-to-head comparator (D2's front-line falsifier) |
| [[2602.02960\|Embodiment-Aware-Distillation]] | embodiment-aware observation distilling experts into one generalist (EAGLE) | beats generalist PPO + Kickstarting/COMPASS across 5 humanoids; zero-shot sim2real on 4 robots | a one-time-trained generalist, not cheap per-body transfer — the distillation face of the generalist-scale comparator |
| [[2601.15419\|Unified-Latent-Cross-Embodiment]] | shared latent control space with per-robot embedding layers | new robot integrated in ~15 min; sub-cm reach (0.13 cm NAO / 0.44 cm H1); 100 Hz real | a shared-latent few-shot route, but validated on reaching/teleop not whole-body loco-manip coordination — the latent-alignment cousin of PHASOR |
| [[2505.05753\|Embodiment-Scaling-Laws]] | scaling the *number* of training embodiments (817) for zero-shot generalization | up to 5× higher cross-class reward with more embodiments; real Go2 + H1 | proves scale buys generalization — the scaling-laws side of the transfer-vs-scale tension (D2's H5 / front-line comparator) |
| [[2606.03476\|Human2Humanoid]] | morphology-invariant end-effector consistency loss (transfers the *objective*) | 88.5% SR, 0.05 cm penetration vs PHC/GMR/Unitree-Retarget | an objective for retargeting, not a policy/representation transfer — the contact face of morphology-invariance (feeds D1) |
| [[2511.09484\|SPIDER]] | physics-informed retargeting shared across 5 hands + 4 humanoids (the data-side invariant) | 2.4 M frames / 800 h, up to 100% SR | shares a *data engine* across bodies, not a learned transferable policy — the cross-embodiment corpus, not the transfer mechanism (feeds D1) |
| [[2604.07993\|HEX]] | structured proprioception pretrained cross-embodiment | 79.8% ID / 61.8% OOD, cross-embodiment pretraining | transfers the *coupling* representation but is evaluated on one platform's tasks — the cross-embodiment coupling complement (feeds A1) |
| [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] | Kinematic Manifold Prior generalizing across configurations | 12.4 mm world-frame EE error; KMP halves hand error | a kinematic-feasibility prior generalizing across *configurations*, not across *morphologies* — the configuration-invariance complement (feeds A3) |
| [[2509.26633\|OmniRetarget]] | interaction-preserving references that augment from one demo to *different robot embodiments* | 82.20–94.73% downstream RL, augments across embodiments + terrains from a single demo | transfers the *reference* across bodies, not a trained policy — the cross-embodiment reference-generation complement (feeds D1) |
| [[2606.16696\|VENOM]] | shared central transformer trunk + per-embodiment input embeddings & action heads (transfers a shared *body* with per-body interfaces) | RMSE ≈0.42–0.58 rad joint tracking matching RL experts across 5 humanoids (G1/H1/H1v2/Fourier N1/Booster T1, 7705 h), G1 95th-pct error 12.36 cm/s beating MLP, noisy-rollout robustness | pushes cross-embodiment *whole-body tracking past locomotion* (the gap PHASOR/UniT/SONIC leave), but the invariant is a shared trunk learned by distillation, not a phase or kinematic structure — no manipulation-coupling and no transfer-cost-vs-morphology-distance study |
| [[2606.12965\|EmbodiSteer]] | training-free joint-space steering: lifts a pretrained Cartesian policy into the target robot's joint space via Jacobian + CBF whole-body collision guidance (transfers *at inference*, no morphology-specific training) | +28.5% sim SR (to 64.2%) / +36.7% real (47/60 UR5+Franka), −46.1%/−90.0% collisions, real-time 9.61 Hz, zero retraining | the only *zero-training* transfer cell — directly counters the end-effector-only invariant (Human2Humanoid) on whole-body constraints, but steers a Cartesian *manipulation* policy, not a whole-body loco-manip tracker, so it bounds collisions rather than transferring loco-coordination structure |
| [[2512.12230\|Get-Up-Across-Morphologies]] | single morphology-agnostic policy with no morphology IDs (transfer is *implicit in shared training*, like a generalist) | 72% zero-shot to unseen Wolfgang (86% with 4 training morphologies), +61% over specialist on NUGUS; morphological-scaling: zero-shot SR rises with # well-chosen training morphologies, degrades on outlier bodies | supplies the generalist-vs-specialist + morphology-distance datapoint H3/H5 need on *unseen-morphology whole-body recovery* that XHugWBC/EAGLE lack, but it is a fall-recovery skill, not loco-manipulation, and never measures transfer *cost* vs morphology distance |
| [[2602.18025\|Cross-Embodiment-Offline-RL]] | morphology-similarity grouping of policy gradients (Fused-Gromov-Wasserstein Embodiment Grouping over a URMA backbone) — transfers by *clustering bodies to avoid gradient conflict* | +33.99% over standard cross-embodiment IQL on 70% suboptimal data; r=0.815 correlation between per-robot transfer gain and gradient-cosine similarity, identifying gradient conflict as the cause of negative transfer | the only row that supplies the *mechanism* behind H3's transfer cliff (negative transfer = gradient conflict, cut by morphology grouping), but on 16 MuJoCo locomotion platforms — loco-only like H-Zero, no whole-body loco-manip body |
| [[2506.14278\|Heavy-Limbs-WBC]] | not a transfer method — an analytic centroidal-momentum coupling term that makes the *inertial* axis explicit (mass/inertia distribution, not link topology, sets the base-limb coupling strength) | replacing the full centroidal-momentum model (`cH`, 1.2 m/s walking / 60 N disturbance rejection) with an SRBD approximation that discards changing mass/inertia distribution (`cI`) collapses performance to 0.5 m/s / 20 N failure on the *same* kinematic structure | supplies H3's inertial-distance evidence directly, but on one robot — no cross-embodiment sweep. The missing axis AnyBody's kinematic-only splits never test |
| [[2509.23745\|LocoFormer]] | Transformer-XL long-context *in-context* adaptation — transfers at *test time* by retaining memory across trials, no explicit morphology input | 0.96 normalized displacement on 10 unseen robots (vs 0.99 expert, 0.98 few-shot); zero-shot real adaptation to locked limbs / wheel failures / payloads, walks on stilts and on knees after leg removal | adds the in-context test-time-adaptation invariant absent from the table (distinct from every PEFT/phase/codebook/scale row) and extends H3's morphology-distance + damage robustness, but locomotion-only like H-Zero — no whole-body manipulation coupling |
| [[2604.08780\|Hardware-Agnostic-Quadruped-WM]] | morphology-conditioned DreamerV3 world model (Physical Morphology Encoder over USD specs) — transfers the *dynamics model*, not the policy, via explicit morphology conditioning | zero-shot locomotion on unseen real Go1 + ANYmal-D with adaptive gaits, tracking comparable to specialists, zero falls; latent disentangles morphology identity from instantaneous state | the explicit-morphology-conditioning invariant that contrasts Any2Any's implicit-in-weights transfer — a distinct 'what is transferred' cell — but quadruped + world-model-level, not humanoid whole-body loco-manip |
| [[2603.00182\|Morphology-Aware-Transformer]] | explicit kinematic-graph *topology* injected into a VLA (π0.5) as a Mix-Mask attention bias (alternating 1-hop kinematic masking + full attention) + Kinematic Tokens (joint-wise factorized action) + per-joint semantic attributes via FiLM | **47.4%** vs **19.7%** π0.5 single-embodiment (DROID); superior multi-robot Macro-SR across DROID + SO101; G=1 chunk optimal | the explicit-kinematic-topology-bias-injected-into-the-policy cell none of the rows fill — [[2606.16696\|VENOM]] transfers a learned shared trunk (body implicit), [[2604.07993\|HEX]] uses part-level encoders + MoE routing, [[2604.08780\|Hardware-Agnostic-Quadruped-WM]] conditions on morphology at the world-model level; the modern VLA-level instance of the morphology-as-kinematic-tree-token cell and the policy-level cousin of explicit-morphology-conditioning, but evaluated on manipulation arms (DROID/SO101), not whole-body humanoids (the same caveat class as AnyBody / EmbodiSteer / Cross-Embodiment-Offline-RL) |
| [[2412.15166\|Human-Humanoid]] | cross-embodiment whole-body *loco-manipulation* transfer across 5 humanoids via a Unified Digital Human (UDH) embodiment-independent prototype + Decomposed Adversarial IL + human-object interaction graphs | target-body training cut **5.81 h → 0.97 h**; transfers a box-carrying task UDH→NAVIAI/H1; higher normalized returns vs AMP/ASE/PMP | sits in a NEW transfer-invariant cell the table lacks (shared-digital-human prototype as the invariant) — distinct from [[2606.16696\|VENOM]]'s learned shared trunk and [[2605.23733\|Any2Any]]'s PEFT-into-weights, and unlike both it lands on the loco-manip *core* of the bet (not just locomotion or tracking), feeding H2/H3 |
| [[2509.11839\|TrajBooster]] | cross-embodiment whole-body *manipulation* transfer across body *type* (wheeled-humanoid Agibot-World → bipedal G1) riding on a 6D end-effector trajectory as the shared invariant, via Harmonized-Online-DAgger retargeting + GR00T-N1.5 post-pre-training + ~10 min real FT | **2.851 cm** wrist MAE (lowest); PPT+3K-step FT beats non-PPT+10K; **80%** OOD-placement vs **0%** without PPT; zero-shot novel-task transfer | fills the EE-trajectory-as-invariant cell at the data/policy level — complementing [[2606.03476\|Human2Humanoid]]'s EE-consistency *loss* (objective-level) and feeding H4 (the manipulation-transfer half phase under-serves) |
| [[2602.00401\|ZEST]] | zero-shot whole-body *multi-contact skill* transfer across EXTREME morphology distance (Atlas + G1 + Spot quadruped) via a minimal-recipe unified PPO imitation with generic rewards | MAE(q) **0.04–0.08 rad** across diverse dynamic multi-contact skills; beats whole-body MPC on dynamic behaviors with scene-interaction coupling; zero-shot novel skills on all three | supplies the generalist-recipe-across-very-far-bodies datapoint H3/H5 need (an extreme distance gradient *including a cross-class quadruped*), distinct from the humanoid-only generalists [[2602.05791\|XHugWBC]] / [[2602.02960\|Embodiment-Aware-Distillation]] |
| [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] | benchmark — 18 morphologies (8 procedural + 10 real) in Isaac-Sim with explicit interpolation / extrapolation / composition distance splits on reach/push, the only standardized morphology-*distance* protocol in the table | ME-Transformer matches/beats SE on interpolation; **0%** on `Arms-push` extrapolation; symlog + discrete actions buy 4× multi-task / 2× zero-shot | manipulation-only reach/push — no bipedal whole-body loco-manip body — and purely *kinematic* (link-structure/geometric variation only, no mass/inertia/CoM term), so it supplies H3/H6's kinematic-distance axis but none of the inertial axis H3's rank-inversion test needs |
| [[2506.09366\|SkillBlender]] | benchmark (SkillBench, the cross-embodiment suite inside SkillBlender) — 3 real humanoid morphologies (Unitree H1/G1/H1-2) × 4 primitive skills × 8 loco-manip tasks (Easy/Medium/Hard) | SkillBlender outperforms vanilla-RL + hierarchical across all 8 tasks on H1/G1/H1-2 while avoiding reward hacking | the only existing *real-humanoid loco-manip multi-morphology* substrate for the transfer-vs-generalist head-to-head — [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] supplies the distance gradient but is manipulation-only (no bipedal whole-body body) and GENBOT-1K (from [[2505.05753\|Embodiment-Scaling-Laws]]) supplies scale but is locomotion-only; SkillBench complements both with the real-humanoid loco-manip-task side H3/H5 need, but covers only 3 closely-related Unitree morphologies (no graded distance gradient) and is composition-instrumented, not transfer-instrumented |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (a morphology-invariant structure makes cheap cross-body transfer possible), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Phase-anchored transfer beats joint-space transfer at equal adaptation budget.**
   - *Prediction*: a [[2606.01851|PHASOR]] phase-anchored representation transfers to a held-out humanoid at higher tracking SR than joint-space transfer given the *same* adaptation data/compute, isolating the phase structure as the invariant (target ≥ 90.3% R@1 cross-embodiment retrieval where joint-space collapses).
   - *Test*: phase-anchored vs joint-space representation, matched adaptation budget, held-out target body; report retrieval + tracking SR.
   - *Row*: PHASOR (phase manifold) vs the joint-space baseline.
   - *Falsifier*: joint-space transfer matches phase-anchored at equal budget → the structure is not the invariant.
2. **H2 — The transferable structure is localized to the dynamics-sensitive pathway.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2605.23733|Any2Any]]'s PEFT into the dynamics-sensitive actor modules recovers more of the ~1%-compute transfer than equal-parameter PEFT into perception or output layers — the transferable structure lives in a specific pathway.
   - *Test*: sweep PEFT injection point (perception / dynamics / output) at matched parameter count; report transfer SR + convergence per placement.
   - *Row*: Any2Any (kinematic-align + PEFT).
   - *Falsifier*: PEFT placement does not matter → transfer is diffuse and not localizable.
3. **H3 — Transfer cost tracks inertial distance, not just kinematic distance, and the two can rank-invert.**
   - *Prediction*: on a pair-matched sweep, a kinematically-*near* target (same [[2505.14986|AnyBody]] interpolation bucket as the source) with *large* inertial distance (mass-distribution / CoM-offset mismatch) needs *more* adaptation compute to reach parity than a kinematically-*far* target (AnyBody extrapolation bucket) with *small* inertial distance — a rank inversion AnyBody's kinematic buckets alone would get backwards. [[2506.14278|Heavy-Limbs-WBC]] shows the mechanism directly: on the *same* kinematic structure, replacing the full centroidal-momentum model with an SRBD approximation that discards changing mass/inertia distribution (`cI`) collapses walking from 1.2 m/s / 60 N disturbance rejection down to 0.5 m/s / 20 N failure — the failure tracks mass/inertia distribution, not link topology, which is exactly what D2's H2 claims the transferable structure is anchored to.
   - *Test*: construct source→target pairs at matched AnyBody kinematic-distance bucket (interpolation vs extrapolation) but swept inertial distance, defined as normalized Frobenius distance on the inertia matrix $M(q)$ at a canonical pose (the doc's own Formal Framing object), or the cheaper fallback proxy — limb-mass-fraction / CoM-offset ratio, the same characterization [[2506.14278|Heavy-Limbs-WBC]] uses for its 40–60%-mass heavy-limb regime. Measure adaptation compute-to-parity per pair; regress compute against kinematic distance alone vs. kinematic-plus-inertial distance; test for the predicted rank inversion.
   - *Row*: AnyBody (morphology-distance protocol) / Heavy-Limbs-WBC (analytic centroidal-momentum coupling term) / Any2Any (kinematic-align + PEFT).
   - *Falsifier*: if AnyBody's kinematic buckets alone already order the pairs' adaptation cost correctly — inertial distance adds no incremental predictive power once kinematic distance is known — the kinematic-only distance protocol suffices, the rank inversion doesn't occur in practice, and the mismatch with H2's dynamics-sensitive-pathway claim is moot (couples to D1's morphology-range limit).
4. **H4 — Phase captures locomotion transfer better than manipulation transfer.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2606.01851|PHASOR]]'s phase manifold transfers loco-heavy skills (cyclic gait) at higher fidelity than non-periodic fine-manipulation skills, so a shared token space ([[2604.19734|UniT]]) transfers manipulation better — the right invariant is task-dependent.
   - *Test*: phase-anchored vs shared-token transfer on loco-heavy vs manip-heavy held-out skills; report per-task-class transfer SR.
   - *Row*: PHASOR (phase manifold) vs UniT (shared token space).
   - *Falsifier*: phase matches shared-token on manipulation → periodicity captures non-cyclic motion just as well.
5. **H5 — Cheap localized transfer beats a one-time generalist below a crossover, on whole-body loco-manipulation (the promoted front-line falsifier).**
   - *Prediction*: comparing cheap localized transfer ([[2605.23733|Any2Any]] PEFT) against a one-time-trained generalist ([[2602.05791|XHugWBC]] ~85% of specialists / [[2602.02960|Embodiment-Aware-Distillation]]) and scaled universal models ([[2511.07820|SONIC]] 100 M frames, [[2505.05753|Embodiment-Scaling-Laws]] 817 embodiments) on *whole-body loco-manipulation*, transfer wins on cost below a crossover, beyond which the generalist matches per-body transfer — transfer is the data/compute-efficient regime, not universally dominant.
   - *Test*: plot final loco-manip SR vs total data/compute for cheap-transfer vs one-time-generalist vs scaled-from-scratch on [[2505.05753|Embodiment-Scaling-Laws]]'s GENBOT-1K substrate (~1,000 procedurally-generated humanoids/quadrupeds/hexapods + explicit reward-vs-number-of-training-embodiments protocol) as the scale-side eval environment; locate the crossover.
   - *Row*: XHugWBC / Embodiment-Aware Distillation (generalist) / SONIC / Embodiment Scaling Laws (scaling environment / GENBOT-1K) vs Any2Any (transfer).
   - *Falsifier*: a generalist or scaled model always beats transfer at equal cost → cheap transfer buys nothing over a universal model.
6. **H6 — A shared token codebook under-utilizes on a distant body.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2604.19734|UniT]]'s discrete token space loses utilization (dead codes) transferring to a body far from the training distribution, degrading transfer SR — the codebook, not the alignment, becomes the bottleneck on distant morphologies.
   - *Test*: measure codebook utilization vs morphology distance — operationalizing 'distance' via [[2505.14986|AnyBody]]'s interpolation/extrapolation/composition gradient (no other benchmark supplies this distance axis) — and correlate with transfer SR.
   - *Row*: UniT (shared token space) / AnyBody (morphology-distance gradient).
   - *Falsifier*: utilization stays high on distant bodies → the discrete codebook is not the bottleneck.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Morphology distance bounds transfer, and kinematic distance alone mis-ranks it** — a body that looks near in link structure can be far in mass distribution, and a body too different overall may share too little structure. → H3 sweeps kinematic *and* inertial distance against adaptation cost and tests for the rank inversion; report where ~1% transfer breaks on each axis (couples to D1's morphology-range limit).
> - **Phase anchoring may not capture manipulation** — phase is natural for cyclic locomotion, less so for fine manipulation. → H4 reports transfer quality on loco-heavy vs manip-heavy tasks; route non-periodic skills through a shared token space ([[2604.19734|UniT]]) where phase underfits.
> - **Transfer can underperform a scaled universal model — the regime-boundary contradiction with A1** — at enough scale, one model may beat per-body transfer, mirroring A1's structured-prior-vs-scale tension at limb level: cheap/explicit structure (localized PEFT here, the explicit coupling term in A1) tends to win in the *low-data tail* and lose to the fine-tuned generalist at scale ([[2602.05791|XHugWBC]] gains +10% peak from fine-tuning, the at-scale winner). → Do **not** assert structure-always-wins: H5 locates the crossover, and the contribution is the *crossover boundary itself* — the data/compute scale at which the one-time generalist overtakes cheap localized transfer on whole-body loco-manipulation — published as a regime map, not a verdict that structured transfer is universally dominant.
> - **A discrete shared codebook can under-utilize** — dead codes on a distant body collapse the shared space. → H6 reports codebook utilization vs morphology distance; prefer a continuous phase manifold ([[2606.01851|PHASOR]]) where utilization is the risk.

### D3 — Whole-Body Synthetic Data Generation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | D — Whole-Body Teleoperation & Human-Motion Retargeting |
| **Thesis** | *Synthesize* coupled loco-manipulation demonstrations with no human in the per-demo loop — amplifying a small seed through whole-body planning, or generating demos from 3D assets and video priors — instead of hand-collecting each demo by teleoperation. The reason it must work: a demonstration is just a physically-feasible coupled trajectory, and feasibility (dynamic stability + contact) is something a generator can *enforce*, not something only a human can produce. The field assumes coupled-demo quantity is bounded by the human collection rate. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2605.27724\|HumanoidMimicGen]] (method), [[2606.05160\|GRAIL]] (method), [[2606.26855\|Humanoid-DART]] (method), [[2410.24185\|DexMimicGen]] (method), [[2601.02078\|Genie-Sim-3.0]] (dataset), [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2605.27724\|HumanoidMimicGen]] PSR 0.89 across 9 loco-manip tasks (1,000 generated demos) vs 0.33 DexMimicGen+, sim-and-real co-training 0.51→0.71 (+20% over real-only), ablations drop 0.89→0.49 (no motion noise) / 0.51 (no init randomization); [[2606.05160\|GRAIL]] 81.4% manipulation SR vs 48.5–49.2% baselines (124 motions), 88.9% sim tracking vs 15.8–24.0%, 20,000+ synthesized sequences, real G1 90% stair-climb / 84% seen / 80% unseen pick-up; [[2410.24185\|DexMimicGen]] 90% real humanoid from 40 sim demos vs 0% from 4 source |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: the whole-body data wall has two exits, and D1 takes the first — *retarget or capture* coupled demos with a human still in the loop — but every captured or retargeted demo still costs a human performing (or being recorded performing) the motion, so that route cannot reach the corpus sizes VLA training wants. The second exit removes the human from the per-demo loop entirely: *synthesize* the coupled trajectory.
- **Today's answers**: the seed-amplification route is now *multiply-instantiated*, not a single-anchor frontier. [[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]] adapts one teleoperated seed via whole-body planning (1,000 demos, 0.89 PSR vs 0.33 manipulation-only DexMimicGen+); [[2510.11258|DemoHLM]] runs the *same* seed → object-centric whole-body replay through a balance-enforcing controller → thousands of demos → sim-to-real G1 (5/5 LiftBox, published Oct 2025, *earlier* than HumanoidMimicGen); [[2510.18316|MoMaGen]] (ICLR'26) does it for mobile manip, training a policy from *one* source demo (60% real Pick-Cup after FT). From-scratch: [[2606.05160|GRAIL]] composes 3D assets + video priors into 4D HOI with no teleoperation (81.4% SR, synthetic-only real G1 90%/84%/80%). Feasibility-enforced generation: [[2509.20322|VisualMimic]] and [[2504.16843|Latent-Diffusion-LocoMan]] generate physically-plausible coupled trajectories; [[2410.18907|SkillMimicGen]] is the mimicgen replay precedent (65% vs 14% valid-demo generation). The "whole-body feasibility is the lever" claim is answered yes (DemoHLM + HumanoidMimicGen both confirm); the per-demo human is removable.
- **The opening**: with the seed route confirmed across three independent groups, the surviving lever is *not* "is whole-body feasibility the lever" but the un-measured ones: the *from-scratch-vs-seed diversity ceiling* (does a fixed seed cap synthesized variety — [[2510.11258|DemoHLM]] / HumanoidMimicGen both need a seed, GRAIL needs none) and the *feasibility→trainability monotone curve* (does lower synthesized penetration train to higher SR), which no prior paper plots.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A loco-manipulation demonstration is just a physically-feasible coupled trajectory — the same body dynamics ($M(q)$) and contact constraints hold whether a human or a planner authored it. Feasibility (dynamic stability, contact consistency, no penetration) is a *property of the trajectory*, checkable and enforceable, not a property of who made it. So a generator that enforces whole-body feasibility produces training-valid demos — and arbitrarily more than a human can teleoperate. [[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]] demonstrates the structure is load-bearing: the manipulation-only generator's 0.33 (vs 0.89) shows feasibility must be enforced *as whole-body dynamics*, not just contact, to make valid coupled demos.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "the human collection rate caps coupled-demo quantity" (the seed route refutes it — [[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]] 1,000-from-one-seed, [[2510.11258|DemoHLM]] thousands-from-one-seed, [[2606.05160|GRAIL]] 20,000-from-assets) but the next-order question the crowd left un-measured: whether a *fixed seed* caps synthesized diversity (the from-scratch-vs-seed ceiling) and whether *enforced contact-fidelity predicts* synthetic-demo trainability monotonically. The whole-body-feasibility-is-the-lever assumption is already settled yes; the diversity ceiling and the feasibility→trainability curve are not.
- **The bet**: (i) From-scratch generation ([[2606.05160|GRAIL]] 81.4% SR, synthetic-only 90% real) breaks the *diversity ceiling* a single-seed route ([[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]] 0.89 PSR, [[2510.11258|DemoHLM]]) hits — sweeping seed/asset breadth, generalization rises then plateaus for the seed route while from-scratch keeps climbing; and (ii) enforced synthesized contact-fidelity → downstream-SR is *monotone* ([[2606.05160|GRAIL]]'s 0.90% penetration / 88.9% tracking), so feasibility-enforcement during generation is a quantitative trainability predictor, with co-training lifting real SR 0.51→0.71 (+20%). Falsifiable: if the seed route's diversity does not plateau below from-scratch, or downstream SR is flat in synthesized feasibility, neither the ceiling nor the feasibility-curve is the lever.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how the coupled demo is synthesized, with no human in the per-demo loop* (seed-amplification via whole-body planning / asset+video-prior generation / SE(3)-replay mimicgen / generated-video→retarget / optimal-control-as-generator / planner-search demo-free / simulation substrate) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | Synthesis mechanism (no per-demo human) | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2605.27724\|HumanoidMimicGen]] | seed-amplification: adapts one teleoperated seed via whole-body planning (learned loco + joint-space manip) | 0.89 PSR (1,000 demos) vs 0.33 manipulation-only, co-train 0.51→0.71, ablations 0.89→0.49/0.51 | still needs *one* human seed per skill; diversity bounded by the seed (H3's randomization ceiling) |
| [[2510.11258\|DemoHLM]] | seed-amplification: object-centric whole-body replay through a balance-enforcing controller → thousands of demos | sim-to-real G1 across 6 loco-manip tasks (5/5 LiftBox/PressCube, 4/5 Handover); ACT/DP beat MLP | a *co-equal* seed-amplification instantiation (Oct 2025, earlier than the anchor) — but never measures the diversity ceiling vs from-scratch (H3) |
| [[2510.18316\|MoMaGen]] | seed-amplification for mobile manip: trains a policy from *one* source demo with visibility constraints | 63% D0 gen-success, 2× visibility ratio; π0 60% real Pick-Cup after synthetic pretrain + 40-demo FT | the mobile-manip seed route — pre-empts the seed half, but doesn't run from-scratch-vs-seed nor the feasibility→trainability curve (H4) |
| [[2606.05160\|GRAIL]] | from-scratch: 3D assets + video-foundation-model priors → 4D HOI, *zero teleoperation* | 81.4% manip vs 48.5%, 88.9% sim tracking, 20,000+ sequences, real G1 90%/84%/80% from synthetic-only | the human is fully removed, but generation fidelity is bounded by the asset pool + video-prior quality |
| [[2509.20322\|VisualMimic]] | feasibility-enforced hierarchical visuomotor generation (noise injection + action clipping) | 3.8 kg box pushed 37 m/min, football dribble 135 m/min; zero-shot real G1 lift/kick/push | generates feasible coupled motion but for a fixed skill set — no asset-driven diversity, no synthesized-feasibility → trainability regression |
| [[2504.16843\|Latent-Diffusion-LocoMan]] | latent-diffusion long-horizon trajectory generation with collision avoidance | physically-plausible long-horizon loco-manip sequences, better collision avoidance than baselines | a feasibility-enforced generation route, but sim-validated only — the dynamic-feasibility-during-generation cousin without a downstream-SR curve |
| [[2410.18907\|SkillMimicGen]] | SE(3) skill-segment replay generating valid demos (the mimicgen precedent) | 65% vs 14% valid-demo generation (Coffee); 95% SR at 5000 demos; zero-shot sim-to-real | manipulation-centric skill replay — the mimicgen ancestor, but no *whole-body* loco coupling synthesized (the gap HumanoidMimicGen/DemoHLM fill) |
| [[2410.24185\|DexMimicGen]] | SE(3)-equivariant replay of a few source demos in sim (the mimicgen precedent) | 90% real humanoid from 40 generated vs 0% from 4 source | replay is manipulation-centric — no *whole-body* planning, so the loco coupling is not synthesized (the gap HumanoidMimicGen fills) |
| [[2512.05094\|GenMimic]] | generated human *video* → 4D retarget → physics-aware RL tracker (keypoint-conditioned, symmetry-regularized) | 86.77% sim SR vs GMT 4.29% / TWIST 2.69%, coherent real G1 zero-shot | the generated video is noisy/morphologically distorted; robustness comes from the tracker, not clean demos |
| [[2604.27711\|ExoActor]] | exocentric video generation as "imagined demos" → whole-body + hand estimation → general tracker | generalizes to new scenarios with no real-world data collection | early-stage; no explicit feasibility enforcement on the generated video — plausibility ≠ dynamic feasibility |
| [[2606.06139\|MotionDisco]] | LLM-evolutionary contact-plan search + kinodynamic optimization, *demonstration-free* | 8/8 long-horizon, zero-shot real | per-task search, no reusable feasible library — synthesizes per-task, not a transferable corpus (also A2) |
| [[2601.02078\|Genie-Sim-3.0]] | LLM-driven high-fidelity simulator with automated scene + task generation (the substrate synthesis runs in) | 10,000+ h synthetic data, R²=0.94 sim-real, 100,000+ eval scenarios | a simulation + scene-generation platform, not a *motion* synthesizer — supplies the world, not the coupled trajectory |
| [[2512.24653\|RoboMIND-2.0]] | real captured mobile-manip corpus across embodiments (the real anchor synthesis augments) | up to 1.0 multi-robot collaborative SR, 20K trajectories / 6 embodiments | real data, not synthesized — the co-training anchor that synthetic demos are mixed with (Q2's real-data requirement) |
| [[2602.10106\|EgoHumanoid]] | human-*captured* robot-free egocentric demos (a human performs each one) — the D1 counterpart | +51 pp generalization, +19 pp in-domain from robot-free human demos | a human performs every demo (capture) — the per-demo-human cost synthesis removes; the capture/synthesis contrast made concrete (D1) |
| [[2605.20373\|SUGAR]] | human-*video* refined into skills — captured priors, not synthesized trajectories | 32.7%→76.0% data scaling, zero-shot G1 | the video records a real human; SUGAR refines captured priors, it does not author new demos — the human-video-as-prior boundary (feeds D1) |
| [[2605.03452\|BifrostUMI]] | robot-free UMI handheld *capture* → whole-body humanoid | recovers whole-body motion from sparse hand-held capture | low-cost *capture*, still human-performed — the cheap-capture complement on the D1 side of the line |
| [[2511.09484\|SPIDER]] | physics-informed retargeting engine that *scales* whatever demos exist (captured or synthesized) | 2.4 M frames / 800 h across 5 hands + 4 humanoids, up to 100% SR | a retargeting multiplier, not a demo *author* — the scale engine downstream of synthesis (feeds D1) |
| [[2505.12748\|TeleOpBench]] | benchmark — evaluates teleop-*captured* data quality across modalities | 30 tasks × 4 modalities × 3 humanoids, sim-real correlation | scores capture quality, not synthesis quality — the capture-side yardstick synthesis is compared against |
| [[2603.20147\|AGILE]] | workflow — standardizes deployment of generated (or captured) loco-manip data | unified deployment workflow + motion-quality diagnostics | standardizes deployment + feasibility diagnostics for synthesized data but authors no demos — the post-synthesis substrate |
| [[2505.12705\|DreamGen]] | from-scratch: video-world-model generation → neural trajectories, paired with DreamGen Bench scoring | DreamGen Bench scores generated trajectories on Instruction-Following + Physics-Alignment with >90% Pearson correlation to downstream SR; GR1 humanoid 37%→46.4% on 4 tasks, 22 novel behaviors 43.2% vs 11.2%, 10 unseen-env behaviors 28.5% vs 0% | scores generated-VIDEO physics-plausibility, not whole-body coupled-trajectory penetration/foot-skating — standardizes the feasibility→trainability correlation principle but narrows rather than closes the gap |
| [[2604.11251\|CLAW]] | from-scratch: composes parameterized kinematic-planner primitives (movement/heading/speed/pelvis-height/duration) in MuJoCo into physically-grounded G1 whole-body trajectories, stitched into long-horizon sequences, no per-demo human | physically-consistent retarget-free G1 trajectories with walk→squat→crawl stitching; automated language annotation in 8 styles, up to 17 full-trajectory variations | a *composable-primitive* from-scratch route (vs GRAIL's asset+video-prior from-scratch) — supplies the H1 diversity knob as primitive-library breadth, but reports no downstream-SR or feasibility→trainability curve, so the diversity-ceiling comparison vs the seed route (H1) is set up, not run |
| [[2604.20841\|DeVI]] | generated-video→imitation for *dexterous* HOI: text→synthetic 2D video → hybrid 3D-human + 2D-object target → physics-RL tracker, no per-demo human | 25-41mm MPJPE(All) on GRAB vs baselines' 91-142mm; Visual-HOI-Alignment cuts contact distance 101mm→18.7mm; 50.0% success ratio vs InterMimic's 0.500% | extends the GenMimic/ExoActor generated-video lever from gross whole-body locomotion to dexterous hand-object interaction with an explicit synthesized-feasibility number — but tracking-fidelity is sim-character imitation, not yet a robot loco-manip SR (the feasibility→trainability link GenMimic/ExoActor also leave open for the manipulation half) |
| [[2508.14120\|SimGenHOI]] | from-scratch *generative + RL-refinement loop*: a DiT generates whole-body humanoid-object interaction from text/object-geometry/waypoints, a contact-aware whole-body RL control policy tracks it while *correcting* penetration + foot-sliding, and a mutual fine-tuning loop co-refines generator and policy | foot sliding **0.44→0.13 mm** + hand penetration eliminated (**0.00 mm**) after policy tracking; **7/7** contact-target tracking SR vs Chois+PHC-X **0/7** | fills the one empty cell on the from-scratch lane — a generative + RL-refinement-loop synthesizer distinct from [[2606.05160\|GRAIL]]'s asset+video-prior and [[2604.11251\|CLAW]]'s composable-primitive routes and from [[2509.20322\|VisualMimic]]'s hierarchical noise-injection; directly instruments H4's contact-fidelity axis (penetration / foot-skating as the feasibility-enforcement-during-generation knob) and is whole-body, but sim-character (Isaac Gym, furniture/box) with tracking-SR not real-robot loco-manip SR — the same sim-validated/tracking-fidelity caveat the cited Latent Diffusion LocoMan / GenMimic carry |
| [[2602.21599\|Iterative-Closed-Loop-Motion-Synthesis]] | automated closed-loop data engine: text-conditioned motion-diffusion generation + VLM/physics *difficulty-aware curriculum* that co-evolves the synthetic dataset with the physics controller, no per-demo human | OOD failure **41.7%→23.1%** (45% relative, six loops); Kungfu **65.8** vs 57.2 / AIST++ **83.9** vs 68.9; later loops generate systematically *harder* motions (VLM difficulty rising monotonically, broader speeds) | a distinct curriculum-co-evolving difficulty-*escalation* synthesizer (CLAIMS) — separate from [[2508.14120\|SimGenHOI]]'s generator↔policy fidelity loop, [[2606.05160\|GRAIL]]'s asset+video-prior, [[2604.11251\|CLAW]]'s composable primitives, and [[2509.20322\|VisualMimic]]'s noise injection; its rising-difficulty result is the first direct evidence an automated generator pushes past a fixed manifold's diversity ceiling, instrumenting H1 + H3 — but it synthesizes whole-body *tracking/control* motion, not object-coupled loco-manip demos (the sim-validated/tracking-fidelity caveat) |
| [[2606.26855\|Humanoid-DART]] | seed-amplification via a *generator↔tracker relabeling loop*: a goal-conditioned Diffusion Transformer proposes kinematic trajectories for sampled goals, an RL motion tracker evaluates them in sim, and successful re-labeled rollouts grow an elite archive across an iterative curriculum | **96.6%** pick-and-place task-space coverage from **4** demos; generalizes to goals **4–5×** further than the base data; real G1 push/kick/pick-and-place | the cleanest evidence for D3's H1/H3 on the *loco-manip* axis — a 4-demo seed amplified to near-complete coverage with 4–5× goal-extrapolation directly measures the seed's diversity reach, and the diffusion-generator↔RL-tracker loop is the relabeling sibling of [[2508.14120\|SimGenHOI]]'s generator↔policy fidelity loop and [[2602.21599\|Iterative-Closed-Loop-Motion-Synthesis]]'s curriculum loop — but it still needs *one* seed per skill (the from-scratch [[2606.05160\|GRAIL]] does not) and reports coverage, not a feasibility→SR regression (H4) |
| [[2606.26215\|TaskNPoint]] | seed-amplification from a *single human video* + a defined "interaction window": retarget the clip as an RL reference, then randomized-target sampling in sim for zero-shot goal generalization, no per-task reward tuning | **93%** sim ballistic-hitting / **98%** pick-place GSR over 3D target volumes; **100%** real slow tennis/soccer, 45–70% fast; <10 demos, <1 hr on one GPU | the *minimal-seed zero-shot-target-volume* datapoint for H3 — generalizes across a 3D volume of goals from one clip via randomized-target practice (no generator, no reward tuning), a distinct seed-amplification mechanism from [[2606.26855\|Humanoid-DART]]'s diffusion-relabeling loop and [[2605.27724\|HumanoidMimicGen]]'s whole-body-planning replay; but the diversity is bounded to the seed clip's skill (the diversity ceiling H1 turns on) and it reports goal-coverage SR, not a feasibility→trainability curve |
| [[2606.25591\|WOLF-VLA]] | from-scratch via *optimal control as the data generator*: solves an OC problem per task across randomized environments to synthesize a large dynamically-consistent + optimal whole-body trajectory dataset, then trains a GR00T-N1.5-initialized flow-matching VLA on it | 99–**100%** Walk-Forward SR with low ROM error, robust to visual distractors, vs ACT **1.4%** / π0.5 **0%** | the *optimal-control-as-generator* synthesis route absent from the table — distinct from [[2606.05160\|GRAIL]]'s asset+video-prior and [[2604.11251\|CLAW]]'s composable-primitive from-scratch routes, it enforces dynamic consistency + optimality *by construction* through OC (the strongest feasibility-enforcement during generation, H4) and removes the per-demo human entirely — but it is locomotion-centric (the object-coupled loco-manip half stays the open contribution) and reports no synthesized-feasibility→downstream-SR regression |
| [[2606.30645\|VLK]] | from-scratch: synthesizes paired vision-language-kinematics loco-manip trajectories by planning *interactions inside 3DGS-reconstructed real scenes* (no per-demo human) | **48,000** trajectories in **600 GPU-h**; real G1 up to **100%** object-directed navigation / **100%** floor-level box manipulation; visual domain randomization lifts nav **41%→90%** | a *scene-reconstruction-grounded* from-scratch route distinct from [[2606.05160\|GRAIL]]'s asset+video-prior, [[2604.11251\|CLAW]]'s composable primitives, and [[2606.25591\|WOLF-VLA]]'s optimal-control synthesis, grounding the synthesized coupling in reconstructed real geometry, but reporting no synthesized-feasibility → downstream-SR regression (H4) nor a from-scratch-vs-seed diversity-ceiling sweep (H1) |
| [[2608.03387\|RoboReact]] | generated-video→retarget with an *agentic VLM iterative-refinement loop* (not a one-shot generate-and-track): calibration rollouts feed a VLM that diagnoses failures and proposes bounded structured edits (keep/align/offset/insert/delete) to the retargeted skill over 15 rounds, plus deterministic feasibility projection and online object-centric re-grounding at test time | **81.3%** avg terminal SR over 4 real-G1 bimanual tasks, matching a *One-Shot Real Prior* (human-video) baseline's **80.0%** — from *generated* video with zero teleoperation; SR non-decreasing over 15 refinement rounds; frozen execution retains **80–94%** under base-pose/object-shift/height perturbations | a distinct mechanism on the generated-video lane beside [[2512.05094\|GenMimic]] (robust tracker absorbs noise) and [[2604.27711\|ExoActor]] (no explicit refinement) — closed-loop *agentic* editing of the skill itself rather than robustifying the downstream tracker, and the first to show generated-video parity with a real-human-video seed on a whole-body bimanual task; sharpens H5 as a third arm (iterative refinement vs robust-tracker vs upstream video-cleaning) but reports no synthesized-feasibility → SR regression (H4) |
| [[2606.12995\|GenHOI]] | generated-video→retarget with *explicit geometric feasibility enforcement*: a real-to-sim digital twin renders the first frame for video-diffusion generation, a VLM extracts a hand-object contact keyframe, and geometry-guided constrained trajectory optimization snaps the motion onto the object mesh before closed-loop tracking, no per-demo human | **76.7%** avg SR / **0.22 m** contact error vs [[2604.27711\|ExoActor]]'s **11.7%** / **0.75 m**; **1 min 51 s** skill acquisition vs ~70 min policy-training; **8/10** at 3.0 m OOD box distance vs **0/10** both baselines | supplies the explicit feasibility-enforcement [[2604.27711\|ExoActor]]'s row flags as missing ("plausibility ≠ dynamic feasibility") via geometric correction rather than tracker robustness — sharpens H5 as a fourth arm (geometric correction vs [[2512.05094\|GenMimic]]'s robust-tracker vs upstream video-cleaning vs [[2608.03387\|RoboReact]]'s agentic refinement), but still reports no synthesized-feasibility → SR regression (H4) |
| [[2608.12063\|SMPC-Bootstrapped RL]] | from-scratch via *optimal-control-as-generator*, the offline-to-online-RL variant: tiled GPU-parallel sampling-based MPC (SMPC) generates ~1 M expert loco-manipulation transitions/hour that bootstrap a sparse-reward off-policy TD3 agent (50% replay-buffer fill, phased out past a 10% success threshold), stacked over a frozen whole-body stabilizer | near-**100%** sim SR across 5 tasks on both a Spot-arm quadruped and a G1 humanoid; beats the SMPC teacher on completion time, some tasks by **>50%**; hardest task (tire-rolling) needs **4M** samples / **4** GPU-hours and collapses below a 50% expert ratio | the RL-bootstrap sibling to [[2606.25591\|WOLF-VLA]]'s OC-per-task-dataset-then-VLA-train route — genuinely *loco-manipulation*-coupled (contact-rich tire-rolling/box tasks) and cross-embodiment (quadruped-arm + humanoid) where WOLF-VLA is locomotion-only, but reports no synthesized-feasibility → downstream-SR regression (H4) and, like the seed-amplification rows, needs the SMPC teacher retuned per task |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (enforced-feasibility whole-body synthesis supplies coupled demos at scale, no per-demo human), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — From-scratch generation breaks the seed's diversity ceiling that DemoHLM and HumanoidMimicGen share.**
   - *Prediction*: sweeping seed/asset breadth, the seed-amplification route ([[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]] / [[2510.11258|DemoHLM]] / [[2606.26855|Humanoid-DART]]'s 4-demo→96.6% with 4–5× goal-extrapolation) generalization rises then *plateaus* at a measurable diversity ceiling tied to the single seed, while from-scratch generation ([[2606.05160|GRAIL]], asset-driven; [[2606.25591|WOLF-VLA]], OC-driven) keeps climbing past it on held-out object/scene variation — the comparison neither seed paper runs. (The whole-body-vs-manipulation-only lever, 0.33→0.89, is already settled by HumanoidMimicGen + DemoHLM, so it is the control, not the test.)
   - *Test*: match generation budget; sweep seed count (seed route) vs asset-pool breadth (from-scratch); plot generalization SR vs diversity for both on [[2505.12705|DreamGen]]'s DreamGen Bench GR1-humanoid generalization splits (22 novel behaviors 43.2% vs 11.2%, 10 unseen-env behaviors 28.5% vs 0%) as the held-out behavior/environment yardstick, locate the seed-route plateau.
   - *Row*: HumanoidMimicGen / DemoHLM (seed-amplification) vs GRAIL / DreamGen (from-scratch generation + generalization splits).
   - *Falsifier*: the seed route's diversity does not plateau below from-scratch → the seed is not the diversity bottleneck and from-scratch generation buys no extra breadth.
2. **H2 — Synthetic-only suffices for some tasks; others need a real-data anchor.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2606.05160|GRAIL]]'s synthetic-only transfer (90% stair-climb) holds for dynamics-dominated tasks, while contact-precision tasks need [[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]]-style real+synthetic co-training (0.51→0.71) — the synthetic-only/co-trained boundary is set by contact-precision demand.
   - *Test*: synthetic-only vs co-trained on dynamics-heavy vs contact-precision tasks; report the SR gap per task property.
   - *Row*: GRAIL (synthetic-only) vs HumanoidMimicGen (co-trained) / RoboMIND 2.0 (real anchor).
   - *Falsifier*: synthetic-only matches co-trained on contact-precision tasks → no real anchor is ever needed.
3. **H3 — Synthesized diversity has a feasibility ceiling per seed, shared across seed-amplification systems.**
   - *Prediction*: sweeping [[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]]'s randomization breadth (the ablation that drops 0.89→0.49 without motion noise, 0.51 without init randomization), [[2510.11258|DemoHLM]]'s object-pose randomization, and [[2606.26215|TaskNPoint]]'s randomized-target-volume sampling from one clip, generalization rises then plateaus, and pushing diversity past a measurable point breaks feasibility — a fixed seed supports only bounded synthesized variety regardless of the seed-amplification system.
   - *Test*: sweep motion-noise + init/object-pose / target-volume randomization magnitude on the seed systems; report generalization SR + feasibility-violation rate vs breadth.
   - *Row*: HumanoidMimicGen / DemoHLM / Humanoid-DART / TaskNPoint (seed-amplification).
   - *Falsifier*: diversity scales without a feasibility ceiling → the seed is not the diversity bottleneck and synthesis is unbounded.
4. **H4 — Enforced contact-fidelity predicts synthetic-demo trainability.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2606.05160|GRAIL]]'s contact/depth-aligned optimization (0.90% penetration, 88.9% sim tracking) traces a monotone synthesized-feasibility → downstream-SR curve — synthetic demos with lower penetration train to higher SR, so enforced contact-fidelity is what makes them trainable (couples to D1's interaction-preservation and A2's feasibility-first imitation).
   - *Test*: vary generation-time contact-enforcement strength; regress downstream SR on synthesized penetration/foot-skating, calibrating against [[2505.12705|DreamGen]]'s DreamGen Bench (Instruction-Following + Physics-Alignment scoring with >90% Pearson correlation to downstream SR) as the standardized generation-feasibility→trainability diagnostic (it scores generated-video plausibility, narrowing not closing the whole-body-trajectory gap).
   - *Row*: GRAIL (asset+video-prior generation) / DreamGen (generation-quality benchmark).
   - *Falsifier*: downstream SR is flat in synthesized feasibility → contact-enforcement during generation is decorative.
5. **H5 — A robust tracker beats clean generation for noisy generated video.**
   - *Prediction*: for *generated* (not captured) human video, [[2512.05094|GenMimic]]'s robustness-from-the-tracker (symmetry regularization + keypoint-weighted rewards) recovers more SR than trying to clean the generated video upstream — the noise is better absorbed by the policy than removed from the data (86.77% vs GMT 4.29% / TWIST 2.69%).
   - *Test*: robust-tracker-on-noisy-video vs cleaned-video-standard-tracker on GenMimicBench; report SR per generation-noise level.
   - *Row*: GenMimic (generated-video→retarget) vs ExoActor (generated-video, no explicit robustness).
   - *Falsifier*: cleaning the video upstream matches the robust tracker → robustness should live in the data pipeline, not the policy.
6. **H6 — A planner-discovered demo is recyclable into a reusable library, not just per-task.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2606.06139|MotionDisco]]'s demonstration-free contact-plan search, when distilled into a reusable feasible-demo library, transfers to *unseen* long-horizon coupled tasks without re-running the per-task search — closing its named "per-task search, no reusable library" gap.
   - *Test*: distill MotionDisco plans into a demo library; evaluate trained-policy SR on held-out tasks vs re-searching per task.
   - *Row*: MotionDisco (planner-search, demo-free).
   - *Falsifier*: distilled plans do not transfer → planner synthesis is irreducibly per-task and cannot build a corpus.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **A feasibility-enforcing generator is itself hard to build** — synthesis replaces collection cost with generator-engineering cost. → [[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]] reuses a learned locomotion controller + classical IK/planning; H1/H3 report demos-per-seed and seed-collection cost, so the amortization (one generator, many demos) is the measured win.
> - **Synthetic demos can carry a sim-to-real gap the policy inherits** — generated feasibility need not equal real feasibility. → [[2606.05160|GRAIL]]'s contact/depth alignment + [[2601.02078|Genie-Sim-3.0]]'s R²=0.94 bound it; H2/H4 report synthetic-only vs co-trained real SR, treating the gap as a measured quantity.
> - **Generation diversity is capped by the seed / asset pool** — a narrow seed or asset set limits coverage. → H3 sweeps randomization breadth; [[2606.05160|GRAIL]] widens it with video-prior assets; report generalization vs seed/asset breadth, not single-task SR.
> - **Generated video is plausible but not dynamically feasible** — a video model can render physically impossible motion. → [[2512.05094|GenMimic]]'s physics-aware RL tracker absorbs the noise (H5); never train directly on generated video — pass it through 4D-retarget + a feasibility-enforcing tracker.

---
## Cross-Cutting Themes

> [!tip] The Coupling Is the Object — Part-Wise Factoring Discards the Load-Bearing Term
> A1 and C1 are two faces of one fact: a humanoid is not an arm bolted to legs. A1 makes the arm–leg inertial coupling ($M(q)$ non-block-diagonal) a predicted quantity; C1 makes the external wrench a whole-body equilibrium term the legs compensate. Both beat part-wise / sequential baselines by modeling the cross-subsystem coupling the factored approach discards — [[2604.07993|HEX]]'s 41.0%→61.8% OOD, [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s 0.60→0.37 — so the coupling, not either subsystem, is the load-bearing term. The same principle held for mobile manipulation's base-arm action ([[2503.05652|BRS]]'s autoregressive base→torso→arm factoring, 13×/21× over DP3/RGB-DP), even after that isolation was retired from this doc as a standalone direction. A4's unified-latent policy is the interface that commands both couplings jointly.

> [!tip] The Action Target Must Be Feasible Before the Policy Can Use It
> A2, D1, and A3 converge on feasibility-of-the-reference as the binding constraint, inverting the "collect more demonstrations" reflex. A2 composes behavior from frozen *feasible* primitives (softmax convex-hull confinement avoids reward hacking); D1 retargets human motion by preserving *interaction* contact so the reference is realizable; A3 closes the loop in the world frame so the tracked target is drift-free. The evidence is a feasibility→trainability chain: [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]]'s zero foot-skating → 82–94% downstream RL, [[2604.00202|DreamControl-v2]]'s 68%-vs-8% valid trajectories → 0.925-vs-0.101 RL, [[2606.03476|Human2Humanoid]]'s 0.05 cm penetration → 88.5% SR — fix the target manifold and small data suffices, the same inversion [[Locomotion|Locomotion]]'s motion-imitation direction reaches for pure locomotion.

> [!tip] Structured Decomposition Beats Monolithic Whole-Body Scaling
> A2, A3, and C1 all reject monolithic end-to-end whole-body policies for structured decomposition, because the coupled high-DoF action space is where flat RL fails ([[2403.10506|HumanoidBench]]). A2 blends frozen primitives; A3 splits a world-frame Commander from a whole-body Tracker; C1 decomposes lower/upper agents with shared proprioception. Each reports the decomposition as the source of the win — [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]] avoids reward hacking, [[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s Commander/Tracker hits 12.4 mm, [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]'s decomposition converges faster than monolithic — the field is converging on structure over scale for the coupled action space, a pattern [[2503.05652|BRS]]'s autoregressive base→torso→arm factoring also confirmed for mobile-manipulation action coordination.

> [!tip] The Whole-Body Data Wall Is Crossed by Human Video, Synthesis, and Cross-Embodiment Amortization
> A4, B2, D1, D2, and D3 all confront the scarcity of *coupled* loco-manip data, and converge on three levers: human video, automatic synthesis, and cross-body transfer. A4 pretrains on action-free human egocentric video (+38.7%); D1 captures or retargets simultaneous loco+manip via whole-body teleop or robot-free egocentric demos (+51 pp generalization); D3 *synthesizes* coupled demos through whole-body planning or from 3D assets + video priors, removing the per-demo human; D2 amortizes a pretrained policy across humanoids at ~1% compute; B2's memory lets one traversal cover a workspace larger than the view. The shared insight — [[2602.10106|EgoHumanoid]]'s +51 pp, [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]]'s +38.7%, [[2605.20373|SUGAR]]'s 32.7%→76.0%, [[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]]'s 0.89 vs 0.33, [[2605.23733|Any2Any]]'s ~1% — is that the coupling must be *in the demonstrations*, whether captured or retargeted (D1), synthesized with whole-body feasibility enforced (D3), or *transferred from a body that has it* (D2), never assembled from decoupled data, the same human-video substrate the [[Embodied-AI|Embodied-AI]] umbrella's egocentric-pretraining and morphology-invariance directions develop.

> [!tip] Whole-Body Deployment Is Bounded by Hard Physical Constraints the Task Reward Omits
> C2, A3, and B1 surface deployment limits that live *off* the task-success surface. C2 enforces balance + collision as hard constraints via certified projection (the task reward only softly discourages violation); A3's Kinematic Manifold Prior keeps the action kinematically feasible; B1 treats observability as an action because a moving base loses sight of the target. The evidence — [[2604.07457|CMP]]'s 4.7%→46.9% OOD survival from projection, [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s collision-free-under-20%-mass-mismatch vs ~50% baseline, [[2306.11565|HomeRobot]]'s 5–15%→0.4–0.6% perception collapse — shows deployability is bounded by constraints (balance, collision, observability) the task reward silently ignores, the same embodiment-cost insight [[Locomotion|Locomotion]]'s force/thermal direction raises for pure locomotion.

> [!tip] The Coupling Can Be Predicted, Shaped, or Collected For — Three Levers, Not Two
> A1, C3, and Cluster D name three distinct ways to handle the arm→base/leg coupling, and they are not the same lever wearing different clothes. A1 *predicts* the coupling term and feeds it forward as an auxiliary target ([[2604.07993|HEX]]'s implicit MoE, the explicit residual A1 bets against it); D1 and D3 *collect* (or synthesize) more coupled data so a fixed architecture can absorb the coupling statistically ([[2602.10106|EgoHumanoid]]'s +51 pp, [[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]]'s 0.89 vs 0.33); C3 *shapes* the coupling at its source by making the region's own stiffness a chosen action, changing how much of the reaction ever reaches the support polygon rather than predicting or absorbing it after the fact. The existing reference-modulation lane inside Cluster C ([[2508.00355|TOP]]'s speed cap, [[2602.06341|HiWET]]'s base/waist absorption) already shapes the coupling too, but through timing and posture, not through the joint's own compliance — C3 is a genuinely new instrument on that shaping axis, not a restatement of A1's prediction or D1/D3's collection.

> [!tip] Data Is the Breadth Substrate, Explicit Coupling Is the Build — on a Fixed Budget the Idea Beats the Data
> On a *fixed* data budget, the architectural idea moves whole-body capability more than data volume, and the sharpest evidence is internal to this doc's anchors. [[2604.07993|HEX]]'s ablation: removing the coupling component (UPP) costs **−5/12** on Pouring (the largest single-component drop), while its 12M-frame pretraining adds only **+1/12** at convergence — "pretraining mainly improves optimization efficiency rather than the final converged performance." [[2503.05652|BRS]] reports up to a **53%** drop from removing autoregressive decoding *on identical JoyLo data*. [[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] gets its **~2×** tracking gain from dual-agent decomposition with *zero* demonstration data. Two head-to-head tests put the idea above the data: [[2603.12263|Psi0]] beats a competitor trained on **>10×** more data by **+40%** via a *data recipe*, and [[2511.05275|TwinVLA]] beats proprietary bimanual data with a cheap coordination term and *zero* bimanual pretraining. What data uniquely buys is generalization *breadth* — [[2602.10106|EgoHumanoid]]'s **+51 pp** (82% vs 31%) from adding robot-free human demos to a fixed policy is real and architecture-on-fixed-data cannot manufacture it — but breadth is a *substrate*, not the contribution. The reorder: **A1's thin explicit coupling is the build, A4 the interface that grounds into it, D1 the breadth substrate the method consumes** — the scarce, idea-bound contribution is making the coupling *explicit*, not collecting more demos. ([[2511.05275|TwinVLA]] proves the coupling is thin: a cheap explicit term beats a coupling-is-everything model trained on far more data.)

---

## Benchmark Gaps

| Gap | Direction | Existing closest |
|---|---|---|
| No benchmark isolates the *arm-as-balance-disturbance* coupling — measuring balance error as a function of reach aggressiveness | A1 | [[2604.07993\|HEX]] (7 ID + 8 OOD whole-body tasks, but coupling not isolated from task SR) |
| No standardized loco-manip skill-composition suite separating reward-hacking from feasibility across embodiments | A2 | [[2506.09366\|SkillBlender]] / SkillBench (8 tasks / 3 embodiments, decomposes accuracy+feasibility but short-episode, not decomposed by primitive coverage) + [[2508.16943\|LHM-Humanoid]] (350 scenes, continuous-episode no-reset + 66-task OOD split — supplies the long-horizon + distilled-monolith comparator SkillBench lacks, but is one-policy not composition-instrumented) — no single suite combines reward-hacking/feasibility decomposition with no-reset long-horizon on one shared skill set. The pipeline-location head-to-head (online blend vs upstream prior vs distilled monolith) now has a benchmark substrate that isolates the high-level/low-level split: [[2606.17833\|HumanoidArena]] decouples the perception-conditioned policy from frozen General Motion Trackers and measures cross-tracker transfer retention on 7 leg-critical tasks, but it scores end-policies on a fixed 40D action interface and supplies no composition rule — so it can host the comparison without itself testing feasibility-by-construction |
| No long-horizon world-frame end-effector-precision benchmark with base-disturbance-vs-distance breakdown | A3 | [[2602.06341\|HiWET]] (12.4 mm world-frame EE RMSE, but a METHOD not a benchmark — no error-vs-locomotion-distance curve); closest standardized SR substrate [[2508.16943\|LHM-Humanoid]] (66 unseen-task 61.60% Success-All, but task-success not EE precision, omits the distance breakdown and the world-frame metric entirely) |
| No benchmark measuring loco-vs-manip latent separation in a unified policy (different-visual-dynamics claim untested) | A4 | [[2508.16943\|LHM-Humanoid]] (unified-vs-E2E-RL head-to-head, 71.14% seen / 61.60% unseen on 350 scenes / 66 unseen tasks; but camera-motion-stratified two-LAM-vs-one-LAM and subgoal-precision LMO ablations not benchmarked); narrowed but not closed by [[2606.17833\|HumanoidArena]], which isolates the high-level-latent ↔ low-level-tracker interface and measures cross-GMT transfer + visual-perturbation sensitivity on 7 leg-critical tasks — the latent↔tracker harness LHM-Humanoid lacks — yet still ships no camera-motion-stratified two-LAM-vs-one-LAM (split-by-visual-dynamics) protocol, leaving H1's defining ablation without a named benchmark |
| No active-perception suite that couples gaze to a *moving base* / measures target-in-frame stratified by base-motion phase | B1 | [[2605.07943\|TAVIS]] (headcam-vs-fixedcam controlled ablation + GALT + ID/OOD on 2 humanoid torsos) and [[2602.01939\|EFM-10]] (10 occlusion/exploration tasks) — but both are FIXED-BASE; neither couples gaze to a moving base, so the active-gaze-conditioned-on-base-trajectory slice (H2/H3) remains open. [[2606.18772\|HALOMI]] now supplies a moving-body-humanoid active-perception disable-ablation (90%→30%) coupled through a whole-body controller — narrowing the "learned gaze coupled to a moving base" gap from the humanoid side — but still leaves un-benchmarked the gaze-conditioned-on-chosen-base-trajectory and gaze-vs-exploration / during-vs-between decompositions (H2/H3/H5), and [[2505.06182\|APPLE-Active-Perception]] adds an RL-objective gaze foil that no visual mobile-manipulation benchmark yet evaluates against imitation-learned gaze |
| No long-horizon multi-room mobile-manip memory benchmark with relocated-object protocol | B2 | [[2603.04639\|RoboMME]] (memory taxonomy + 14-variant floor, but tabletop not mobile) / [[2411.04999\|DynaMem]]-DynaBench (relocated-object, 70% dynamic / 74.5% query, but single-room) — neither sweeps multi-room extent. The RoboMME comparator pool now includes a write-efficient event-driven episodic baseline ([[2606.17463\|WeaveLA]] 19.0%→24.7%, 0%→47.8% SWINGXTIMES) and a VLM-retrieval episodic baseline ([[2510.20328\|MemER]] on π0.5), so the H6 footprint-vs-SR sweep and the H1 episodic-channel ablation now have explicit external write-frequency datapoints. The graded-memory-amount axis (H3/H4) now has a principled metric — [[2603.01229\|RMBench]]'s Task Memory Complexity bins M(1) vs M(n) with a persistent-anchor-vs-sliding ablation and a Markovian floor (ACT 0.0% / Pi0.5 5.83%) — and the out-of-view-via-occlusion case (H2) has [[2511.11478\|LIBERO-Mem]]'s object-permanence-through-occlusion suite (Embodied-SlotSSM 14.8% vs OpenVLA 5.0%, demonstrating VLA token-scaling intractable past a few hundred frames, an H6 footprint datapoint) — but RMBench/LIBERO-Mem are tabletop, and none carries an allocentric scene map or a dynamic relocated-object purge, so the scene+episodic-fused, dynamically-purged, multi-room combination the bet turns on remains unbuilt |
| No standardized force-adaptive whole-body benchmark — neither a graded/step-wrench CoM-excursion + tracking-error protocol in the first ~100 ms (H1's anticipatory-vs-reactive headline) nor a torque-limit-aware-curriculum-vs-naive ablation under matched graded wrench (H2) | C1 | the generic suite [[2403.10506\|HumanoidBench]] (27 loco/manip tasks) carries no force/wrench/payload term; every force result ([[2505.06776\|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]] N/M/L 0.37 vs 0.60, [[2511.21169\|Kinematics-Aware-Multi-Policy]] 4 kg + 112.8 kg cart, [[2510.26280\|Thor]] 167.7 N) is reported on each method's own ad-hoc force schedule, not a benchmark. Two real-humanoid (Digit) impulse-perturbation protocols now supply the standardized step-impulse + recovery-success machinery the H1 first-~100 ms test borrows: [[2308.14636\|Linear-Impactor]] characterizes the perturbation by *impact momentum* with non-monotonic success-vs-severity (tol 26.376 kg·m/s, <0.1 m/s repeatability) and [[2404.19173\|Single-Contact++-RL]] supplies a five-metric success-rate-vs-impulse + drift framework (up to 258 N @ 500 ms) that HumanoidBench's 27 force-free loco/manip tasks lack — but both perturb the torso/base during walking, not the *hand wrench during manipulation*, so the hand-wrench-during-loco-manip application remains a protocol extension. The reactive estimate-then-compensate rung now spans three signal-sources the anticipatory bet must beat at sudden onset: model-based-analytic wrench observation ([[2606.16542\|ADAPT-Locomotion]]), learned force-estimate → EE+base impedance ([[2505.20829\|Unified-Force-Position-Control]]), and history-based load latent ([[2606.03297\|SplitAdapter]] / [[2603.14308\|Load-Aware-Loco-Manipulation]]) — but none forward-models the wrench's whole-body reaction before the next action lands, so the first-~100 ms CoM-excursion advantage (H1) remains unmeasured. [[2607.06052\|ThorArena]] now supplies the first *standardized* force-aware whole-body eval (human motion-force demos, a force-replay protocol, and a Force-Aware Tracking Score + episode-survival metric) that exposes the force-induced degradation (TWIST2/GMT/SONIC to 0.73–0.81 survival) HumanoidBench's 27 force-free tasks hide, but it replays *recorded* interaction forces during motion *tracking*, so it scores force-robust tracking rather than the graded *step-wrench* CoM-excursion (H1) or the torque-limit-aware-curriculum ablation (H2), narrowing the gap while leaving the anticipatory-vs-reactive transient protocol the open contribution |
| No certified-safety-vs-reward-shaping benchmark for whole-body control under OOD load + model mismatch | C2 | [[2310.12567\|Safety-Gymnasium]] (standardized constrained-vs-unconstrained safe-RL protocol: per-step violation-cost metric + MuJoCo-Humanoid velocity-constraint tasks — but no OOD-load/mass-mismatch sweep, no external-wrench/payload-equilibrium term, no balance-under-payload or interaction-pose-fidelity axis). [[2502.03132\|SPARK]] now supplies the humanoid-specific substrate that hosts the H1 certified-vs-reward head-to-head — 5 SOTA safe-control algorithms compared under one shared safety-cost framework on a real Unitree G1 (real-time 0.011–0.015 s loop), which Safety-Gymnasium (generic MuJoCo) and HumanoidBench (no safety term) lack — but SPARK scores end-controllers on obstacle/whole-body-motion tasks and ships no payload/wrench-equilibrium safe set or OOD-mass-mismatch sweep, so the under-load balance axis (H1/H2/H4) remains the open contribution |
| No benchmark varies or reports compliance/impedance *allocation* as a controlled experimental condition — every force-adaptive suite treats stiffness as a fixed nuisance property of the platform, not a swept independent variable, so C3's CoM-excursion-vs-tracking-error Pareto (H1/H6) has no standardized protocol to run on | C3 | [[2308.14636\|Linear-Impactor]] / [[2404.19173\|Single-Contact++-RL]] (both supply the standardized step-impulse + recovery-success machinery C3's Pareto test borrows — impact momentum, <0.1 m/s repeatability, five-metric success-vs-impulse — but neither varies nor reports the responding policy's compliance setting as a condition, so the compliance-as-covariate axis is unmeasured) |
