| No interaction-preservation benchmark tying contact-fidelity (penetration/foot-skating) to downstream-RL trainability, and no base-travel-stratified whole-body-vs-fixed-base *capture* subtraction on a legged platform | D1 | [[2510.02252\|GMR]] (compares 4 retargeters on a shared LAFAN1 subset with a fixed BeyondMimic training pipeline to isolate retargeting effects on tracking SR, but does not regress the contact-fidelity → downstream-SR slope/R² across the full ≥5-method spread nor isolate which contact term is load-bearing at fixed joint accuracy). The H2 cross-method contact-fidelity spread now spans a GRF/dynamics lever ([[2603.09956\|Kinodynamic-Retargeting]]), a cheap learning-based dynamically-feasible variant ([[2509.15443\|Implicit-Kinodynamic-Retargeting]]), and a hand-object contact-topology face ([[2606.16272\|TopoRetarget]]), broadening the fidelity axis — but none reports the fidelity → downstream-SR regression slope + R², so the designed-against monotone predictor remains the unclaimed core. For the capture-isolation test (H7), [[2407.07788\|BiGym]] (40 mobile-bimanual tasks with whole-body-vs-bimanual action-mode switch = the coupling-present/absent knob, but no base-travel-stratified subtraction or demos-per-human-hour cost metric) + [[2505.12748\|TeleOpBench]] (4-modality comparison but in-place dexterity) + [[2412.05313\|λ/LAMBDA]] (the data-efficiency reference: SR-vs-demo-budget at 571 demos for language-conditioned long-horizon mobile manip, BC 2.44–5.1% vs neuro-symbolic 44.4% zero-shot — but pick-and-place MoMa where the base navigates *between* manipulations, not whole-body simultaneous coupling) — no single suite runs the base-travel-stratified subtraction [[2509.04443\|EMMA]] ran on wheels (H7), or a demos-per-human-hour-vs-demos-per-seed cost metric. [[2606.17833\|HumanoidArena]]'s 7 leg-critical egocentric HOI/HSI tasks let H7's controlled subtraction run across interchangeable low-level GMTs, exposing whether the high-level policy overfits the tracker rather than learning the coupling |
| No cross-embodiment whole-body-transfer benchmark sweeps a DYNAMICS/inertial-distance axis alongside kinematic distance — [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]]'s interpolation/extrapolation/composition splits (and every distance protocol built on them) are purely kinematic: link-structure/geometric variation only, no mass, inertia, or CoM term anywhere in the robot-state encoding, so morphology-distance-vs-adaptation-cost is measurable only on the geometric axis, never the inertial one that D2's own H2 dynamics-sensitive-pathway claim requires | D2 | [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] (18 morphologies with interpolation/extrapolation/composition splits = a standardized *kinematic*-distance protocol, but manipulation-only reach/push — no bipedal whole-body loco-manip body, and no inertial axis at all); [[2506.09366\|SkillBlender]]'s SkillBench adds the only real-humanoid loco-manip multi-morphology body (3 Unitree H1/G1/H1-2 × 4 skills × 8 tasks) the distance protocols lack, but covers only 3 closely-related morphologies (no graded distance gradient, kinematic or inertial) and is composition-instrumented, not transfer-instrumented. H3's kinematic-vs-inertial rank-inversion test gains two distinct mechanisms, neither yet a benchmark: [[2602.18025\|Cross-Embodiment-Offline-RL]] shows kinematic transfer cost rises where inter-robot gradients conflict (r=0.815 transfer-gain vs gradient-cosine, locomotion-only platforms), and [[2506.14278\|Heavy-Limbs-WBC]] shows the *inertial* mechanism directly — replacing its full centroidal-momentum model with an SRBD approximation that discards changing mass/inertia distribution collapses performance on the same kinematic structure (1.2 m/s / 60 N → 0.5 m/s / 20 N) — but on one robot, no cross-embodiment sweep. No benchmark anywhere in the cited literature turns this into a swept inertial-distance axis |
| No whole-body synthetic-data benchmark tying generated feasibility (penetration/stability) to downstream SR, with synthetic-only-vs-co-trained and whole-body-vs-manipulation-only-generator ablations | D3 | [[2505.12705\|DreamGen]] (DreamGen Bench: Instruction-Following + Physics-Alignment with >90% Pearson correlation to downstream SR, GR1-humanoid behavior/environment generalization splits — but scores generated-VIDEO physics-plausibility, not whole-body coupled-trajectory penetration/foot-skating, and runs no generator-type ablation) + [[2605.27724\|HumanoidMimicGen]] (0.89 vs 0.33 on its internal 9-task suite, a method's own eval) |

> [!tip] Humanoid reading path
> The humanoid is read across all three subsystem docs. Its **legs** — whole-body balance, terrain traversal, dynamic agile skills, fall-recovery — are in [[Locomotion|Locomotion]] (the **Bipedal** cluster). Its **arms + hands** — two-arm coordination and multi-fingered in-hand control — are in [[Manipulation|Manipulation]] (the **Bimanual** + **Dexterous** clusters). And the **whole-body coupling that integrates them** — the arm reach that disturbs balance (A), the base that extends the manipulation workspace (B), the legs that compensate for the force the arms feel (C), and the human motion retargeted onto the coupled body (D) — is **this** doc (Loco-Manipulation + Mobile Manipulation + Force-Adaptive Control). Read Locomotion for the legs, Manipulation for the arms, and this doc for the coordination that makes them one body.
